- **Mauj Darya Bukhari's Akbari endowment**: "دو لاکھہ روپیے کی جاگیر" with qasba Batala (p. 79, folio 82) **against** a jagir of one lakh twenty-five thousand at Batala[OCR?] (p. 279, folio 282).
- **Shah Sharaf's reburial**: through **Faqir Aziz-ud-Din**, in Haji Muhammad Saeed's enclosure at Dola Wari, with a small pakka chabutra by the Maharaja's order (pp. 80–81, folios 83–84) **against** through **Faqir Nur-ud-Din**, under "نہایت مختصر چبوترہ و چار دیواری", at the demolition of Khwaja Muhammad Saeed's tomb and mosque (pp. 287–288, folios 290–291).
- **Shah Dargahi's well**: the rotis and one paisa charaghi **taken by the zamindar of the well** (p. 82, folio 85) **against** the rotis **distributed to the people** and one paisa given to the well's zamindars (p. 299, folio 302).
- **Abu al-Maali / Abul Muali's festivals**: the mela of both Eids plus an annual urs "ہزار ہا [OCR?] پر" (p. 87, folio 90) **against** three melas a year with chiraghan and dancing courtesans, qawwali, plus both Eids (p. 285, folio 288).
- **Major MacGregor's office**: "ڈپٹی کمشنر لاہور" restoring a mosque in 1850[OCR?] (p. 143, folio 146) **against** "اسسٹنٹ ایجینٹ[OCR?] ریزیڈنٹ" dismissing a faqir's petitions (pp. 300–301, folios 303–304).
- **Mr Cope / Coop[OCR?]**, the mosque's private occupant, is spelled both ways and once given the office "مہتمم مطبع لاہور" (p. 168, folio 171; p. 268, folio 271).
- **Jahangir's tomb**: "سنہ ۱۰۳۴[OCR?] ہجری" against the grave inscription's "سنہ ۱۰۳۷[OCR?]" (pp. 314, 316; folios 321, 323).
- **Mulla Majid's mosque**: "سنہ ١٢٣٥[OCR?]" and "سنہ ١٢٩٤" on one inscription (p. 183, folio 186).
- **The Mian Mir church**: "سنہ ۱۸۵۰[OCR?]ء و سنہ ۱۸۵۷[OCR?]ء یعنی قریباً چھہ سال کے عرصہ میں" (p. 390, folio 397).
- **Wasdev's renovation**: "فی زمانہ حال یعنی سمت ۱۹۳۷[OCR?]" against "سمت ۱۹۳۶[OCR?]" on his own brick (p. 130, folio 133).
- **Two elapsed-age figures that cannot share one moment of writing**: 226[OCR?] years (p. 152, folio 155) and 251 years (p. 155, folio 158).
- **Two statements about Sikh-era schooling**: thousands taught and the whole Punjab's respect (p. 38, folio 41) against "سرکار انگریزی نے وہ سب نقص دور کر دیئے" (p. 39, folio 42).
- **One offence stated twice and differently** in the Haqiqat Rai execution (p. 208, folio 211).
- **رکھہ چلو[OCR?]** (p. 239, folio 242) and **رکہہ جلو[OCR?]** (p. 360, folio 367; pp. 360–361, folios 367–368), left as printed.
- **کانسی کار / کام کانسی کا بسنتی** (p. 268, folio 271; p. 270, folio 273) against **کاشی کاری** (p. 277, folio 280; p. 286, folio 289; p. 260, folio 263; p. 262, folio 265; p. 263, folio 266; p. 264, folio 267).

**Doubled pagination — a systematic discrepancy across the pp. 119–235 and pp. 236–348 partials that a human must resolve.** The following appear in both partials, each within its own assigned range, with page and folio references differing by one hundred. Both citations are reproduced; **neither has been adjusted, and no correction is implied.**
- Mufti Ghulam Sarwar buying the burial-enclosure land from the khanqah's mujawirs: (p. 167, folio 170) / (p. 267, folio 270).
- Daya Anga / Dai Anga's waqf property, "بہت سی جائداد بانیہ … وقف": (p. 167, folio 170) / (p. 267, folio 270).
- The Huzuri khanqah's two-day mela, "ایک روز چراغ دوسرے روز قوالی": (p. 167, folio 170) / (p. 267, folio 270).
- The Dai Anga mosque's powder store, nazul entry, Mr Cope's kothi, the railway's purchase, and the English preserving its ornament for beauty: (p. 168, folio 171) / (p. 268, folio 271).
- Hassu Teli's six bighas with a wooden-wheel well and the income released for expenses; the urs of 3 Shawwal; the jhanda, the daily lamp and the Thursday lamps at his shop: (p. 169, folio 172) / (p. 269, folio 272).
- Jani Khan's maqbara and garden deorhi: built "احمد شاہ بادشاہ کے عہد میں" (p. 169, folio 172) / deorhi bought from the government by a zamindar, dome "کام لہر یا بسنتی کانسی کار" (p. 270, folio 273).

**Figures that must never be presented as clean numbers.** Both money figures in the Haqiqat Rai grant are `[illegible]` (p. 209, folio 212); the sum Munshi Najm-ud-Din spent on the Nila Gumbad is `[illegible]` (p. 287, folio 290); the English-period span of the brick-digging is `[illegible]` (p. 33, folio 36); the farman's year for the office of ifta is `[illegible]` (p. 52, folio 55); the number of rotis vowed and the recipients are `[illegible]` (p. 294, folio 297); the Quran-and-sword valuation is only "کسی ہزار روپیے" (p. 265, folio 268); the physician's day total "بایس[OCR?] لاکہ" does not reconcile with its own components (p. 160, folio 163); the jagir written "نو ہزار نو سو پچپن ۹۹۵۵[OCR?]" carries the flag on the numerals (p. 67, folio 70); the Quran-readers at Jahangir's tomb are "یا نسو[OCR?] آدمی" (p. 315, folio 322); the wells at Bhauri Sarkar's are "نہ چار دہ نہ[OCR?] چاہ" (p. 201, folio 204); Bahadur Khan's octagon sides are "۱۸۔۱۷ [OCR?] گز" (p. 258, folio 261); Jahangir's platform is "دو سو ہاٹہہ[OCR?] فٹ" with minaret steps "اکسٹہہ[OCR?]" (pp. 316–319, folios 323–326); Shalamar's bigha figures do not add up (pp. 351–352, folios 358–359); the samadh-keeper's salary is "ایک سو[OCR?] روپیہ سالانہ" (p. 212, folio 215); the muafi land is "چوودہ[OCR?] بیگہ" (p. 214, folio 217); the Than Bhairo land is "twelve ghumaon[OCR?]" as the partial prints it (p. 232, folio 235); the khanqah's dead are "ایکسو[OCR?] درویش" (pp. 194–195, folios 197–198); and the explosion's dead "قریبا دو سو آدمی کی جان[OCR?]" (p. 330, folio 337).

**Flagged readings on which a whole fact depends.** "حمام وزیر خان جو مسجد کے اوقات میں سے شمار ہوتا ہے" — the printed word is "اوقات", **unflagged**, and the reading as *awqaf* is the source partial's doubt, not the page's (p. 15, folio 10). The front-matter "ذکر سجادہ و مقابر و منادر بیرون لاہور[OCR?]" is flagged entire (p. 3, folio 2). "سنہ ۱۸۵۶ [OCR?]" carries the Khoja mosque's whole chronology (pp. 84–85, folios 87–88). "سنہ ۱۸۵۰ء[OCR?]" carries MacGregor's restitution (p. 143, folio 146). The Egerton release "after thirty-two[OCR?] years" carries the Sunehri Masjid's recovery (pp. 147–148, folios 150–151). By contrast, **1849 AD for the Shia–Sunni riot (p. 302, folio 305), Sambat 1895 Bikrami at Begampura (pp. 276–277, folios 279–280), "سمبت ۱۸۹۸ بکرمی" at Tali Sahib (p. 228, folio 231), "١٢٨٢ ہجری" at Masjid Pholiyan Wali (p. 176, folio 179), "سنہ ۱۸۷۲ء" for the New College (p. 381, folio 388) and "سنہ ۱۸۸۱" for the Medical School (p. 429, folio 436) are printed unflagged** and are the range's firmest dates.



## Figures and sites not in the archive

Candidate list for future archive rows, merged from the four Pass 2 partials (pp. 1–118, 119–235, 236–348, 349–463). Page and folio references are reproduced exactly as the partials give them; no folio has been computed or adjusted. `[OCR?]` and `[illegible]` flags are carried on the words they were attached to.

### Muslim shrines, khanqahs and saints' tombs

- **پیر ذکی شہید / مزار پیر ذکی[OCR?]** — gate-guard martyred defending Zaki/Makki Gate "مغلیہ [illegible] کے وقت"; two graves, the head in the gate and "جسم بے سر" in a stable inside the city, both "زیارت گاہ خاص و عام" with the Fatiha recited (pp. 20–21, folios 15–16); martyrdom on the authority of *Tuhfat al-Wasilin* (pp. 164–165, folios 167–168); contents "مزار پیر ذکی[OCR?] ۱۶۷" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3). **Deliberately not mapped to peer-makki** — see Doubts.
- **شاہ شرف (mohalla outside Bhati Gate)** — "ایک عالم و کامل شخص" with thousands of murids; founded the mohalla, built a magnificent mosque and his own pakka maqbara in his lifetime; both demolished for Ranjit Singh's glacis, coffin reburied under a pakka chabutra through فقیر عزیز الدین (pp. 80–81, folios 83–84); contents "شاہ شرف کا محلہ[OCR?]" ۸۴ and "مزار شاہ شرف" ۳۰۰ (pp. 2, 4). Whether the contents' ۳۰۰ is this saint or the Shah Sharf below is **unresolved**.
- **محمد فاضل and his son شاہ شرف** — khanqah east of the jail road between the Chand Mari encampment and a flagged landmark; Muhammad Fazil a **Qadiriya** faqir of the Shahjahani era, d. "۱۱۱۰ [OCR?] ہجری"; his son and murid Shah Sharf his successor, taught lifelong in a mosque now traceable only by its remains, murids including **Aziz Allah**, d. "سنہ ۱۱۴۷[OCR?] ہجری"; **two melas a year with food; eight bighas cultivated and seven uncultivated revenue-free** (p. 297, folio 300). Kept separate from the Bhati Gate Shah Sharaf.
- **شاہ شرف[OCR?] (small enclosure at Khwaja Muhammad Saeed's)** — a small enclosure for Shah Sharaf[OCR?] inside Khwaja Muhammad Saeed's pakka enclosure facing the Nila Gumbad (pp. 287–288, folios 290–291). May be either of the two above; not resolved.
- **شاہ درگاہی / درگاہی شاہ (and ہاہی[OCR?] شاہ)** — "قادری سلسلہ کا ایک فقیر عابد و زاہد"; nothing of his mohalla remains, "صرف ایک مزار شاہ درگاہی کا باقی ہے جو سر راہ بجانب شرق واقع ہے" (p. 82, folio 85); **no enclosure wall, only a takya (تکیہ)**, pakka platform, mosque built by **Rahim Bakhsh, merchant of Delhi**; murid of **Sayyid Shah Chiragh**; his murid ہاہی[OCR?] Shah buried by the roadside; the **healing well with its Sunday children's rite** and a paisa paid to its zamindars (p. 299, folio 302); contents "مزار شاہ درگاہی[OCR?]" ۳۰۲ (pp. 2, 4).
- **شاہ بدر الدین گیلانی / مقبرہ سید بدال دین[OCR?] گیلانی** — "عابد زاہد و صاحب کرامت"; settled the mohalla south of Shah Dargahi's and "اپنے ہم قوم شریف سید لوگ اسمیں آباد کئے"; inhabited from the Akbari era to the end of the Chughtai sultanate; his mosque a powder magazine, then a brick-mine (pp. 82–84, folios 85–87); tomb built in the Shah Jahan era, nisba **گیلانی** only, d. ۱۰۷۲[OCR?] ہجری, children raised the dome, site became **Government House** (pp. 394–396, folios 401–403); the Gilani mohalla and grand mosque west of it, their ruin cross-referenced by the book to "پہلے حصہ کتاب ہذا" (p. 395, folio 402) — that cross-reference is why the two partials' figures are treated as one; also the **کشتی والہ گنبد**, the same dome once wrestlers made an akhara beside it (p. 395, folio 402).
- **سید بدرالدین شاہ عالم, مزار سبز گنبد** — north of Raja Suchet Singh's haveli; the Sadat Bukhari claim him; built by **Nawab Saad Allah Khan**, its garden absorbed into the haveli, its land now the Tehsil (pp. 144–145, folios 147–148). **Not** the Badr-ud-Din Gilani above — name-trap.
- **حاجی محمد سعید** — the elder whose presence saved Dola Wari from plunder, visited for ziyarat by **Ahmad Shah Durrani**, who posted a royal guard; "تیسرا احاطہ مزار حاجی محمد سعید اب تک موجود ہے", very large, its gate to the west, containing among others the grave of "حاجی مسروح [OCR?]" (p. 80, folio 83). Possibly the same site as Khwaja Muhammad Saeed below (both Durrani-favoured, both a large enclosure) — **not resolved**; enclosure and gate are described differently.
- **خواجہ محمد سعید لاہوری** — pakka enclosure east of and facing the **Nila Gumbad**, a kotha for a faqir at the gate; graves of himself, **Haji Ibad-ullah** and **Abdul Rahman**, his grandsons; **Qadiriya**, "صاحب کرامت و خوارق"; long in **Kabul**, **Ahmad Shah Durrani** his great devotee; d. "۱۱۸۵ [OCR?]", chronogram "شیخ وصل سعید"; tomb and mosque demolished for Ranjit Singh's gates and moat, the stone sent to Amritsar (pp. 287–288, folios 290–291); contents "مزار خواجہ محمد سعید ۲۹۰" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **عبد اللہ شاہ بلوچ / شیخ عبد اللہ شاہ بلوچ, Mozang** — "ایسا صاحب باطن فقیر ہوا جسکے لاہور کے علما و فضلا مرید تھے"; a settlement with pakka rampart named after him, the founder's name broken (pp. 77–78, folios 80–81); famous for zuhd, riyazat and karamat "شہر لاہور کے تین حاکمون کیوقت"; bai'at in the **Qadiriya**; founded **Kot Abdullah Shah**; his **Punjabi verses** still current; murid **Hafiz Ghulam Muhammad, imam of Wazir Khan's mosque**; d. "۱۲۱۸ [OCR?] ہجری"; present tomb and mosque built by **Sardar Khan Baloch, lambardar of Mozang** (pp. 282–283, folios 285–286). **Deliberately not mapped** — not shrine-of-abdullah-shah-ghazi; see Doubts.
- **میان وڈا / شیخ محمد اسمعیل المشہور میان وڈا** — khanqah and teaching madrasa at Teel Pura, "مدرسہ مسجد [OCR?] تیل المشہور میان وڈا", still inhabited (p. 89, folio 92); born "سال نوسو نا نوین[OCR?] ہجری", pupil of Makhdum Abdul Karim at five, khirqa of khilafat in the **Suhrawardiyya**, madrasa a mile south of Shalamar, d. "سنہ ۱۰۴۵[OCR?] ہجری"; *kham* grave by his own will; three khalifas buried beside him — **Shaikh Jan Muhammad**, **Shaikh Nur Muhammad**, **Hafiz Muhammad Salih** (pp. 237–239, folios 240–242). His **sajjada nashin** keeps Shaikh Hamid Qari's khanqah (pp. 252–253, folios 255–256) and claimed Mian Jan Muhammad's grave (p. 259, folio 262); Raja Suchet Singh was killed at Khanqah Mian Wadda[OCR?] (pp. 194–196, folios 197–199). The richest institutional entry in pp. 236–348.
- **The sayyids of محلہ سید سر [OCR?]** — "اُن سیدوں کے ہزاروں مرید تھے", with a pakka tank believed to heal sick children; destroyed by the Bhangi misl after an informer's charge (p. 88, folio 91).
- **گلابی شاہ** — named only as the site "متصل گلابی شاہ"; no further detail (p. 88, folio 91).
- **تاجا** — a faqir whose grave is inside Taj Pura; no further detail (p. 78, folio 81).
- **خواجہ خاوند محمود المشہور حضرت ایشان** — Sayyid and **Naqshbandi** faqir of **Bukhara**; set out at twenty[OCR?] via Samarkand, Herat, Qandahar, Kabul to **Kashmir**, "لاکہون لوگ مرید ہوے"; expelled by **Husain Chak[OCR?]** for converting "ہزارون شیعہ امامیہ" to Sunnism; brought to Hindustan by **Jahangir** "بفرط اعتقاد"; khanqah at **Mughalpura**; d. "سنہ ۱۰۵۲[OCR?] ہجری"; son **Baha-ud-Din** in a second grave in the same rauza (pp. 239–242, folios 242–245); contents "روضہ خواجہ [illegible] محمود ۲۴۲" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3). The pp. 1–118 partial records **حضرت ایشان [OCR?]** only as a second mausoleum in Nawab Zakariya Khan's graveyard complex, one flagged phrase, no date, order or custodian (p. 91, folio 94) — probably this rauza, **not asserted**.
- **شاہ ضیا قادری and his khanqah** — Mohalla Nasbi[OCR?], Taksali Darwaza; of the **Shattariya Qadriya** family, d. 1126[OCR?]; **annual mela with sama and wajd**; pir of the archive's Shah Inayat Allah (p. 144, folio 147); contents "خانقاہ شاہ [illegible] قادری ۱۴۷" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **سید اسحاق گاذرونی المشہور میران بادشاہ / پیر سبز** — sayyid of Gazrun in Fars, came in the Tughlaq era, thousands of murids, d. 706[OCR?]; grave in a **tahkhana inside the Wazir Khan mosque, predating it**; **mujawir, Thursday mela, annual urs and sama**; the tree on his grave (pp. 156, 161–163; folios 159, 164–166); contents "مزار [illegible] گازرونی[OCR?] ۱۶۴" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **امام غلام محمد المشہور امام کامون[OCR?]** — imam of Wazir Khan's mosque, **Qadiriya** faqir, author of *Ganj-e Makhfi*, d. 1234[OCR?]; murid of **Abdullah Baloch Qadri of Mozang**; his dome built by a devotee under early British rule; **mela and sama now stopped** (p. 163, folio 166); contents "مقبرہ امام غلام محمد[OCR?] ۱۶۶" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3). Named again as Abdullah Shah Baloch's murid at pp. 282–283, folios 285–286.
- **مقبرہ مسجد صوف[OCR?]** — outer chowk of the Wazir Khan mosque; occupant unnamed, said by some to be Sayyid Ishaq's brother; rauza built over it by a contractor in a British road-clearance (pp. 163–164, folios 166–167).
- **مزار سید سر بلند** — north of the Wazir Khan mosque, in a room the Nawab built and **made waqf**; **Thursday crowds and an annual urs in decline** (p. 164, folio 167); contents "مزار سید سربلند[OCR?] ۱۶۷" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **پیر بائی[OCR?] / پیر بلخی[OCR?]** — roadside mazar in Kashmiri Bazaar; real name unknown even to the author's source; came from **Balkh** after Chingiz Khan's conquest, killed fighting Quli Khan at Lahore, buried in his own hujra; rebuilt when a bazaar was straightened (p. 165, folio 168); contents "پیر بلخی[OCR?] ۱۶۸" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3) — the contents name is taken as this mazar on the printed page number; both spellings flagged.
- **سید محمود حضوری و سید شاہ نور الدین; سید جان محمد حضوری و سید سرور دین (the Huzuri Sayyids)** — two grand pukka **twelve-doored** tombs in one unheaded enclosure, with an ancient mosque and a **Persian-wheel well**, at mauza Garhi[OCR?]; **Musavi Sayyids** from **Koh-e Ghor** to **Uch** to Lahore, rising in Shah Jahan's era; "مقتدا و پیشوا ے طریقت"; the title *Huzuri* glossed; Jan Muhammad d. 764[OCR?] in one partial and "سنہ ۱۰۶۸ [OCR?] ہجری" in the other — **the two death years are irreconcilable as printed**; thirty-plus-generation shajra printed back to **Imam Musa Kazim**; descendants **Sayyid Ahmad Shah and Husain Shah** living; **annual mela "ایک روز چراغ دوسرے روز قوالی"** (pp. 166–167, folios 169–170; pp. 265–267, folios 268–270). A **سید جان محمد حضوری[OCR?]** and محلہ مسجد سرا[OCR?] are named again as the elders in whose service Abul Khair stayed (p. 362, folio 369).
- **شیخ حسو تیلی / حسو تیلی** — a **Teli by caste**, oil-seller of Chowk Jhande[OCR?], murid of **Shah Jamal Suhrawardiyya**, d. 1000[OCR?] AH in one partial and death year **[illegible]** in the other; grave pakka lime-plastered on a raised platform with a **pakka lamp-stand at the head**; khalifas **Shaikh Sa'd-ud-Din Sitr Posh[OCR?] / Shaikh Sa'dullah Dastarposh** and **Mian Khan** buried on the lower platform; present building by چہنن[OCR?] Shah Faqir; **six bighas[OCR?]; urs 3 Shawwal; a jhanda and daily lamps at his old shop, many on Thursdays**; the saying about **Lal Husain** (pp. 168–169, folios 171–172; pp. 268–269, folios 271–272); contents "مزار حسو تیلی[OCR?] ۱۶۱[OCR?]" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3), the number flagged.
- **مزار پیر سراج الدین المعروف پیر شیرازی** — محلہ جوطے[OCR?] موری; came from **Bukhara** in Sultan Muhammad Tughlaq's era, buried in his own dwelling; refused the qaziship and closed his teaching (pp. 149–150, folios 152–153); contents "مزار پیر شیرازی[OCR?] ۱۵۲" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **مزار پیر بہولا** — mohalla of the hammam of Pachila[OCR?]; an unrestrained majzub[OCR?] faqir of the Alamgiri era; sweets from under his arm; **boys' Friday replica-mazars** (p. 150, folio 153); contents "مزار پیر بہولا[OCR?] ۱۵۳" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شاہ بلاول / مزار سید شاہ بلاول قادری** — the allama of Akbar's, Jahangir's and Shah Jahan's eras; father Sayyid Usman, grandfather Sayyid Isa, from **Herat**, granted the fort of Sheikhupura in jagir by Humayun; born at Sheikhupura, taught by Maulvi Abul Fath Lahori and Shaikh Shamsuddin Qadri; d. 1046[OCR?], era not printed; **a langar of thousands a day**; exhumed by Ranjit Singh's order through Faqir Nur-ud-Din, the body incorrupt after two hundred years (p. 235, folio 238); *pukhta* mazar at the centre of a khanqah **one kos east of Lahore** by Raja Dina Nath's garden on the old Shalamar road, the khanqah owning the land inside its wall; **mela 28 Shaban shared by Hindus and Muslims** with fireworks[OCR?]; walls fallen for want of repair and oversight (p. 236, folio 239); contents "مزار سید شاہ بلاول[OCR?] ۲۳۸" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3). **Deliberately not mapped**; see Doubts.
- **مزار گہوڑے شاہ بخاری** — "اصلی نام سید جہولن شاہ بخاری", also given as Baha-ud-Din; three-generation Bukhari line from **Uch**, grandfather **Sayyid Usman** the first to come to Lahore and buried **inside the Fort**, titled **Jhula Bukhari** from the palsy; "مادر زاد ولی", **died aged five**, "ماہ ربیع الاول سنہ ۱۰۰۳[OCR?] ہجری"; no dome; **mosque, well and rooms attached with a resident faqir sajjada nashin; thousands of clay horses offered at the annual mela** (pp. 236–237, folios 239–240); Shaikh Mahmud Shah Naqshbandi's tomb just north, "صرف رستہ اسکے درمیان حد فاصل ہے" (p. 260, folio 263). The contents' "مزار گوہر شاہ[OCR?] ۲۳۹" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3) is on the same printed page and is probably this mazar — both spellings flagged, **not asserted**.
- **شیخ موسی آہنگر** — green-domed maqbara north of Qila Gujar Singh; alive in the **Lodhi** sultanate, worked as a blacksmith; *iradat* in the **Suhrawardiyya** at the service of Shaikh Abdul Jalil Chauthar[OCR?] Bandagi; d. "۹۲۵[OCR?] ہجری"; **blacksmith murids run his annual urs**; the woman of the spindle miracle buried in the same enclosure under a fallen dome (pp. 245–246, folios 248–249); contents "مقبرہ شیخ موسی آہنگر ۲۴۸" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شیخ چوتہر عبدالجلیل قطب عالم** — buried very near Shaikh Musa in a *tah-khana* with a second grave over the vault; **Suhrawardiyya**; "اب بھی ہزارون لوگ اسکے گہر کے مرید ہیں"; son-in-law of **Sultan Bahlol Lodi**[OCR?]; d. "سنہ ۹۱۰[OCR?] ہجری", the date from the word *shaikh*; **تذکرہ قطبیہ** written on his karamat; outer enclosure rebuilt "۱۲۷۴[OCR?]" by a descendant, with an **absentee custodian at Ratta Piran, Sialkot** (pp. 246–247, folios 249–250); contents "مقبرہ شیخ چوہڑ[OCR?] ۲۵۰" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3), spelling flagged.
- **The Rasul Shahi faqirs and نور حسین** — a **branch of the Suhrawardi family** led by **Rasul Shah**, at a famous house outside the **Delhi gate**; dress rule, body-marking[OCR?], and "اس فرقہ کے لوگ لکھنے پڑہنے[OCR?] ہے ضرور ہوتے ہیں کوئی ناخواندہ نہیں ہوتا"; house built for Nur Husain by **Raja Dina Nath**; licensed still, then closed (pp. 247–248, folios 250–251).
- **شاہ محمد غوث قادری** — outside the eastern wall between the Akbari and Delhi gates; "تمام مسلمان لاہور بلکہ تمام پنجاب کے اہل اسلام اس مکان کا بدل و جان ادب کرتے ہیں"; of **Muhammad Shah's** era, from **Peshawar**, father **Sayyid Hasan** with a maqbara there; genealogy joining that of[OCR?] **Ghaus-ul-Azam**; d. "سنہ ۱۱۵۲[OCR?] ہجری"; two graves, his and **his wife's**; mosque by **Ghulam Nabi Kothi[OCR?]-war**; **a latticed sajjada nashin's seat**; demolished in the 1840 clearance and rebuilt after Nau Nihal Singh's death (pp. 248–250, folios 251–253); contents "مقبرہ شاہ محمد غوث ۲۵۱" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شاہ رحمت اللہ قریشی** — "عہد عالمگیر مین ایک عالم فاضل و صاحب تصرف آدمی", murids beyond number; grave on a chabutra in a pukka enclosure with many graves above and below it, **north of Bibi Pak Daman's khanqah**; death year not stated; **hereditary piri-muridi line and custody in his family at mauza Dhulnawal and Lahore** (p. 251, folio 254).
- **شیخ حامد قاری** — khanqah two miles east of Lahore; titled **Qari** for mastery of recitation; in **Muhammad Shah's** time, taught in his own mosque; d. "سنہ ۱۰۶۴ [OCR?] ہجری"; bai'at[OCR?] in the **Suhrawardi** family at the hand of Maulvi Taimur; **mela 13–14[OCR?] Jumada al-Thani**, his death date; **kept by Mian Wadda's sajjada nashin** (pp. 252–253, folios 255–256); contents "خانقاہ حامد قاری[OCR?] ۲۵۵" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3). **Dates irreconcilable**; see Doubts.
- **شاہ شمس الدین قادری لاہوری** — **Qadiri**, murid of **Shah Abu Ishaq Qadri** and **pir of Shah Bilawal Qadri**; "آزاد طبع و مجرد"; date "۱۰۲۴ [OCR?]" from two nastaliq couplets; square four-doored tomb, corner minarets now demolished, south-east of the Lieutenant Governor's kothi; **tomb and attached garden built by Shah Jahan**, the garden now traceless (pp. 255–256, folios 258–259). **Deliberately not mapped**; see Doubts. The contents' "مقبرہ شاہ شمس الدین ۲۵۶[OCR?]" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3) may be this tomb but the printed number does not match its folio — left in the unmatched contents list below.
- **شیخ محترم نقشبندی** — **Naqshbandi**; came in Akbar's era, lived to Alamgir's, d. **1102 AH**; square pukka tomb with four corner domelets north of the Shalamar road; three graves, his and **two of unknown name**; walls carrying Arabic and Persian verses, "کچہ پڑہے جاتے مین اور کچہ نہین"; **rauza auctioned to an Englishman, verandahed into a kothi and the graves demolished** (pp. 256–257, folios 259–260); contents "روضہ شیخ محترم[OCR?] ۲۵۹" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **میان جان محمد** — maulvi and mudarris of the Qasab Khana mohalla, pupil and murid of **Mian Wadda**, "علم و فضل و عمل و ولایت اسکی ذات مین جمع تہی"; d. "سنہ ۱۲۳۸ [OCR?] ہجری"; grave beside the mosque, contested by Mian Wadda's sajjada nashin (p. 259, folio 262). Distinct from the Maulvi Jan Muhammad of the Kashmiri Bazaar mosque (p. 175, folio 178) — name-trap.
- **شیخ محمود شاہ نقشبندی** — of the **Mujaddidiya Naqshbandiya** family, in Guzar Kashmiri Bazar; murids beyond number; d. "سنہ ۱۲۸۳ [OCR?] ہجری"; tomb "نو تیار انگریزی عہد کا", built by his murids in his lifetime over a basement holding his coffin; **annual urs**; stands just north of Ghore Shah Bukhari's mazar (pp. 260–261, folios 263–264; p. 260, folio 263).
- **مسکین شاہ امری[OCR?] / میر عنایت اللہ** — khadim of **Mian Mir**, who gave him the title; d. "سنہ ۱۰۵۸ [OCR?] ہجری"; tomb built by order of a patron whose name is unreadable, "دار [OCR?] اسکو" (pp. 263–264, folios 266–267); contents "مقبرہ مسکین شاہ امری[OCR?] ۲۶۶" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **سید عبد الوہاب قادری** — mauza Garhi Shahu, south of Mian Mir; **Qadiriyya**, "صاحب تصرف و زہد و ریاضت"; d. "سنہ ۱۰۵۸ [OCR?]", no era; marble floors stripped by Ranjit Singh's order and the grave demolished; a **great well "جسکو تالاب کہنا چاہیئے"** survives (p. 265, folio 268); contents "مقبرہ سید عبدالوہاب[OCR?] ۲۶۸" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3). **Deliberately not mapped**; see Doubts.
- **فقیر سائین قطب شاہ قادری** — east of mauza Khoi Miran; majzub and renouncer, labouring by day; murid of **Shaikh Ghulam Hasan** of the **Qadiriyya**; d. "سنہ ۱۲۹۰ [OCR?]", no era; mazar not built pakka by his own will; **urs twice a year with bhandara and a full day of qawwali, kept by murids rather than his offspring** (p. 271, folio 274); contents "مزار قطب شاہ قادری[OCR?] ۲۷۴" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **فتح شاہ مست** — khanqah south of Padao Badhu[OCR?]; two graves, his and his servant **Abd-us-Samad Shah**'s; famous in **Muhammad Shah's** era, wandering "مستی و مجذوبی [OCR?]"; bai'at in the **Shattariyya** through **Shaikh Latif Burhanpuri**; d. "سنہ ۱۱۵۵ [OCR?] ہجری" aged "ایکسو گیارہ [OCR?]"; "اسکی کرامتین صد ہا مشہور مین", none narrated; **mela 7 Shawwal, shrunk since Sikh rule** (pp. 273–274, folios 276–277); contents "مزار فتح شاہ[OCR?] ۲۷۶" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شاہ ابو تراب المشہور شاہ گدا لاہوری** — among Lahore's famous mazars; his grave with **Qazi Afzal Gada**'s and many others, a pakka domed mosque, a great wooden-wheel well, **faqirs' pakka quarters and cultivated land**; a **Persian verse divan** pointing to *hama ost*; from **Tabriz[OCR?]**, came with **Humayun[OCR?]**, bai'at with **Shaikh Wajih-ud-Din Gujarati Shattari**, twelve years in the jungles; d. "سنہ ۹۹۱ [OCR?] ہجری" aged "ایکسو چودہ [OCR?]" (pp. 274–275, folios 277–278). **Not** the سید عبد القادر المشہور شاہ گدا of the Gilani Sayyids' graveyard — name-trap.
- **شاہ ابو اسحاق قادری** — very near Mozang to the east; murid and khalifa of **Khwaja Daud of Sher Garh[OCR?]**; inlay of **Surah Yasin and Surah Mulk** on the four walls; a second tomb behind[OCR?] the mosque holding **three graves of his sons محمد حسین، ملک حسین، یار حسین**; d. "۹۵۸ [OCR?] ہجری بعہد اکبر بادشاہ"; chronogram "ابو اسحاق تاج عارفان"; **seven bighas with a running well feeding the mujawirs; mela 5 Muharram**, Quran read all night then raga singing (pp. 283–284, folios 286–287); contents "مقبرہ شاہ ابو اسحاق قادری[OCR?] ۲۸۶" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **تاج شاہ مجذوب / تاجے شاہ** — outside Mochi Gate; pakka grave, mosque, small garden and faqirs' houses; d. "روز دو شنبہ ہفتم [OCR?] ماہ بیساکہہ سمت ۱۹۰۰ [OCR?] بکرمی مطابق سنہ [illegible] ہجری"; a majzub of the **Sikh era** called sahib-e-kashf o karamat in his lifetime; **annual mela on his death date, paid for by a murid, نور محمد, and his son بوٹا**, born by the saint's prayer (pp. 285–286, folios 288–289); contents "مزار [illegible] شاہ مجذوب ۲۸۸" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شیخ عبد الرزاق مکی and the نیلا گنبد** — octagonal tomb with a dome in **kashi-kari of green-turquoise**, Sadr Bazar Anarkali; came from **Mecca[OCR?]** in Humayun's era, became **Mauj Darya Bukhari's murid**; the nobles of Lahore believed in him and thousands attended; d. "سنہ ۱۰۸۵ [OCR?] ہجری عہد عالمگیری"; later a powder magazine, shot-store and musket smithy, then a British mess, then released and **now Najm-ud-Din's mosque** (pp. 286–287, folios 289–290); contents "مقبرہ عبدالرزاق مکی[OCR?] ۲۸۹" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شیخ اشرف / شاہ اشرف** — alim and **amil** of Lahore without a second in his time, the nobles his devotees and "بادشاہ وقت مطیع تہا"; d. "۱۱۰۴ [OCR?]"; the grand building over his grave built by **Alamgir Aurangzeb's order** and annihilated in the Sikh time, the spot now "خاکروبون [OCR?] کا ٹکیہ [OCR?]", a sweepers' takiya (pp. 288–289, folios 291–292).
- **میرزا ہدی[OCR?] / محمد مسلم, titled پیر زیدی[OCR?]** — third tomb of an unnamed tract; a faqir of the **Naqshbandiyya Mujaddidiyya**; **the contingent vow of rotis is his karamat "فی زمانہ"**, current in the author's own time (pp. 293–294, folios 296–297).
- **مولوی جامی لاہوری** — fifth enclosure, by Shaikh Tahir's enclosure wall; in the **Jahangiri and Shahjahani era** a great scholar whose fatwa all Muslims accepted, with entry to the emperor's court; d. "سنہ ۱۰۵۳[OCR?] ہجری" (p. 294, folio 297).
- **شیخ عارف چشتی** — eighth enclosure; "اس بزرگ کی بزرگی کے سب[OCR?] لوگ قائل ہین"; grave repaired again recently; d. "سنہ ۱۰۶۴[OCR?] ہجری", the date from the words "عارف چشتی"; the vanishing lamp on his grave related by the aged people of **mauza Muridke** (p. 295, folio 298).
- **مستان شاہ** — a careless **majzub** of the **Sikh era** who threw away Ranjit Singh's thousand-rupee nazrs; no site detail (p. 295, folio 298).
- **سید چراغ شاہ حکیم** — tenth enclosure; **alive and present in Lahore at the time of writing**; **Sayyid Sabzwari[OCR?]**, **physician**, also carrying on **piri-muridi**, murids in the **Chishtiya and Qadiriya**, "ہزار ایک مرید"; bought the land from the zamindars, built the enclosure, made his family's graves in it, planted a garden, mounted a Persian wheel and built a faqir's kotha (p. 296, folio 299). Possibly the same man as **سید چراغ علی شاہ حکیم** of Mubarak Haveli, a "طبیب و فقیر" with a damaged **Chishti** claim and many murids, sons **سید حاکم علی، بہادر علی، نادر علی** (p. 332, folio 339) — **not asserted**.
- **پیر ہادی شاہ، محسن شاہ، عبد الصمد شاہ[OCR?]** — three graves in a closed **basement** beneath a very old twenty[OCR?]-doored domeless tomb east of the jailkhana and Anarkali; all three "شمسی سید شمس الدین تبریزی کی اولاد مین سے"; built "شاہ[OCR?] کے وقت", the reign unrecoverable; marble and red stone carried to **Jammu[OCR?]** by Raja Dhian Singh; its mosque and well now an Englishman's kothi (pp. 296–297, folios 299–300).
- **شاہ اسماعیل** — a very ancient domeless tomb near the Deputy Commissioner's kothi and the **pani-wala well**, on a platform roughly one storey above road level, covered in creepers[OCR?] with a **neem tree** at the grave's head; came to Lahore in **Sultan Mahmud Ghaznavi's** time and spread Islam, preaching every Friday so that thousands "جامہ اسلام پہینتے تہے"; "سنہ ۴۱۸[OCR?] ہجری مین یہ لاہور مین آیا اور سنہ ۴۵۸[OCR?] ہجری مین مر گیا ۳۶[OCR?] برس لاہور مین قیام رکہا", chronogram "مہتاب"; **its garden sold to the English by the mujawir** (pp. 297–298, folios 300–301); contents "مزار شاہ اسمعیل ۳۰۰" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شیخ سعدی بلخاری لاہوری** — old building south of Mozang; **Naqshbandiyya Mujaddidiyya**, murid of **Shaikh Adam Banuri**; exposition in **Khazinat al-Asfiya**; d. "سنہ ۱۰۸۵[OCR?] عہد عالمگیری"; chronogram "زندہ دل سعدی بلخاری"; **no dome, by his will**; garden and two wells lost, one closed for the road and one cultivated by **Hidayat Khan Baloch**; only the mazar and its three-gaz enclosure remain (p. 298, folio 301).
- **شاہ یعقوب زنجانی المشہور صدر دیوان** — outside Shah Alami Gate; a **Sayyid** from **Zanjan**, came "۵۳۵[OCR?] ہجری", d. "۵۵۶[OCR?] ہجری"; five graves on a raised platform with an inscription, his own faced with **red stone**; grand three-mihrab mosque; **a former Thursday mela and a great Rajab mela, both decayed, and its graveyard closed** (p. 301, folio 304); contents "مزار سید صدر دیوان[OCR?] ۳۰۴" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **امام بارہ شیعہ امامیہ and گامی شاہ** — outside Bhati Gate south of Data Ganj Bakhsh; a domed rauza "بنام نہاد امام حسین صاحب کے" over a **sard-khana** with **replica graves** of Imam Hasan and Imam Husain carrying the taziya, and the grave of **Gami Shah**, "بانی مبانی اس مکان امام بارے کا"; patrons **Nawab Nawazish Ali Khan Qizilbash and Nawab Nasir Ali Khan**; "یہ مکان مربع قوم شیعہ امامیہ کا ہے"; the **Zuljanah procession and the 1849 riot** (pp. 301–302, folios 304–305); contents "امام بارہ شیعہ امامیہ ۳۰۴" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **شاہ سروانی / رکن الدین** — old tomb north of "موضع غزنگ[OCR?]"; **Chishtiya**, murid of **Shaikh Shams-ud-Din Turk Panipati**; lifelong in **Ahmadabad and the Deccan[OCR?]**; d. "سنہ ۶۳۱[OCR?]", **no era printed**, the year from the phrase "پیر سروانی[OCR?]"; **urs twice a year, 9 Safar and 9 Rabi al-Awwal, with food and qawwali**; dug up under the Sikhs and restored under the British by a descendant (pp. 302–303, folios 305–306); the contents' "مقبرہ شاہ مردانی[OCR?] ۳۰۵" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3) is on this printed page and is probably this tomb — both spellings flagged, **not asserted**.
- **The Chisht family graves at میدان زرین خان[OCR?]** — pakka enclosure with mosque, garden and pakka graves of **Muhammad Siddiq (d. 8 Zil-Hajj 990 AH)**, **Muhammad Salim (d. 23 Zil-Hajj 1030 AH)**, **Muhammad Abdul Khaliq (d. 16 Zil-Hajj 1035, no era)**, **Fazil Khurd** and **Fazil Kalan**, learned faqirs of abstinence, none of the three dates flagged; succession to **Khizr Shah**, who reached 101 years and died "۷ ذی الحج سنہ ۱۲۴۶[OCR?] ہجری", then to **Rukn-ud-Din**, who died a few years ago; family **Sabiriya Chishtiya**, silsila connecting with **Shah Sarwani** (pp. 303–304, folios 306–307); contents "مقابر میدان زین خان[OCR?] ۳۰۶" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3), the name flagged in both partials.
- **شاہ کنہہ[OCR?]** — north of Shah Abul Muali's tomb outside Mochi Gate; pakka enclosure with many graves, the tomb rebuilt **1294[OCR?] AH** by **Jawahar Shah**, who became **sajjada nashin in the Naushahi silsila**; a two-storey chaubara by **Alahi Shah, mujawir**; dalan, kothri and well for the faqirs (p. 304, folio 307); contents "مقبرہ شاہ کہنہ[OCR?] ۳۰۷" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **فضل شاہ مجذوب** — a faqir who wandered Lahore in the last years of Sikh rule, from **mauza Masidpur[OCR?] Kuhna, Zafarwal, Sialkot**; a mosque post and then **spectacle-making** before faqiri; murid of **Rahman Shah, a Naushahi faqir**; died of **dropsy** "دہم ساون سمن ۱۹۱۰[OCR?] بکرمی"; khanqah between Masti and Kashmiri gates inside a government garden granted to Nawab Nawazish Ali Khan; son **Buland Shah** sold the properties and died destitute; granddaughter **Nawardah Begum** lives in a purdah house inside the mazar's enclosure; **no karamat is narrated at all** (pp. 325–326, folios 332–333); contents "خانقاہ فضل شاہ[OCR?] ۳۳۴[OCR?]" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3), number flagged and not equal to the folio.
- **شاہ رستم غازی** — a famous and blessed mazar on a **high mound** west of Nawan Kot; "عالم فاضل یکتائے زمانہ"; the damaged phrase "علمی استہا[OCR?] اور زیب النسا بیگم کا" appears to make him **Zeb-un-Nisa Begum's teacher**; d. "سنہ ۱۰۶۰[OCR?] ہجری"; **Zeb-un-Nisa built his red-stone tomb and marble grave**, pulled down by Ranjit Singh; two underground chambers holding four graves, **the women's and men's separate** (p. 312, folio 315); contents "مزار شاہ رستم غازی[OCR?] ۳۱۵" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **جنگلی چراغ شاہ, سید علی شاہ and چراغ شاہ** — so named because the murid **Chiragh Shah** planted many trees, "جنگلی پنجابی زبان میں … درختوں کو کہتے ہیں"; **Sayyid Ali Shah**, "قوم کا سید گیلانی", came from the **Deccan** in Ranjit Singh's early administration and d. "سنہ ۱۲۳۳[OCR?] ہجری"; Chiragh Shah his **جانشین**, d. "۱۲۵۵" (unflagged), built the present four-minaret pakka building; two more graves of their **servants**; pakka mosque, well and kotha attached (p. 314, folio 321). The contents' "خانقاہ سید علی ۳۲۵[OCR?]" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3) may be this khanqah — number flagged and not equal to the folio, **not asserted**.
- **ابوالخیر** — Baghdad scholar who settled at Lahore in Shah Jahan's era, taught by order of Alamgir and Zeb-un-Nisa[OCR?], died aged 105 in Muhammad Shah's reign and was **buried inside his own madrasa**, his grave still pakka and "لوگ اسکا بہت ادب کرتے ہیں"; now under trees with other dervish-students' graves nearby (pp. 362–363, folios 369–370; p. 365, folio 372). His khalifa **محمد نعیم** went on teaching in the madrasa until his own death (p. 363, folio 370).
- **مدرسہ / گڑہی شاہو، formerly خیر گڑہ[OCR?]** — fort-like madrasa built by the subah of Lahore's order, with a pakka domed mosque, students' cells and a fort-style rampart, **charged on the Lahore treasury**; later seized by the plunderer **شاہو**, whence the name, then held by four Shaikhs whose progeny **still hold it** (pp. 362–365, folios 369–372). The contents' "گدھی شاہ ابو[OCR?] ۳۶۹" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3) falls on this printed page and may be this foundation (or Abul Khair's grave in it) — both readings flagged, **not asserted**.
- **خانقاہ کہہوٹے[OCR?] شاہ** — a Lahore khanqah named only as a landmark for Bagh Raja Dina Nath, nothing said of its saint (p. 353, folio 360).
- **تکیہ[OCR?] بابا فرید گنج شکر** — a Lahore takia named only as a landmark for the two ranis' samadhs (p. 443, folio 450).
- **شیخ طاہر's enclosure** — named only as the enclosure wall beside which Maulvi Jami Lahori's enclosure stands (p. 294, folio 297).
- **پرانا مقبرہ in Government House's lower storey** — an unnamed old tomb whose wide outer chabutra set the octagonal plan of the kothi built over it; one dome's doorway "مسدود", blocked (p. 396, folio 403). May be the Badr-ud-Din Gilani tomb above, described from the building's side — **not asserted**.

### Muslim graveyards and burial grounds

- **گورستان بیگم پورہ** — with its **Qadam-e-Rasul**, the graves of **Nawab Abd-us-Samad Khan** and **Zakariya Khan Bahadur**, a great deorhi, dalans, a ruined garden and a **kashi-kari mosque** with a marble Kalima slab and two hadiths (pp. 275–278, folios 278–281); contents "گورکستان بیگم پور[OCR?] ۲۷۸" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).
- **نواب ذکریا خان's graveyard complex, Mughalpura** — the Nawab a Mughalpura resident; men's and women's enclosures and a **chini-kar mosque**, "یہ مسجد نواب زکریا خان کی یادگار ہے"; a second mausoleum in the complex recorded only as **حضرت ایشان [OCR?]** (pp. 90–91, folios 93–94). **May be the same complex as گورستان بیگم پورہ** above (Zakariya Khan's grave, a chini-kar/kashi-kari mosque) but the two partials place them at Mughalpura and Begampura — **not resolved; both kept**.
- **گورستان میانی و پنج پیر ڈہیر** — two miles south (pp. 291–292, folios 294–295). **Rani Gul Begum's enclosure is the second in the Miani graveyard** (p. 292, folio 295; p. 293, folio 296).
- **قبور تکیہ انبلی والہ, the Gilani Sayyids' graveyard** — outside Lohari Gate, five platforms, marble-set "بادشاہان اسلام کے وقت"; **سید صوفی** "صاحب عبادت و ریاضت و کرامت" d. "۱۰۵۰[OCR?] ہجری", **سید قاسم** d. "۱۰۸۸[OCR?] ہجری", **سید جعفر** d. "ماہ صفر ۱۰۶۵[OCR?] ہجری", with **سید عمر، سید ہاشم، سید عبد القادر المشہور شاہ گدا، سید اسماعیل، پیر محمد شاہ، شاہ سردار / شاہ سروار[OCR?]**; stripped by Ranjit Singh so that the mazars collapsed, then **settled with rent-paying tenants by its own custodian** (pp. 308–309, folios 311–312); contents "قبور تکیہ [illegible] والہ ۳۱۱" (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3).

### Mosques

- **مسجد بادشاہی** — built from the red stone left over from Mian Mir's rauza; **Fidai Khan Koka** superintendent; barracks, arsenal and gun platform; **nikah cess, mutawalli, committee, madrasa**, Nisbet's[OCR?] darbar (pp. 138–142, folios 141–145).
- **مسجد مستی دروازہ** — founded by **Maryam Zamani**, wife of Jahangir; "۱۰۲۳" on the arch; powder works, nazul, restored by MacGregor in 1850[OCR?] (pp. 142–143, folios 145–146).
- **مسجد خورد نواب وزیر خان** — the zenana mosque of the governor's fallen palace, south of Taksali Darwaza; still in use (p. 143, folio 146).
- **تکسالی والی مسجد** — beside Shah Jahan's dar al-zarb (mint) where the rupee was struck; mostly fallen, no trace of the mint (p. 144, folio 147).
- **اونچی مسجد, the old Bhati Darwaza mosque** — بازار لکو ہارے[OCR?]; Alamgiri era, founder unknown; in use (p. 145, folio 148).
- **مسجد طلائی / سنہری مسجد, Kashmiri Bazaar** — founded by **نواب میر سید بکہاری[OCR?] خان**, Chishtiya devotee of Miran Sayyid Bhikh[OCR?] Chishti; 1163[OCR?] AH; **shops as endowment**; two fatwas; Akali seizure; the founder "مختار ومدارالمہام سلطنت" under Mir Muin-ul-Mulk's widow, murdered in the palace, d. 1165[OCR?] AH and **declared a martyr by the author** (pp. 145–149, folios 148–152).
- **مسجد بازار بی[OCR?]** — Shahjahani, founder **Ghulam Mahindi Khan** by inscription, year unreadable; powder store under the Sikhs; revived by petition and a butcher's money; **imamat in one family** (p. 150, folio 153).
- **مسجد پری محل** — built by **Nawab Wazir Khan** opposite Shah Alami Gate for his own prayers only; never desolate (p. 151, folio 154). The **عمارت پری محل** itself is listed among the range's havelis (pp. 326–347, folios 333–354) and as his memorial inside the city, bought from the Sarkar and rooted out to its foundations (p. 358, folio 365).
- **پست مسجد / نیچی مسجد** — founder **Zulfiqar Khan**, "سر دفتر" to Haibat Khan[OCR?], subah of Lahore under the Lodhis; **no endowment, mulla kept by the mohalla** (p. 151, folio 154).
- **مسجد محمد صالح کمبو, Mochi Darwaza** — founder **Munshi Muhammad Salih**, diwan of Punjab, Kambo by caste, nephew and son-in-law of the author of *Bahar-e Danish*; 1058[OCR?] AH; **shops sunk half into the risen bazaar** (p. 152, folio 155).
- **مسجد چنیان والی[OCR?]** — founder **Nawab Sarfaraz Khan**, faujdar, protégé of Dara Shikoh[OCR?]; 1082[OCR?] / 1080[OCR?] AH; gateway stripped, **land sold by an imam, now in Wahhabi possession** (pp. 152–155, folios 155–158).
- **مسجد وزیر خان** — founder **Shaikh Ilm-ud-Din Ansari**, physician of Chiniot, who cured Shah Jahan's mother and lanced Nur Jahan's boil by a footprint stratagem; 1043[OCR?]/1044[OCR?] AH; **sixteen cells, hundred-square-yard tank, bookbinders' waqf** (pp. 155–162, folios 158–165); named again as the Nawab's memorial inside the city (p. 356, folio 363); repaired for thousands by Mian Nura (pp. 173–176, folios 176–179).
- **The unnamed mosque of the Dilawar Khan land case** — Mohalla Takiya Sadhuwan area; small because of the encroachment, "مگر بسبب قدمت کے مشہور زیادہ ہے"; **imam by the mutawalli's permission**; rebuilt by **Nawab Abd al-Majid Khan** (p. 170, folio 173).
- **مسجد محلہ تکیہ سادہوان** — built 1266[OCR?] AH by **Nur Muhammad Sadhu** on land taken from the takiya's courtyard (pp. 170–171, folios 173–174).
- **ضابطہ خان and مرزا محمد المشہور میرزا موٹا's mosque** — Zabita Khan a devout ascetic, d. 1251[OCR?]; the mosque rebuilt 1278[OCR?] with **shops below and a waqf residence for the imam**; imitation domes on the parapet (pp. 171–172, folios 174–175).
- **سید امیر شاہ's mosque** — rebuilt 1285[OCR?] by a wardi major in the English army; in the form of the Sunehri Masjid (pp. 172–173, folios 175–176).
- **صوفی ولی مسجد** — founder **Haji Maula Dad**, who **takes the two shops' rent himself**; the mulla's son evicted through the court; five hundred rupees for land taken for the road (p. 173, folio 176).
- **The kotwali mosque of میان نورا ایمان والہ / نور محمد** — titled by Ranjit Singh; mosque of 1239[OCR?] (pp. 173–176, folios 176–179).
- **میان نورا's second mosque in Kashmiri Bazaar** — his second foundation; he gave a haveli to **Maulvi Jan Muhammad**, a famous preacher of the Sikh era with thousands at his Friday sermons, **whose descendants hold the mosque** with his son **فضل حق** (pp. 173–176, folios 176–179; p. 175, folio 178).
- **مسجد پہولیان والی and میاں عمر دین** — Lohari Mandi; rebuilt 1282 AH; founder an employee of the Barrack Mastery department, the author's own relation to him cut by the chunk boundary (p. 176, folio 179).
- **مسجد شیخ نواب امام الدین خان** — Mohalla Chehla[OCR?]; founder **nazim of Kashmir at Ranjit Singh's darbar**, son of Shaikh Ghulam Muhi ud Din; 1266 AH; **care vested in his son** (pp. 177–178, folios 180–181).
- **مسجد حفیظ چابک سوار / محمد حفیظ "حفیظ ٹنڈا[OCR?]"** — Rang Mahal bazaar by the Mission School; **shop rents made waqf**; the horseman's false oath, his hands cut off, d. 1293[OCR?] (pp. 178–179, folios 181–182).
- **مسجد موران طوایف** — Papar Mandi, Shah Alami; 1223[OCR?] AH; **imam's shop rents, resident darweshes, a Maharaja-appointed imamat and a madrasa** (pp. 179–181, folios 182–184).
- **مسجد بوکن خان** — Mochi Gate; 1255[OCR?] AH by the daroga of Ranjit Singh's special stable; **garden and hujras for darweshes**; chronogram by Farid, poet of Lahore (pp. 181–182, folios 184–185).
- **مسجد امام شاہ والی** — rebuilt 1294[OCR?] AH at his own cost by **Nawab Abdul Majid Khan, rais of Lahore** (pp. 182–183, folios 185–186).
- **مسجد ملا مجید, Mohalla Chehl Bibian** — founder a Wilayati[OCR?] trader who built plain in 1235[OCR?] and grand in 1294; died before the whitewash; completed by the mohalla and a Canal Department officer (pp. 183–184, folios 186–187).
- **مسجد دایہ انگا** — one of two honoured wet-nurses who settled mohallas, each leaving only a pakka mosque (pp. 86, 88; folios 89, 91); wet-nurse of Jahangir's harem, name **Zeb-un-Nisa[OCR?]**; powder store, then Mr Cope's[OCR?] residence, then a railway bungalow (pp. 167–168, folios 170–171); listed again among the range's mosques (pp. 250–252, 267–268, 278, 294; folios 253–255, 270–271, 281, 297 — reference given collectively for five mosques).
- **مسجد دایہ لاڈو** — **wet-nurse of Prince Salim, i.e. Jahangir[OCR?]**; a damaged clause ties her to a **Chishti** "who was the qutb[OCR?] of his time" and to the prince's birth at **Fatehpur Sikri**; husband **Muhammad Ismail**, also a lifelong devotee; **built her mosque and her grave in its courtyard in her own lifetime**; d. "سنہ ۱۱۱۹[OCR?] ہجری"; the mosque's **thirty-year sanyasi occupation, forcible retaking and failed petition** (p. 86, folio 89; pp. 299–301, folios 302–304).
- **مسجد نقیبان** — named in the range's mosque list, no detail recorded (pp. 250–252, 267–268, 278, 294; folios 253–255, 270–271, 281, 297 — collective reference).
- **مسجد محمد صالح سندہی** — founder a **diwan of the suba, Shah Jahan's era** (pp. 250–252, 267–268, 278, 294; folios 253–255, 270–271, 281, 297 — collective reference). Possibly the same founder as مسجد محمد صالح کمبو above, or as the **محمد صالح** of *Bahar-e-Danish* (pp. 312–313, folios 315–316) — the nisbas differ (Sindhi / Kambo) and this is **not resolved**.
- **مسجد میان محمد سلطان ٹہیکہ دار** — the contractor's own mosque (pp. 250–252, 267–268, 278, 294; folios 253–255, 270–271, 281, 297 — collective reference). Distinct, as far as the partials say, from his travellers' mosque below.
- **مسجد خواجہ ایاز** — built by **خواجہ محمد ایاز، mir-e-imarat of Shalamar Bagh** under Shah Jahan as his memorial, with a **marble hadith slab** and the legend "بندہ درگاہ خواجہ محمد ایاز", and a garden now held by the **Sardaran Sindhanwala[OCR?]** (p. 279, folio 282; p. 278, folio 281; and pp. 250–252, 267–268, 278, 294; folios 253–255, 270–271, 281, 297 — collective reference). **Deliberately not mapped**; see Doubts.
- **A domed travellers' mosque built by محمد سلطان ٹہیکہ دار** — west of his sarai "مسافروں کی نماز کے لئے", with the "دو چار مسجدین" he built against the many old mosques he demolished (p. 358, folio 365; p. 360, folio 367).
- **A half-demolished (نیم مسمار) mosque near Sarai Muhammad Sultan** — half its bricks taken out, intact until "اس نے وہ خرید کر شہید کی", he bought it and martyred it (p. 358, folio 365).
- **The mosque with minaret and dome adjoining the Garhi Shahu compound's western[OCR?] wall** (p. 365, folio 372).
- **موتی مسجد / موتی مندر** — inside Lahore Fort, named only within the collective fort–Hazuri Bagh–Shalamar entry (pp. 338–348, folios 345–355); a religious site carried here so it is not lost in that secular entry.

### Hindu temples, shivalas, thakurdwaras and asthans

- **Shiwala north of مسجد وزیر خان** — on an appropriated mosque enclosure; rebuilt by **Raja Dina Nath**; **no stipend or jagir, living on daily offerings**; Dina Nath's son and Kashmiri Pandits in attendance; an **unnamed majzub's samadh** stands where he sat (p. 94, folio 97).
- **شوالہ راجہ دینا ناتہہ** by the Kotwali — built during his diwani; **land, stipend and shop rent**; Kashmiri Pandits and named family members spending; **Monday special puja**; land taken for the Kotwali maidan in "۱۸۵۰ [OCR?] ء" (pp. 95–97, folios 98–100).
- **شوالہ بخشی بہگت رام** — Samvat 1900[OCR?] Bikrami, the date said to be inscribed; the confiscated village; the Dharamshala platform; the **gilded samadh and portrait**; thirteen fountains; a twelve-image inventory; the five-hundred-rupee waterworks award (pp. 97–101, folios 100–104).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ راجہ تیجا سنگہ**, end of Moti Bazar — built under English rule twenty-five years before writing on a demolished haveli; **shop rents from its own frontage**; costly vestments and jewellery on the Krishna and Radha[OCR?] images; "صبح شام صدہا[OCR?] ارادتمند" (pp. 103–104, folios 106–107). **Deliberately not mapped** — not shahwala-teja-singh-mandir; see Doubts.
- **شوالہ گلاب رائے جمعدار** — inside Masti Gate behind Jamadar Khushhal Singh's haveli, its founder a jamadar of Ranjit Singh's darbar; the partial's entry breaks off (p. 104, folio 107).
- **An unnamed temple with fourteen shops** — gilded dome, red stone, **no patron but its Brahmin servant**, "بے مرمت و خراب پڑا ہے"; entry begins in chunk 007 (p. 105, folio 108).
- **ہنومان جی کا مندر**, Moti Bazaar — sindhur-smeared Hanuman image and a dark Krishan image, rebuilt by a sarraf, **pujari Thakur Das living on offerings**; **گنیش داس و مہیش داس**, two Bairagis, the building's first holders (pp. 105–106, folios 108–109).
- **شوالہ تر پولیہ والہ** — claimed as old as the city's settlement; silver jalhari, marble screen, **mahants' samadhs in its dalans, jogi pujaris, shops**, a water-giving Brahmin at the door, a municipal demolition at the door, and the theft and subscription-remade jalhari; eponym **پریم نانہہ[OCR?] جوگی**; late mahant **بابا پریم ناتہہ[OCR?]**, "اُسکے صاحب باطن ہونے مین شک نہین کرتے" (pp. 106–108, folios 109–111).
- **بیکنٹہہ داس کا ٹہاکر دوارہ**, Chakla Bazaar — claimed "ہزارہا سال" old but "کوئی ٹکڑا عمارت کہنہ کا باقی نہین"; rebuilt ten years before writing **by subscription** (p. 114, folio 117).
- **شوالہ دیوان شنکر داس[OCR?]** — founder a Diwan under the Sikhs and Honorary[OCR?] Magistrate[OCR?] of Lahore under the English; **Pandits Prem Nath and Shiv Nath serving, paying a Brahmin a salary** (pp. 114–115, folios 117–118).
- **شوالہ رکناتہہ[OCR?] مستری[OCR?] / رگناتہہ[OCR?] مشر[OCR?]** — built forty years before writing at one Brahmin's expense; kothris for pujaris; brass jalhari; **mahant Sundar Nath Jogi** (pp. 115–116, folios 118–119).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ باکہو مشر** — gilded kalas, mirror-work, roofed courtyard against the rain; built by subscription by **باکہو مشر المشہور لولہا مشر**, who "تمام آمدنی یہ مندر کی آراستگی پر خرچ کر دیتا ہی" (pp. 116–117, folios 119–120).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ / مندر بانکے بہاری**, near Bhai Vasti Ram's haveli — "لاہور کے نامی گرامی مندرون مین ہی", six images, gilded and mirrored upper storey, **naqara at arti** (pp. 117–118, folios 120–121); named again as the city temple on which a dependent thakurdwara relies (p. 442, folio 449).
- **An unnamed Thakurdwara attached to مندر بانکے بہاری** — its **offerings taken by Banke Bihari's mahant, who appoints its pujari Tehra Das[OCR?]** (p. 442, folio 449).
- **An unnamed thakurdwara** — entry opens before p. 119; image dug out at **Kankhal[OCR?] near Hardwar** on the strength of a dream and carried through **six custodians** to Lahore; **offerings-only economy, Ekadashi mela** (pp. 119–120, folios 122–123).
- **بیشنو دیوی کا مندر** — Mohalla Talwara, Bhati[OCR?] Darwaza; stone murti imported from Chowk Bala[OCR?] Singh, Amritsar, by **Bhagu Bhagat**; devi-dwara built **by chanda forty years ago** (pp. 120–121, folios 123–124).
- **مکان شوالہ ولباغ رے (Wilbagh[OCR?]/Dilbagh[OCR?] Rai)** — Mohalla Talwara; founder diwan of the household of Raja Dhyan[OCR?] Singh, wazir; built on disputed well-land at his own cost (pp. 121–122, folios 124–125).
- **مکان شوالہ ٹہبی والہ** — Mohalla Thibi, east[OCR?] of Raja Suchet Singh's haveli, now the Lahore Tehsil office; **subterranean, a storey and a half below ground, lamp burning all day**; two samadhs of earlier mahants in the court; rebuilt by **Raja Dina Nath** (pp. 122–124, folios 125–127).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ چور مور والا** — lane of Dai Bhuli[OCR?]; two and a half storeys, mirror-work; named from the thief-and-peacock tale told to Ranjit Singh; founded by **the mother of Kanhaiya Lal Kamboh[OCR?]** (pp. 124–125, folios 127–128).
- **مکان بہدر کالی (in-city)** — Mohalla Pahla[OCR?] Lakhpat Rai; small, old, **heavily worshipped; takes the overflow of the Naya Zabeg[OCR?] mela** (pp. 125–126, folios 128–129). Related to the Niaz Beg Bhadr Kali below as the partial states; distinct site.
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ جوالا دئی** — Mohalla Wachhowali; Krishn ji and Radha ji murtis; **founded and made waqf by a woman**, nine years before writing (pp. 126–127, folios 129–130).
- **مکان رام دوارہ** — Mohalla Wachhowali; **twice-daily katha, tulsi malas, twice-daily langar**; built six[OCR?] years before writing (pp. 127–128, folios 130–131).
- **بہیروجی کا استہان** — Mohalla Wachhowali; three stacked chabutras, marble shrine, **roadside offerings lifted at once**; pujaris' house opposite (pp. 128–129, folios 131–132).
- **مندر باوا مہر داس, the enclosure of four mandirs** — Mohalla Wachhowali; the oldest **not less than two hundred years**; two shivalas of Samat 1930, and the **Devi who came out of the ground in Samat 1931 / 1875 AD** (pp. 129–132, folios 132–135).
- **شوالہ پنڈت رادہاکشن** — Lagshti[OCR?] Bazaar toward Mohalla Said Mitha; a **gold-plated Hanuman murti in place of the kalas**; "سمت ۱۸۴۷[OCR?] بکرمی" (p. 132, folio 135).
- **مندر کالی دیوی** — in Kali Mata ki Gali off Lagshti[OCR?] Bazaar; black stone image under a gilded dome; **Navratri mela, pilgrims from other countries, Pandit Bishan Das mahant** (pp. 132–133, folios 135–136).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ پنڈت رادہاکشن** — inside the pandit's own haveli toward Mohalla Syed Mitha; "سنمت ۹۱۹[OCR?]"; **the Chief Pandit succession** (pp. 133–134, folios 136–137).
- **چوبارہ چہجو بہگت** — one cult on two sites: his original dwelling in علاقہ گنڈ[OCR?] ڈہل[OCR?] with a shivala, a thakurdwara and **the tank in which he made the Ganga flow** (pp. 134–136, folios 137–139); and the out-of-city chaubara with his samadh and shop — a Bhatia[OCR?] sarraf of Shah Jahan's reign, friend of Shah Bilawal Lahori and Mian Wadda, d. Samat 1696[OCR?] / 1053[OCR?] AH or vanished sitting in the chaubara, **sajjada appointed by the three rulers of Lahore, Dau Panth[OCR?] celibacy rule, Monday and Tuesday qawwali, a village-sized estate** (pp. 184–186, folios 187–189).
- **A neighbourhood thakurdwara** — bought by the mohalla on a confectioners'[OCR?] shop site, ten or twelve years old (pp. 135–136, folios 138–139).
- **سادہو چوہڑا's well and shivala** — بازار مچہی ہٹہ[OCR?]; a sweeper-thief hanged, **the well endowed from his stolen goods and named after him**; **Shiv Gar Sanyasi** serves the shivala, built by bazaar subscription under Sikh rule (pp. 136–138, folios 139–141). The same sanyasi's own samadh-shivala is listed below.
- **استہان سیتلا ماتا** — outside the wall midway between Shah Alami and Lohari Gates; **smallpox vows, a worshipped tree and stone lion, rents and offerings, mortgaged land**, a hospital road bought across it; **resort of both communities** (pp. 186–188, folios 189–191). Named again only in cross-reference as the second Sitla Devi temple, "جسکا ذکر تحریر پر ہو چکا ہے" (p. 452, folio 459).
- **مندر سیتلا دیوی اندرون شہر** — Guzar Mochi, Mohalla Pir Bayan[OCR?]; small but "پوجا بہت بڑی ہوتی ہے"; a round pindi under a red ghilaf, a small image of Devi Sitla, **a smallpox vow of cash and a goat**, pujari **Mahtab Rai** (pp. 452–453, folios 459–460).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ باوا منگل داس** — outside the Delhi[OCR?] Gate; three mandirs (Krishan/Radha/Ram/Sita, Hanuman, a shivala); built under a Maharaja whose name the OCR lost; **cultivated land and offerings to the mahant** (pp. 188–189, folios 191–192).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ رانی لچہمی** — Ravi nala bank; Persian wheel and about ten[OCR?] ghumaon; **Bawa Ram Das Bairagi[OCR?] its pujari and owner** (pp. 199–200, folios 202–203).
- **ٹہاکر دوارہ بہوری سرکار** — Ravi nala bank; **land purchased and made waqf**, garden, mill, wells, resident gardeners and cultivators, **many samadhs of former successors**; Madho Das Bairagi[OCR?] holding مالکانہ قبضہ (pp. 200–201, folios 203–204).
- **ٹھاکردوارہ رانی جنداں** — outside Masti Gate; roofs fallen for want of care; **one Brahmin, Murli, living on the offerings** (p. 205, folio 208).
- **شوالہ نزد چاہ بدہو والہ[OCR?]** — founded in Akbar's time; **Phagan mela**; offerings plus **fourteen[OCR?] revenue-free bighas** held by the office; western door closed by a railway purchase at five hundred rupees; **Bera Nath[OCR?] Jogi hereditary mahant** (p. 214, folio 217).
- **شوالہ و دیوی دوارہ گنگا پربت[OCR?]** — south of the railway stopping-ground; founded twenty-five[OCR?] years before writing by **Ganga Parbat Sadh**, whose own samadh is inside a mandir he built in his lifetime; a marble Devi worshipped, a footprint shrine, a dome over a well; **three roadside shops whose rent the pujari takes**; **Ganesh Parbat now mahant** (pp. 214–216, folios 217–219).
- **مکان مرگہٹ, the Hindu cremation ground** — outside Taksali Gate; enclosure built by British order; peepal and banyan, **many samadhs**; a government sepoy guard, an attendant at one anna per corpse, and **Rai Mela Ram's bathing tank with separate ghats** (p. 223, folio 226).
- **شوالہ لالہ نہال چند** — on the north bank of the marghat tank; **Narain Puri Sanyasi its pujari** (p. 223, folio 226). Distinct from his five-mandir thakurdwara below.
- **شوالہ لالہ / دیوان رتن چند مع تالاب و سرائے** — outside Shah Alami Gate; founder huzur-navis under Ranjit Singh and later **Honorary Magistrate of Lahore**, building in the English era; **women's ghat, canal-fed tank, sarai, shops and a great mandi**; his samadh and a grand Thakurdwara built by his eldest son (pp. 223–225, folios 226–228); named as founder and contractor also at pp. 35–36, 65, 69; folios 38–39, 68, 72.
- **مندر بہدر کالی, Niaz Beg** — six miles[OCR?] south; **no image, only a pindi[OCR?]**; origin unknown to anyone; **the largest mela in Lahore, in Jeth, attended by Muslims too**; the Devi who refused the larger dome (pp. 229–230, folios 232–233).
- **مندر تہان بہیرو, Ichhra** — three miles[OCR?] south; pre-Sikh worship by **Godar[OCR?]** of the Bania caste and his son **Masti Ram**; **Moran's hundred cartloads of bricks** for a cure; mandirs and tank built by the **Nazims of Multan**; **Jogi possession upheld against the ancient occupants in a Settlement suit**; **langar, Bhadon mela, Sunday pilgrimage, twelve ghumaon[OCR?] revenue-free** (pp. 230–233, folios 233–236).
- **مندر ٹہاکردوارہ و شوالہ سنت شاہ و دیویدیال[OCR?]** — a marble upper-storey Thakurdwara and facing Shiwala built by two renowned sahukars, with **shops below whose rent funds it**, a new well and red-stone sabil, a piped jal dhara through a brass snake, and **a separate lane staircase by which women come to worship** (pp. 432–435, folios 439–442).
- **مندر ٹہاکردوارہ لالہ نہال چند** — begun on **nazul land auctioned by government** and bought by a Central Jail contractor; "یہ ایک مندر نہیں ہے بلکہ پانچ مندر ہیں", five mandirs in one, four corner shrines of Shivji, Devi ji, Sumaj[OCR?] ji and Ganesh ji still "نیم تیار", half-ready (pp. 435–437, folios 442–444).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ ماکہو شاہ** — a house bought seven years ago by the sahukar **Makho Shah** and made a thakurdwara, still haveli-shaped, with stone images of Sri[OCR?] Krishan ji and Radha ji and the **pujari Ram Kishan Das Bairagi** in the upper storey (pp. 437–438, folios 444–445).
- **مندر دیوی دوارہ رجو و دہرمن** — an old and much-revered Devi Dwara in کوچہ رجو و دہرمن, **the lane itself named for it**; sanctum sunk like a cellar, eight steps down, a clay[OCR?] pindi marking where Rajo, Dharman and the Devi's manifestation sank into the earth, two sacred charpais, **two Ashtami melas and year-round jagas** (pp. 438–440, folios 445–447). **رجو و دہرمن**: two women of Akbar's era, of the اڑوٹری[OCR?] qaum, to whom the Devi gave darshan on call; they revived a dead child, passed Akbar's test of the seeds, and sank into the earth rather than marry Jahangir's Muslim subah of Lahore (pp. 438–439, folios 445–446).
- **ہمری[OCR?] گیان مندر و شوالہ ایشری[OCR?] پرشاد کایتہ** — outside the Mori/مندری[OCR?] gate, built in the Sikhs' time by Ishri[OCR?] Prashad Kayath, **with much land, four کوٹہر[OCR?] and a well**; its platform the lecture ground of **پنڈت سردہا رام پہلوری[OCR?]**, a learned man of Sanskrit and Shastra whose katha drew great throngs against the Arya and Brahmo, d. "چند سال" ago, and then of **پنڈت گوپی ناتہہ، ایڈیٹر اخبار عام** (pp. 440–441, folios 447–448; p. 441, folio 448).
- **An unnamed شوالہ و ٹہاکردوارہ pair outside Mori Gate** — in one enclosure with **three roadside shops let to woodsellers** and many peepals; the partial's entry breaks off (p. 441, folio 448).
- **مندر ٹہاکردوارہ باوا دیہو نا داس** — built by **Baba Dehona Das, sadhu**, a mile south-west of the بہری گیان[OCR?] مندر; no dome; a Shiv figure "لاہور کے اور کسی ٹہاکردوارہ میں نہیں پائی جاتی"; **the founder's own pakka samadh stands west of it**; **موران طوائف[OCR?]**, courtesan[OCR?] and devotee, helped build it, sank a new well and had a nishastgah built (pp. 442–443, folios 449–450; p. 442, folio 449).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ و شوالہ لالہ جسنا[OCR?] دیبر[OCR?] و جنگم** — Anarkali; built in two stages, Sambat 1910 and 1937, out of land bought from government, begging and subscription; **its owner-pujari aged eighty[OCR?] takes all Anarkali's Hindu offerings** (p. 445, folio 452).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ لالہ مہین لال[OCR?]** — small but fine, marble **جگ موہن** and eight-sided marble columns, one door into a public lane and one into the founder's own house, **expenses borne by his family[OCR?]** (pp. 446–447, folios 453–454).
- **مندر شوالہ لالہ چہوٹے لال** — an eight-sided red-stone shiwala with an underground jalhari, built by **Lala Durga[OCR?] Prashad** on the site of **بودہر داس[OCR?]**'s shiwala, with **eight shops' rent and a salaried Brahmin pujari** (pp. 447–449, folios 454–456). **بودہر داس[OCR?]**: a God-worshipping sadhu famous in the Sikhs' time, named for a بودہر tree he planted; "اصلی نام اُسکا شاید کوئی اور تہا" (p. 447, folio 454).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ پنڈت اوقت[OCR?] ناتہہ** — Kucha Jawahar Mal; **fully endowed by its childless founder**, named in the body as **پنڈت وشنا تہہ**, who demolished his own house to build it; **pujari Pandit Balkishan** (pp. 449–451, folios 456–458).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ چہوجہی مل کہتری** — Akbari Mandi; built on a demolished stable, **made over in trust[OCR?] to "جنار دہن", with its two endowed shops now taken by the founder's brother** (pp. 451–452, folios 458–459).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ عطر سنگہ صوبہ دار** — built Sambat 1934 in Katra[OCR?] Bor Bayan[OCR?], Guzar Mochi, its whole courtyard roofed; founder dead, **his son Indar Singh[OCR?] serving it** (pp. 453–454, folios 460–461).
- **شوالہ دہرم چند والہ** — a shiwala "عہد شاہان سلف کا", demolished and resited by **Dharm Chand Arora** in Sambat 1935, marble platform and jalhari, a Nandi Gan[OCR?] bull, a gilded chakra and trishul, and **a per-wedding levy for its pujari Salag Ram Pandit** (pp. 454–455, folios 461–462).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ رائے سنگہ رسالدار خلف کرم سنگہ** — Shah Alami bazaar front, Sambat 1938, on the site of an ancient mandir with a peepal and roofed well **whose land the municipality took for the water supply**; separate entrances for women by the lane and men from the bazaar; no well (pp. 455–456, folios 462–463).
- **مکان مندر بال ناتہا[OCR?] / the Mata temple of کوچہ بال ناتا** — very old, **the mohalla named for it**; a brass image **بال بائی[OCR?] ماتا** bought from a Muslim soldier, "تمام شہر اس ماتا کا بدل و جان معتقد ہے", **eight jagas a month**, caretaker-pujari **Junda Bhagat[OCR?]** (p. 456, folio 463; pp. 457–458, folios 464–465).
- **شوالہ سکہہ دیال سود** — between the Shah Alami and Mochi gates; **Sukh Dayal cut a portion off his own house forty years ago** to make it; silver jalhari, brass gagar, a dalan that is the mandir's below and the founder's family's above; **no endowment stated** (pp. 458–459, folios 465–466).
- **ٹہاکردوارہ لالہ بہگوان داس خلف دیوان زین چند[OCR?]** — **the book's own newest building**, on the southern bank of the late Diwan's tank; red-stone jag mohan, ship-chandelier, coloured mirror dariches, gilded flag and trishul, images of Sri Krishan ji, Mahadev ji and Sri Ganga ji worshipped day and night, quarters for several pujaris, a foundation slab, **fourteen waqf shops and an Ekadashi qawwali assembly** (pp. 459–462, folios 466–467, p. 461, folio 469).

### Sikh, Udasi and ascetic samadhs, dharamsalas and gurdwaras

- **حال مکان ٹالی صاحب (بابا اسری چند خلف بابا نانک)** — south of the lunatic asylum, abutting the railway; Sri Chand not counted among the ten Gurus but honoured as a sahibzada; the **Udwasi/Udasi** faqr defined, the **Nanakpanthi** sect named, and this gurdwara **"اس سب قوم کا مرجع"**; Nag Devta samadh; mandir of Sambat 1898 Bikrami; **a rupee a day stopped**; land lost to the railway and **the sacred tahli felled and regrown** (pp. 227–229, folios 230–232).
- **سمادہ باوا جہینگر شاہ المشہور ستہرا** — a Lahore faqir of Aurangzeb's time and chela of **Guru Har Rai**; **a paisa per shop per year in every city by Alamgir's order**; **the gaddi and masnad of all the Punjab's Suthra Shahi faqirs**; repeated demolition attempts for overlooking the fort (pp. 203–204, folios 206–207).
- **سمادہ بہائی وستی رام[OCR?]** — a renowned Granth-reciter **whom Ranjit Singh called his guru**; lakhs in nazr, **jagirs and allowances**; marble samadh on the pattern of the Darbar at Amritsar, against the fort's north wall, with fountains, inlay, a flag and a garden (pp. 201–203, folios 204–206). His haveli is the landmark for Thakurdwara Banke Bihari (pp. 117–118, folios 120–121).
- **مکان سمادہ حقیقت رائے** — a boy executed for words about the Prophet / the Ahl-e Bait under **Zakariya Khan**; **Basant mela with good offering income**; **the four-stage grant across three regimes** (pp. 207–209, folios 210–212).
- **بابا خدا سنگہ (real name جسونت سنگہ)'s dharamsala** — "ایک سکہہ موحد فقیر"; travelled to Iran[OCR?] and Kabul[OCR?], regarded by **Amir Dost Muhammad Khan**; dead eighteen years before the writing; founder of the dharamsala at the head of "چونی[OCR?] بازار", with **بہائی پریم سنگہ** his chela, successor and **gaddi nashin** (pp. 102–103, folios 105–106; p. 103, folio 106).
- **دہرم سال کوکہ, the Kuka dharamsal** — western lane of Moti Bazaar; fifteen years old, flourishing four or five, then **emptied by the Malerkotla prosecution** (p. 105, folio 108).
- **دہرم سال بہائی گوما سنگہ[OCR?]** — two miles out on the Shalamar Bagh road; built for Sikhs' overnight stay and bhang-drinking **with the sarkar's permission**; **forty bighas, a well and a mill feeding travellers**; Nihang Singh "تصویر والا بابا" (pp. 212–214, folios 215–217).
- **ملتانی دہرم سالہ** — outside Shah Alami Gate; **than of Bhairo ji**, samadhs of **Sambhal Nath** and **Bhol[OCR?] Nath**, a Brahmin water-giver, two wheel-wells; named from **Guru Hari[OCR?] Multani, chela of Sambhal Nath** (pp. 226–227, folios 229–230).
- **سمادہ بہائی روپا** — a God-fearing ascetic of Ranjit Singh's time, **accepted by Hindus and Muslims alike**, free of greed; **samadh built by the Maharaja's order** (pp. 197–198, folios 200–201).
- **شوالہ شیو گر سنیاسی** — his samadh in the sunk storey, **in which he sat down alive and the door was walled up**; forty years since; **one well and eight[OCR?] ghumaon held by his grand-disciple** (pp. 198–199, folios 201–202). He also serves Sadho Chuhra's shivala (pp. 136–138, folios 139–141).
- **مکان سمادہ دریاگر سنیاسی** — lived his whole life in an underground cellar and never begged; d. ten years before writing of his own sigh; **willed a kham samadh; sevaks still bring offerings** (pp. 209–210, folios 212–213).
- **سماوہ راجہ تیجا سنگہ / سچا سنگہ[OCR?]** — outside Masti Gate in Jamadar Khushhal Singh's garden; **octagonal, marble throughout, unfinished**; d. Samat 1919; work undertaken by the state's مختار (pp. 189–190, folios 192–193; p. 353, folio 360).
- **سماوہ رام سنگہ پسر جمعدار خوشحال سنگہ / شوالہ جمعدار خوشحال سنگہ** — Samat 1895 Bikrami, built in the father's lifetime for a son who died young after completing the Arabic and Persian sciences; **Shiv ji installed inside**, which is why it **survived Sikh destruction**; built by his disciple[OCR?] (pp. 190–191, folios 193–194; p. 353, folio 360).
- **سماوہ جمعدار خوشحال سنگہ** — "رکن اعظم سلطنت سکہہی", great builder in Lahore and Amritsar, d. 1844[OCR?] CE and **buried** at the spot; **his wife sati with him**; unfinished, no kalas (pp. 192–193, folios 195–196; p. 353, folio 360).
- **سماوہ راجہ اودہم سنگہ** — eldest son of Maharaja Gulab Singh, killed under the fort gateway wall with Kunwar Nau Nihal Singh; **eleven[OCR?] asthans of Shiv ji; golden kalas** (pp. 193–194, folios 196–197).
- **سماوہ راجہ سوچیت سنگہ** — killed by order of Raja Hira Singh's wizarat at **Khanqah Mian Wadda[OCR?]** under cannon fire; cremated where the samadh stands; **a Brahmin on the Jammu payroll serves the enclosure** (pp. 194–196, folios 197–199).
- **سماوہ سردار جواہر سنگہ** — maternal uncle of Dalip Singh, killed by the Sikh army at the end of 1847[OCR?] CE; founded by Dalip Singh's mother, unfinished at her exile, **now ownerless**; Sri Ganesh ji carved over the door (pp. 196–197, folios 199–200).
- **سمادہ مہاراجہ شیر سنگہ والی پنجاب** — killed with his son Partab Singh by the Sandhanwalia sardars in the **Shah Bilawal barahdari**; kham for years, then built by the family's adherents; the rani sati, **Rani Rind Awi[OCR?] with a dated marble epigraph**, Rani Partab Kunwar, and the samadhs of Budh Singh, Bur Singh and his sati wife, Naga Singh and three more servants; **one seventeen-year-old caretaker on a government salary** (pp. 210–212, folios 213–215).
- **سمادہ مہاراجہ کہڑک سنگہ و کنور نونہال سنگہ** — the joint building on the western edge of Ranjit Singh's chabutra; Kharak Singh's withdrawal after the killing of Sardar Chet Singh, his dying prophecy, the parapet at the Roshnai gate; **four and two sati samadhs, a Granth section, two gilded-kalas bungalows**; finished by the English (pp. 221–223, folios 224–226).
- **سمادہ رانی راجکوران المشہور رانی نکائین** — Ranjit Singh's wife and Kharak Singh's mother, who **held Sheikhupura and all Sandal Bar in jagir**; square, sixteen feet each side, with a gilded kalas (pp. 443–445, folios 450–452).
- **سمادہ رانی چند کنور** — Kharak Singh's wife and Nau Nihal Singh's mother, briefly mistress of the Punjab, imprisoned by Sher Singh and rumoured murdered by her maidservants; **left unfinished, no kalas raised, doors without leaves** (pp. 443–445, folios 450–452).
- **راجہ دہیان سنگہ's samadh** — at the centre of his own haveli, named within the collective haveli entry (pp. 326–347, folios 333–354).

### Brahmo and Christian foundations

- **مکان مندر برہم سماج** — the Brahmo sect's kothi-shaped place of worship in Anarkali outside Lohari Gate, **Sunday worship of "خدائے واحد" and a paid chowkidar** (p. 449, folio 456).
- **چھاونی میانمیر کا گرجا، سمیت میری میگلینز[OCR?]** — the cantonment church, plaster ruled to pass for white stone, coloured glass, a 125[OCR?] ft clock-and-bell minaret that is the cantonment's only minaret, marble حرم, قربان گاہ, the Ten Commandments carved in English, seating for about eight hundred (pp. 389–394, folios 396–401).
- **رومن کیتہلک والوں کا گرجا** — west of Anarkali, in a kachcha-walled enclosure; the partial's entry breaks off (p. 425, folio 432).
- **An unnamed church with a girls' school** — two bells, one rung at noon, a marble stoup of blessed water, a three-tier altar with an آلٹار[OCR?] and ٹہیبر[OCR?] ناکل[OCR?], images of Bibi Maryam, a life-size clay Christ, a black-robed Maryam, a childhood Christ and eleven angels, and a dais for the لاٹ پادری (pp. 426–429, folios 433–436).

### Tombs of rulers, nobles and their households (no cult recorded — weak candidates)

- **رانی گل بیگم** — married by **Maharaja Ranjit Singh** "حسب رسوم اہل اسلام کے", the barat coming from **Amritsar**; her jagir the largest of all and, under the English, "ایک ہزار دو سو روپیہ ماہوار"; **garden and tomb built in her own lifetime**, chronogram numeral "۱۲۴۷[OCR?]"; grave on a **black stone platform** with marble over it; d. "سنہ ۱۲۸۲[OCR?] ہجری"; adopted son **Sardar Khan** buried with her, his sons **Bahawal Khan and Jahangir Khan** surviving; **the second enclosure in the Miani graveyard** (p. 292, folio 295; p. 293, folio 296).
- **علی مردان خان** — Shahjahani counsellor, son of Ganj Ali Khan, **subahdar of Qandahar under the Safavids**, who handed the fort to Shah Jahan and rose to administer **Lahore, Delhi and Kashmir**; "کروڑون روپیہ اس کے ہاتہ سے صرف ہوے"; the **Nau Lakha** garden, the **Delhi canal** and a canal watering Shalamar credited to him; d. "سنہ ۱۰۶۹ [OCR?] ہجری"; **the tallest tomb in Lahore** (pp. 253–255, folios 256–258).
- **بہادر خان** — amir al-umara at **Akbar's** court, "شاہجہان [OCR?] ۱۰۱۰ [OCR?] مین فوت"; one of Lahore's ancient grand tombs by the Amritsar iron road; **the grave demolished under railway occupation**, over which the author signs his own verse (pp. 257–258, folios 260–261).
- **مخدومہ بیگم[OCR?] and نواب ابو الحسن خان** — her husband's tomb cost **ten lakh rupees**, was struck by **lightning**, burned and shattered, then **auctioned as nazul and quarried away**; she built her own tomb smaller out of deference (pp. 259–260, folios 262–263).
- **نواب نصرت خان المشہور نستر خان[OCR?]** — Chughtai amir, real name **Khwaja Sabir**, title **Khan-e Dauran Nusrat Khan**, chronogram "۱۰۷۷ [OCR?]" from **گنجینہ سروری**; his tomb made General Court Sahib's residence, its mosque absorbed and its grave floored over, then nazul, then sold to **Lala Mela Ram** (pp. 261–262, folios 264–265).
- **شرف النسا بیگم المعروف شیر نعیم بیگم[OCR?]** — "ہمشیرہ نواب خان بہادر"; the **سرو والا مقبرہ**; **daily Quran reading after Zuhr**, a **Quran and a sword** left in the tomb by her will, and the tomb kept stairless (p. 264, folio 267).
- **مقبرہ جانی خان / نظام الدولہ** — built in Ahmad Shah's era, the older tombs' tablets decayed (p. 169, folio 172); son of **Qamar-ud-Din Khan** killed at **Karnal**, who with **Mir Muin-ul-Mulk** defeated **Ahmad Shah Durrani** and remained "افسر فوج پنجاب"; d. "۱۱۹۴ [OCR?]"; **a zamindar now stores chaff in his tomb** (pp. 269–270, folios 272–273).
- **شاہنزادہ پرویز** — son of Jahangir, killed at Lahore by **Asaf Jah** at Shah Jahan's accession; his tomb's dome "مقبرہ نواب علی مردان خان سے کم نہین"; marble stripped by Ranjit Singh's order, "اب کچہہ بہی نہین" (pp. 270–271, folios 273–274).
- **نواب میان خان پسر نواب سعد اللہ خان** — natural son of Shah Jahan's wazir-e-azam; two grand havelis in Lahore; from **Chiniot**, whose **black stone** he used everywhere; d. "سنہ ۱۰۸۳ [OCR?] ہجری"; a tomb complex costing "کسی لاکہہ روپیہ", with **a mosque built only for beauty**; grave annihilated by Raja Suchet Singh, garden auctioned for 2,200 rupees (pp. 271–273, folios 274–276; p. 329, folio 336).
- **یکی[OCR?] خان and the سفید گنبد / لکا گنبد** — an octagonal dome east of Begampura, sound within and decayed above, its platform fallen; a noble of **Muhammad Shah's** era who d. "سنہ ۱۱۶۰ [OCR?] ہجری" (p. 278, folio 281).
- **زیب النسا بیگم / مخفی** — called **Aurangzeb's sister**; **Chauburji** is her garden gate, three of its four minarets standing; her garden counted **second[OCR?] to Shalamar**, its roads of red stone and its rauza marble from bottom to top; d. "سنہ ۱۰۸۰ ہجری" in the prose and "۱۰۹۰[OCR?]" in the chronogram; her tomb's stone taken for a marble baradari, the graves' place now "ایک مٹی کا تودہ", zamindars' houses built over its platform (pp. 309–312, folios 312–315; pp. 361–362, folios 368–369). She also built Shah Rustam Ghazi's tomb (p. 312, folio 315) and had Abul Khair teach by her order (pp. 362–363, folios 369–370).
- **The foundress's مقبرہ at موضع نیا کوٹ** — its stone prised out by Maharaja Ranjit Singh (p. 361, folio 368).
- **محمد صالح و شیخ عنایت اللہ کمبو's tomb** — two Lahore men, **sar-daftar of the suba** in Shah Jahan's era; **Bahar-e-Danish[OCR?]** composed by Salih and completed by Inayatullah; dates as printed make Inayatullah die first, "سنہ ۱۰۵۱[OCR?] ہجری", and Salih in "۱۰۸۰[OCR?]"; **their tomb became a powder store, then a kothi, then a kitchen** (pp. 312–313, folios 315–316).
- **نور الدین جہانگیر بادشاہ غازی** — northern bank of the **Ravi**, four miles north of Lahore; forty cells, four minarets, eleven surviving tanks, marble inlaid with agate, lapis, sapphire, coral and more, "اکثر نگہینے اتار بہی لئے گئے ہیں"; **jagirs and salaried Quran-readers**; an Afghan jagirdar quartered in it; marble sent to **Sri Darbar Sahib**; British embankment and repair (pp. 314–319, folios 321–326), with the adjoining **Shahjahani sarai** and its counted cells (pp. 319–320, folios 326–327).
- **آصف جاہ** — tomb second only to **Ali Mardan Khan's** in Lahore and comparable only to "مقبرہ تاج بی بی", its foundation dug out and only the marble ta'wiz left, "ہو بہو مثل تعویذ قبر شاہ جہانگیر کے" (pp. 321–324, folios 328–331).
- **نور جہان بیگم المشہور نور محل** — tomb with **twenty-eight outer openings** and two graves, hers and **her daughter's**, whose stone Ranjit Singh removed and whose **ebony coffins** were taken from the basement and the bodies buried in earth (pp. 321–324, folios 328–331).

### Secular, British-era and infrastructural sites (recorded for completeness; none is a shrine candidate)

- **Gardens** — باغ شالامار and its seven gardens, Angoori, Inayat[OCR?] Abad, Faiz Bakhsh, Hayat Bakhsh with the marble تخت شاہی, Farah Bakhsh, Mahtabi and Gulabi, the Sawan Bhadon, stone hammam, two imperial gates, canal and mangoes (pp. 349–352, folios 356–359), the **broken marble throne** ordered to Darbar Sahib, Amritsar (p. 349, folio 356) and **مکان نگارخانہ / نکاہ خانہ** (p. 351, folio 358); باغ آلوالیہ (p. 352, folio 359); باغ جمعدار خوشحال سنگہ (pp. 352–353, folios 359–360); باغ راجہ تیجا سنگہ (p. 353, folio 360); باغ راجہ دینا ناتہہ, where **Hindu and Muslim جشن alike were held** (pp. 353–354, folios 360–361); باغ لالہ رتن چند ڈاڑہی والہ with the آسمانی چاہ (pp. 354–355, folios 361–362); باغ بہائی مہان سنگہ, the first garden of the Sikh kingdom, **sold to the padris** (p. 355, folio 362); ڈیوڑہی باغ نو لکھہ (pp. 355–356, folios 362–363); بارہ دری باغ نواب وزیر خان / سو نخلہ[OCR?] وزیر خان (pp. 356–357, folios 363–364; p. 387, folio 394); چوبرجی (pp. 361–362, folios 368–369); بارہ دری باغ کامران (p. 367, folio 374).
