- **قلعہ لاہور, حضوری باغ and its بارہ دری** — pre-Akbari fort "مختصر اور خام"; Akbar's khwabgah, Shah Jahan's takhtgah and Mushamman[OCR?] Burj; **Hathi Paur** and **Roshnai** gates; barracks, treasury and the government liquor store; **the baradari built of stone stripped from Muslim tombs**; Shalamar on the model of the Shalamar Bagh of Kashmir by **Ali Mardan Khan** with **Nawab Fazil Khan** as mir-e-imarat and the plan by **جانی معمار** (pp. 338–348, folios 345–355).
- **Havelis, katras and the Pari Mahal** — کٹرہ حاجی امان خان / کٹرہ دولتہ / کٹرہ حکیم ولی شاہ, اندہی حویلی, حویلی دیوان لکہپت رائے و جسپت رائے, حویلی میاں خان with its **Rang / Tang Mahal** and **Qalai Khana**, حویلی ثانی پتہران والی, مبارک حویلی (Shah Shuja and the **Koh-i-Noor**), عمارت پری محل of Nawab Wazir Khan, حویلی کلو بائی[OCR?], حویلی میر جواد, and the havelis of کنور نونہال سنگہ, مہاراجہ کہڑک سنگہ, جمعدار خوشحال سنگہ, راجہ تیجا سنگہ, راجہ دہیان سنگہ, راجہ دینا ناتہہ and شیخ امام الدین (pp. 326–347, folios 333–354); حویلی مہاراجہ کہڑک سنگہ again (pp. 435–437, folios 442–444); حویلی راجہ سوچیت سنگہ / راجہ دہیان سنگہ reused as Tehsil, court and Government School (pp. 370–372, folios 377–379).
- **Sarais, tanks and public works** — سرائے محمد سلطان ٹہیکہ دار and لنڈہ بازار, with **the cold-water well and its two-month mela** (pp. 357–361, folios 364–368); سرائے المشہور گولیان والی at Ichhra (pp. 365–367, folios 372–374); تالاب رائے[OCR?] میلا رام ٹہیکہ دار (pp. 367–368, folios 374–375); سرائے و تالاب دیوان زین چند[OCR?], cross-referenced as already entered earlier in the book (p. 459, folio 466); مہاراجہ رنجیت سنگہ's five-mile embankment, actually attributed to شاہ عالمگیر (p. 361, folio 368); پختہ پل ریلوے دریائے راوی (pp. 409–410, folios 416–417); سٹیشن ریلوے (pp. 420–425, folios 427–432).
- **British-era public buildings** — مکان کوتوالی (pp. 368–370, folios 375–377); مکان تحصیل و منصفی (pp. 370–371, folios 377–378); کچہری صدر ضلع لاہور (pp. 371–376, folios 378–383); مکان سینٹ ہال (pp. 376–377, folios 383–384); مکان ہسپتال سرکاری (pp. 377–380, folios 384–387); مکان نیو کالج لاہور (pp. 381–384, folios 388–391); مکان آرٹ سکول یعنی مدرسہ صنعت کاری (pp. 384–387, folios 391–394); کوٹھی تار گھر (pp. 387–389, folios 394–396); گورنمنٹ ہوس / کوٹھی لفٹنٹ گورنر, octagonal on the old tomb's plan (pp. 394–399, folios 401–406); منٹگمری ہال و لارنس ہال (pp. 399–402, folios 406–409); مکان عجائب گاہ لاہور (pp. 402–405, folios 409–412); بندی خانہ یعنی زنانہ جیلخانہ (pp. 405–409, folios 412–416); سنٹرل جیل لاہور (pp. 410–415, folios 417–422); ڈسٹرکٹ جیل (pp. 415–417, folios 422–424); مکان پاگل خانہ (pp. 417–420, folios 424–427); مکان میڈیکل سکول لاہور (pp. 429–432, folios 436–439).
- **Monuments and relics** — بڑی توپ احمد شاہی / بہنگیاں والی توپ (pp. 403–404, folios 410–411); لال لکڑ, the Sikh-era mutilation post (p. 369, folio 376); چوک شہزادہ دارا شکوہ, demolished by the English[OCR?] and its foundations dug out for brick (p. 91, folio 94; p. 358, folio 365).

### Contents-list pointers not matched to any entry above

The pp. 1–118 partial reproduced the book's own contents list with its printed page numbers (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3). Most items are folded into the entries above. These six are **not yet matched to any described site** and each needs a human check at the printed page:

- **مزار سید مٹھہ[OCR?] ۱۷۱** — Mohalla Said Mitha is named repeatedly in the partials, but no shrine entry for it was recorded.
- **مزار گنج شہیداں ۱۷۹**
- **مقبرہ میاں منیر[OCR?] ۲۴۵** — Mian Mir appears in the partials only as a landmark and as the pir of Miskin Shah Amri.
- **مزار شاہ [illegible] الدین چشتی ۲۵۶[OCR?]**
- **مقبرہ شاہ شمس الدین ۲۵۶[OCR?]** — possibly the شاہ شمس الدین قادری لاہوری entry above (folios 258–259), but the printed number does not match; **not asserted**.
- **مزار سید کمال[OCR?] ۳۱۶**

### Named but not a candidate row

Recorded so a future session does not re-open them: **خواجہ خضر**, invoked only as the eponym of Khizri Gate, "چونکہ خواجہ خضر کو دریاؤں کے ساتھ کمال نسبت ہے", with **no shrine, ziyarat or practice attached** (p. 20, folio 15); **گورو گوبند سنگھ**, named only as giver of *pahul* to the Bhai family's ancestor, no site, no date (p. 65, folio 68); **گرو ہر گوبند**, only the cause of the Baoli's seizure (pp. 111–112, folios 114–115); **جوالاجی**, a pilgrimage destination outside Lahore that kills the notable who goes (p. 64, folio 67); **کربلا معلے[OCR?]**, destination of a corpse and of two ziyarats (pp. 70–71, folios 73–74); **رام سنگہ**, "گرو کوکون کا مقتدا[OCR?]", sent to Rangoon (p. 105, folio 108); **مفتی غلام سرور** of mauza Mazang, author of **خزینۃ الاصفیا** and **حدیقۃ الاولیا** among seventy (or eighteen[OCR?]) works, mutawalli and "شاعر لاہور" (pp. 53, 55, 78; folios 56, 58, 81; pp. 167, 170; folios 170, 173; p. 462, folio 469; p. 463) — see Doubts; **کنہیا لال صاحب**, named in the first chronogram's heading (p. 463; see Doubts). Founders, patrons, officials, rulers and the learned, medical and commercial classes named in the partials without a site of their own (لنگر خان بلوچ ملتانی, زین خان [OCR?], شہزادہ دارا شکوہ, محمد سلطان ٹھیکہ دار, رئیس میلا رام, دیوان رتن چند, لالہ نہال چند, فقیر عزیز الدین, the Kotli Muftian line, the five qazis, the presses and booksellers, the hakim families, the Qizilbash and Multani Nawabs, the Faqir family, and the British and Sikh officials listed at pp. 148, 142–148, 143, 234, 213, 141–142, 170, 182, 183, 231, 147, 235, 223, 230–232, 227, 177, 119–222 passim and pp. 349–463 passim) are **not shrine rows**; the pp. 1–118 partial notes that the Kotli Muftian material bears on shrine-of-bahauddin-zakariya and the Qizilbash material on Muharram practice (pp. 38–75, folios 41–78).

**Count: 211 distinct candidate sites are listed above** — 68 Muslim shrines, khanqahs and saints' tombs; 4 Muslim graveyards and burial grounds; 36 mosques; 61 Hindu temples, shivalas, thakurdwaras and asthans; 22 Sikh, Udasi and ascetic samadhs, dharamsalas and gurdwaras; 4 Brahmo and Christian foundations; 16 tombs of rulers and nobles (weak); plus 6 unmatched contents-list pointers still to be identified. The secular, British-era and infrastructural group is recorded but holds no shrine candidates.



## Citable passages

- **Mujawirs' equal share of offerings, Data Ganj Bakhsh** — "اس مزار کے مجاور بہت سے ہین گنتی ستر آدمیون سے زیادہ ہے آمدنی چڑہاوے کی وہ حصہ مساوی تقسیم کرتے ہین۔" — This mazar's mujawirs are many, more than seventy men by count; they divide the offerings income in equal shares. (p. 291, folio 294)
- **Charaghi and rotis at Shah Dargahi's healing well** — "بعد غسل کے وہ روٹیاں زمیندار چاہ کا مع ایک پیسہ چراغی کے لیے لیتا ہے۔" — After the bath the zamindar of the well takes those rotis, with one paisa as charaghi. (p. 82, folio 85)
- **Origin of the Bibis declared unproved** — "مگر یہ امر کسی طرح سے ثابت نہیں ہوا کہ یہ بی بیاں لاہور میں کب آئیں اور کس ملک سے آئیں۔" — But it has in no way been proved when these Bibis came to Lahore, or from what country. (p. 304, folio 307)
- **Author's caution on Sikh-era rebuilding** — "مگر سکہی عہد میں پرانی عمارت کے مندر بہی از سر نو بنا لئے گئے جنکی عمارت تازہ نظر آتی ہو۔" — But in the Sikh era even temples of old fabric were rebuilt anew, so that their building looks fresh. (p. 93, folio 96)
- **Four annas per nikah for the Badshahi Masjid** — "شہر مین جو نکاح ہو چار آنہ نکاح کرنے والے سے آبادی مسجد کی امداد خرچ کیواسطے لئے جائین۔" — On every nikah in the city, four annas be taken from the one performing it for the aid of the mosque's upkeep. (p. 141, folio 144)
- **Khanqah stipend lapsed since Ranjit Singh, Mauj Darya Bukhari** — "مہاراجہ رنجیت سنگہ کے وقت چالیس روپے ماہوار اس خانقاہ کے اخراجات کے واسطے ملتا تہا اب کچہ بہی نہین ملتا۔" — In Maharaja Ranjit Singh's time forty rupees a month were received for this khanqah's expenses; now nothing at all is received. (p. 280, folio 283)
- **Akalis' demand for the mosque by the Baoli** — "یہ مسجد بہی باولی کے ساتہہ شامل ہمارے قبضہ مین رہنی چاہیئے یا گر ادہی جائے۔" — This mosque too should remain in our possession along with the Baoli, or be demolished. (p. 147, folio 150)
- **Mandir funded only by offerings** — "اس مندر کے ضروری اخراجات کا صرف … چڑہاوے کی آمدنی پر ہی جاگیر روزینہ کوئی مقرر نہین ہو۔" — The necessary expenses of this mandir fall only on the income of offerings; no jagir or daily allowance is fixed. (p. 120, folio 123)
- **Two-lakh jagir made waqf for a khanqah's dervishes** — "بادشاہ نے بکمال ارادت و دو لاکھہ روپیے کی جاگیر درویشان خانقاہ کے خرچ کے لئے اُسکو وقف ہو کے تھے۔" — The emperor, in complete devotion, made over to him as waqf a jagir of two lakh rupees for the expenses of the khanqah's dervishes. (p. 79, folio 82)
- **Mujawir sells shrine land to the English** — "اب وہ زمین مجاور نے انگریزون کے پاس فروخت کر دی ہے اور انہون نے شامل کوٹہی کے کر لی ہے۔" — The mujawir has now sold that land to the English, and they have taken it into the kothi. (p. 298, folio 301)
- **Order to strip stone from Muslim tombs** — "حکم ہوا کہ ان کا پتہر اُتروا کر اس بارہ دری کی عمارت میں لگایا جائے۔" — Of the stone rauzas and tombs of Muslim nobles outside the city: the order was given that their stone be taken down and used in this baradari's building. (p. 340, folio 347)
- **Sitla Mata asthan shared by both communities** — "یہ استہان ہندو مسلمان دو نو قومہون[OCR?] کا مرجع ہے۔" — This asthan is the resort of both the Hindu and the Muslim communities[OCR?]. (p. 187, folio 190)
- **Chiraghan mela extended to two days** — "پہلے ایک روز میلا ہوتا تہا اب سرکار نے دو روز میلا کر دیا ہے۔" — Of the chiraghan mela in the garden: formerly the mela was one day; now the Sarkar has made it a two-day mela. (p. 351, folio 358)
- **Government auction of land as nazul** — "اب سرکار نے بصیغہ نزول یہ زمین نیلام کر دی" — Now the government auctioned this land as nazul. (p. 436, folio 443)
- **Mandir completed after the book was printed** — "بوقت تحریر حالات سابق کے یہ مندر موجود نہ تہا اب بعد تحریر و طبع کتاب اسکی عمارت اسی سال میں ختم ہوئی۔" — When the earlier accounts were written this mandir did not exist; now, after the writing and printing of the book, its building was finished this same year. (p. 459, folio 466)


## Doubts and review points

Everything below is carried from the four consolidated partials for pp. 1–463. No doubt has been
resolved, no flagged word has been guessed at, and no folio has been computed. Page and folio
references are quoted exactly as the partials give them. `[OCR?]` and `[illegible]` are the
transcribers' own flags and are reproduced wherever they fall.

### The folio series — nothing in this book may be cited by a computed folio

The printed folio series does four different things across the book and breaks twice. State it whole
before citing anything.

- **It restarts inside the front matter.** The contents quire runs p. 3 → folio 2, p. 4 → folio 3,
  p. 5 → folio 4; the body then starts again at p. 7 → folio 2 … p. 14 → folio 9. **Folios 2, 3 and 4
  each occur twice**, and pp. 1, 2 and 6 print no marker at all. "(p. 3, folio 2)" and "(p. 7, folio 2)"
  are different pages. Folio 1 of the body is p. 6, which prints no marker (pp. 1–14).
- **pp. 15–25 print folios 10–20** (the partial records this as folio = page − 5).
- **p. 26 prints folio 29**, and pp. 26–30 print 29–33 (folio = page + 3). **The prose reads on
  continuously across the p. 25 → p. 26 join** — p. 25 ends "نظام الملک ابو سعید وزیر نے" and p. 26
  begins "ملتان تہا [OCR?] اس کا لحاظ [OCR?] کیا تو وہ سکھر میں جا کر قلعہ میں بیٹھ گیا" — so the
  eight-number jump is **not** explained by missing text there. A human must check the printed folio on
  PDF pp. 25–26 against the original before any folio is used for this book.
- **Chunks 003 and 005–008 (within pp. 31–118) run folio = PDF index + 3 on every page.** The partial
  records chunk 004's relation as "folio = PDF index − 3 on every page" while quoting its markers as
  p. 46 → folio 49 … p. 60 → folio 63; **the stated relation and the quoted markers do not agree in the
  note itself**, so for pp. 46–60 the printed marker on each page must be read off the lithograph and
  neither statement relied on.
- **pp. 119–235 each print a marker exactly three above the PDF index**, and every folio in that partial
  is one a chunk note reports as printed.
- **pp. 236–313 run +3** (p. 236 → folio 239; p. 250 → folio 253; p. 251 → folio 254; p. 264 → folio
  267; p. 265 → folio 268; p. 278 → folio 281; p. 279 → folio 282; p. 292 → folio 295; p. 293 → folio
  296; p. 306 → folio 309; p. 307 → folio 310; p. 313 → folio 316).
- **p. 314 prints [folio 321], not 317**, and **pp. 314–348 run +7** (p. 314 → folio 321; p. 320 → folio
  327; p. 321 → folio 328; p. 334 → folio 341; p. 335 → folio 342; p. 348 → folio 355). **Printed folios
  317–320 are absent from the scan.**
- **pp. 349–456 run +7 throughout** (p. 349 → folio 356; p. 363 → folio 370; p. 364 → folio 371; p. 379
  → folio 386; p. 380 → folio 387; p. 395 → folio 402; p. 396 → folio 403; p. 410 → folio 417; p. 411 →
  folio 418; p. 425 → folio 432; p. 426 → folio 433; p. 441 → folio 448; p. 442 → folio 449; p. 456 →
  folio 463). Chunk 016 of the same book prints **+3** (p. 222 → folio 225), so the series shifted
  somewhere between.
- **The series breaks again in the last chunk.** Printed markers: folio 464 (p. 457), 465 (p. 458), 466
  (p. 459), 467 (p. 460), **no folio marker at all at p. 461**, 469 (p. 462), and `[colophon]` at p. 463.
  **Folio 468 is never printed** and has not been inferred; facts taken from p. 461 carry the page
  reference only.

What a human must settle, and it governs every citation in this file:

- **Whether the p. 26 jump (folio 20 → 29, no break in the prose) is a printing error, a scan disorder
  or a folio restart.** Check printed pp. 25–26.
- **Whether the p. 314 jump (+3 → +7) is a page gap in the scan, a printing shift or a folio restart**,
  and where printed folios 317–320 went. All that can be said from the notes is what the two runs print.
- **The p. 314 break falls exactly at the شاہ جمال entry.** The **shah-jamal** entry is one damaged line
  at p. 313 (folio 316) and then stops; the next page (folio 321) opens mid-entry on **جنگلی چراغ شاہ**,
  whose heading is not printed and is recoverable only because the passage explains its own name.
  Whatever lies between is in no partial. **Nothing about Shah Jamal's shrine may be written from that
  range, and "Jangli Chiragh Shah" must not be treated as a book heading.**
- **A separate seam at pp. 29–30 (folios 32–33) may be a scan fault.** p. 29 breaks off inside the eighth
  "صدمہ" — the king has just sent his order to Sultan Shah Lodi against Jasrat[OCR?] — "مگر" — and p. 30
  **opens mid-sentence in a completely different period**, then heads "تیرھواں صدمہ". **The ninth, tenth,
  eleventh and twelfth blows are entirely absent**, and the consecutive folios 32–33 do not account for
  it. Establish whether pages are missing, duplicated or out of order in the PDF before the catalogue of
  "blows" is used as a series.
- **Folio arithmetic must not be extended.** No folio anywhere in this file has been computed, inferred
  or corrected from an offset; every one is copied from a marker a chunk note reports as printed. A folio
  must never be read off one range for a page in another.

### Identification doubts — every name a partial declined to map, and every conflict with an archive row

These are the doubts that would put a wrong fact under a real shrine. The reasoning is kept; nothing is
resolved. Where the same refusal recurs across ranges it is one entry carrying every page set.

#### Declined and refused mappings, with the reason each partial gave

1. **peer-makki / پیر ذکی شہید — left unmapped, and flagged as a likely future mis-match.** The heading
   reads "تیرہواں ذکی دروازہ المعروف مکی دروازہ" (p. 20, folio 15); the archive's peer-makki is Hazrat
   Syed Azizuddin, Bhati Gate area, d. 1215. The book's figure is a **gate guard** of Mughal times,
   martyred at the gate, with a severed head and a second grave in a stable; **no Syed descent, no date,
   no silsila**, and he is **not** at Bhati Gate — Bhati is this book's seventh gate, described separately
   with no shrine (p. 18, folio 13). The "مکی" alias is presented as a corruption of "ذکی", not a
   different person. In the second range both the man's name and the gate's are flagged on the same word,
   "پیر ذکی[OCR?]" and "ذکی[OCR?] دروازہ" — **if the reading is wrong, the figure and the city gate are
   wrong together** (p. 164, folio 167). The contents also carry "مزار پیر مکی" unflagged with its
   numeral ۳۲۵[OCR?] flagged (p. 5, folio 4). Gate-alias plus archive-name similarity makes this the most
   dangerous name in the book.
2. **shrine-of-baba-shah-chiragh — cannot be resolved, and three or four figures are in play.** The row
   reads "Shrine of Baba Shah Chiragh — The Mall (near Lahore High Court) — Syed Abdul Razzaq (Shah
   Chiragh)". The book has (a) **سید چراغ شاہ گیلانی**, whose mohalla stood exactly where "محکمہ چیف کورٹ
   کی عمارت" was being built (p. 79, folio 82); (b) **سید عبد الرزاق**, a khalifa of Mauj Darya Bukhari in
   محلہ دولا واڑی whose rauza is called "ٹبیلہ [OCR?] گنبد" (p. 80, folio 83). **The book never equates
   them, never calls Abd al-Razzaq "Chiragh", and never calls Chiragh Shah "Abd al-Razzaq"**; separate
   mohallas, tombs and mosques. **The two sets must not be merged.** The contents list a third entry,
   "مقبرہ عبدالرزاق مکی[OCR?]" at printed page ۲۸۹, distinct from both "مقبرہ سیدہ شاہ چراغ[OCR?]" (۲۸۴)
   and "مزار پیر مکی" (۳۲۵[OCR?]) — a name combining this row's figure with peer-makki's nisba, at a
   third page; **none may be merged** (pp. 4–5, folios 3–4). Note also that "سیدہ[OCR?] شاہ چراغ" is a
   **feminine form before a masculine name — do not carry it** (p. 4, folio 3). In the third range the row
   is matched to "سید عبد الرزاق بن سید عبد الوہاب گیلانی" titled **Shah Chiragh** (p. 281, folio 284),
   whose **death year is [illegible]**: "وفعات اسکی ۲۲ ماہ ذی قعدہ [illegible] ہجری مین واقع ہوئی" — day
   and month survive, the year does not. A **separate** "شیخ عبد الرزاق مکی" of the **Nila Gumbad**, who
   came from Mecca[OCR?] in Humayun's era and died 1085[OCR?] AH (p. 286, folio 289), is **not** matched
   to it. And **which Shah Chiragh is Shah Dargahi's pir is not chosen**: "سید شاہ چراغ … جسکا ذکر پہلے
   درج ہو چکا ہے" (p. 299, folio 302) could be the p. 281 figure, or the **living** "سید چراغ شاہ حکیم" of
   the tenth enclosure three pages earlier (p. 296, folio 299), who also has murids in the Chishtiya and
   Qadiriya; the archive also holds **darbar-sakhi-shah-chan-charagh** (Rawalpindi), a different place.
   A human must decide which figure the row is before anything from pp. 79–80, 281, 286, 296 or 299 is
   cited under it.
3. **shrine-of-miran-hussain-zanjani-zanjani-sahib — rests on a flagged nisba, and the book's year
   contradicts the row's field.** The contents print "شاہ حسین زنجانی[OCR?]" where the archive reads
   "Miran Hussain Zanjani"; no location, no date (p. 4, folio 3) — confirm from printed page ۲۷۸ before
   merging. In the body the book prints "سال وفات اسکا سنہ ۶۰۴ [OCR?] ہے" with **no era** (p. 275, folio
   278), while the archive row's `figure_died` reads **1042**. **Both stand**; a human must decide which
   era each figure belongs to. Separately, the relation between **Shah Yaqub Zanjani** and **Sayyid Husain
   Zanjani** is lost in a damaged clause, "یہ سید حسین زنجانی کے لاہور میں آ کر سکونت پذیر ہوا" (p. 301,
   folio 304): read literally it says only that he came to Lahore and settled; whether kinship,
   discipleship or chronology is meant is not recoverable, **so no relation is asserted**.
4. **shrine-of-mian-mir — a toponym in three ranges, a village in another man's enclosure in a fourth,
   and never once a shrine the book describes.** Five occurrences at pp. 34–35 (folios 37–38), always
   "چھاؤنی میانمیر [OCR?]", "میانمیر [OCR?] کے میدان" or "صدر بازار میانمیر [OCR?]", always flagged; a
   police posting at p. 62 (folio 65); a church in the cantonment in the contents (p. 5, folio 4); the
   cantonment as the place the cantonment was moved to, "چہاونی میانمیر" (p. 357, folio 364); and a
   church entry placed in "چھاونی میانمیر" (pp. 389–394, folios 396–401) where the partial declines the
   match because that chunk "says nothing whatever about a shrine of that name and nowhere connects the
   cantonment's name to a saint". **No partial names a saint, grave, mazar, urs or custodian there, and
   none says the place is named after anyone.** The nearest tomb candidate in the contents, "مقبرہ میاں
   منیر[OCR?]" at printed page ۲۴۵, reads *Munir* and is flagged (p. 4, folio 3) — check printed page ۲۴۵.
   Where the book does speak of Mian Mir's rauza, the grammar puts **Mauza Mian Mir inside Mulla Shah
   Qadri's enclosure**: "پہر ملا شاہ قادری اپنے مرشد کا روضہ بنوایا جسکے حاطہ مین اب موضع میانمیر ایک
   گاؤن آبا دہی" (p. 139, folio 142) — the village is in Mulla Shah's enclosure though it bears Mian Mir's
   name. The archive row locates the shrine at Alam Ganj (Dharampura); **that range gives no quarter for
   either rauza**, and the relation term must not be flattened: Mian Mir is Dara Shikoh's "دادا مرشد", his
   pir's pir, and Mulla Shah his own. Finally, **"خانقاہ میان وڈا[OCR?]"**, the khanqah stormed with cannon
   (pp. 194–195, folios 197–198), is **left unmapped**: do not attach it to this row on the strength of
   "میاں" alone; no saint, no order, no location beyond the name. A human must decide whether a bare
   toponym is worth linking at all.
5. **loh-temple-lava-temple — rests on the flagged name لو[OCR?] alone, and the fort chapter names no
   temple.** The chunk names the founder only as "مسمی لو[OCR?]" and "لو", never as Lava or Luv, and
   **never mentions a temple in the fort**; the link is to the archive row's own alternate name, and the
   same page offers "لوہار پور" as a rival etymology (pp. 13–14, folios 8–9). Treat as a hypothesis.
   The Shiv temples of pp. 105–118 (folios 108–121) are **not** this row. The book describes **قلعہ لاہور**
   at length (pp. 338–339, folios 345–346), the archive's stated location for this row, but **names no
   temple inside the fort** — only the Moti Masjid, renamed Moti Mandir and used as a treasury, which is
   not the same thing.
6. **shrine-of-fariduddin-ganjshakar — two Lahore buildings, neither the Pakpattan shrine.** "چلہ بابا
   فرید گنج شکر[OCR?]" at printed page ۳۱۹ is a *chilla* in Lahore; name and numeral both flagged; nothing
   connects the two sites (p. 5, folio 4). "تکیہ[OCR?] بابا فرید گنج شکر" is used **only as a landmark**
   for locating the two ranis' samadhs (p. 443, folio 450) — "the book here describes a Lahore takia, not
   that site, and says nothing else about it".
7. **The two Guru Arjan rows — gurdwara-dera-sahib and gurdwara-baoli-sahib-guru-arjan-dev-ji-lahore —
   are entangled across four ranges and one of the partials disagrees with another (see "Where the
   partials disagree").** In the contents, "سمادہ گورو ارجن [illegible]" at printed page ۲۰۸ could be
   either row and the word after the name is illegible (p. 3, folio 2); "مکان باولی صاحب[OCR?]" at printed
   page ۱۱۱ matches the Baoli row by title but sits inside the book's **first, Hindu, qism** begun at
   printed page ۹۶, and the name is flagged — the three-qism scheme places Sikh samadhs and dharamsalas in
   that same qism (p. 11, folio 6), so the section is neither evidence for nor against (p. 2). In the
   body, **everything about Guru Arjan in pp. 110–114 (folios 113–117) belongs to the baoli and its
   langar**; the book names no Dera Sahib and gives no death date for the Guru. **Do not carry any of it to
   the Dera Sahib row.** Guru Har Gobind appears only as the cause of the Baoli's seizure and attaches to
   **none** of the archive's four Hargobind rows (pp. 111–112, folios 114–115). The chunk that maps
   **gurdwara-dera-sahib** does so on locational bearings only — **outside** the city, opposite the
   Roshnai Gate, fort wall east and Ranjit Singh's samadh west (pp. 205–206, folios 208–209), which does
   not fit Dabbi Bazaar — **but the same site has a baoli** (p. 206, folio 209), exactly the feature that
   could pull it toward the Baoli row; **the book itself never uses either name**, and the mapping needs a
   human sign-off. And "سمادہ گرو ارجن" at p. 226 (folio 229) **cannot be mapped at all**: the Mozang
   building's income goes to the pujari of "گرو ارجن کی سمادہ" with **no location given**; record it as
   "the samadh of Guru Arjan" and **do not pick a row.**
8. **The two Lahore martyr rows — gurdwara-shaheed-ganj-singh-singhnian and
   gurdwara-shaheed-bhai-taru-singh — cannot be told apart.** The contents carry "مکان شہید گنج" (۲۱۹) and
   "مزار گنج شہیداں" (۱۷۹) as **two separate entries**, with no figure, date or quarter given for either,
   and ۱۷۹ in the inside-the-city Islamic qism against ۲۱۹ in the first qism (pp. 3–4, folios 2–3); both
   left unmapped. In the body the book gives the place only one name, Shahid Ganj, and its central domed
   mandir is said to be "سمادہ بابا تارو سنگہ کی" (p. 216, folio 219), which is why that material sits
   under the Taru Singh row; **but the book names no women or children, and its one location statement —
   outside Delhi Gate on the eastern edge of Bazar Landa — is not enough to tell the two rows apart.** A
   human must decide, and that range must not be cited for either row without the decision.
9. **shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal — a name-trap in the contents, a second Shah Kamal with a nisba, and a
   silsila that contradicts the row.** "مزار سید کمال[OCR?]" (۳۱۶) is *Sayyid* Kamal, not the archive's
   *Baba Shah* Kamal, with no quarter, order or date (p. 4, folio 3). "شاہ کمال کیتہلی [OCR?]" is **left
   unmatched**: he appears only as Shaikh Tahir's Qadiri pir (p. 292, folio 295), carries a nisba the row
   does not, and nothing of him is placed at Lahore. Where the row **is** matched, on the Ichhra location
   alone, **the book puts him in the سہروردی خاندان (p. 313, folio 316) while the row's silsila field
   reads Qadiri** — and that sits in tension with the Qadiri Shah Kamal of p. 292. Both stand.
10. **shah-noorani-shrine-syed-bilawal-shah-noorani — five Lahore "Shah Bilawal" occurrences, none of
    them this row (Lahoot Lamakan, Khuzdar, Balochistan).** "مزار سید شاہ بلاول[OCR?]" (۲۳۸) is a Lahore
    entry (p. 4, folio 3). "شاہ بلاول لاہوری" (p. 184, folio 187) is named only as a friend of Chhajju
    Bhagat, styled *Lahori*, with no grave, date or order. **سید شاہ بلاول قادری** of p. 235 (folio 238)
    is born at Sheikhupura, taught in Lahore by Shaikh Shamsuddin Qadri, buried on the Ravi bank and
    reburied under Ranjit Singh — **a candidate for a new row, not a match.** The khanqah "شاہ بلاول" one
    *kos* east of **Lahore** by Raja Dina Nath's garden (p. 236, folio 239) is a different province from
    the row; **leave unmapped, and note the shared name as a likely future mis-match.** A further "شاہ
    بلاول قادری" appears only as Shah Shams ud-Din's murid with no place, date or shrine (p. 256, folio
    259). The Sher Singh site is located "west of the **Shah Bilawal** barahdari" in a damaged phrase
    (pp. 210–211, folios 213–214).
11. **shrine-of-shah-shams-ud-din-sabzwari (Multan) — three refusals on a shared given name.** "مقبرہ شاہ
    شمس الدین" (۲۵۶[OCR?]) in the contents is not it (p. 4, folio 3). "شاہ شمس الدین قادری لاہوری" is
    **Qadiri**, at **Lahore**, near the Lieutenant Governor's kothi, murid of Shah Abu Ishaq Qadri, with a
    chronogram reading "۱۰۲۴ [OCR?]" (pp. 255–256, folios 258–259), against a row that is **Sabzwari**, at
    **Multan**, with no silsila given — shared given name only, **leave unmapped**. And **Shams-ud-Din
    Tabrizi at Multan is not asserted as this row**: Pir Hadi Shah, Muhsin Shah and Abdul Samad Shah[OCR?]
    are "شمسی سید شمس الدین تبریزی کی اولاد مین سے … جسکا روضہ ملتان مین زیارت گاہ ہے" (p. 296, folio 299),
    but the book prints **تبریزی**, not **سبزواری**; identifying them would be an addition from outside
    the source. **Left for a human.**
12. **shrine-of-abdullah-shah-ghazi (Clifton, Karachi) — refused in two ranges.** "عبد اللہ شاہ" the
    Baloch of Mazang is **not** that row — shared name only, no date and no grave stated (p. 77, folio 80).
    "شیخ عبد اللہ شاہ بلوچ", a Qadiri of **Mozang, Lahore**, founder of Kot Abdullah Shah, died 1218[OCR?]
    AH (pp. 282–283, folios 285–286) — the row's location and figure differ, **no match made**. Neither
    partial says whether the two ranges describe the same man; do not join them.
13. **ranmal-sharif — refused twice, and the second mapping rests on one name in a shajra.** "خاندان
    نوشاہیہ قادریہ" at p. 57 (folio 60) is a Lahore physician's **silsila affiliation**, with no shrine,
    location, date or principal figure, and must **not** be mapped to this row; the unrelated family name
    "حکیم نتہ[OCR?] نوشاہ" on the same page is a further reason for care with "Naushah" in this book.
    Where the row **is** matched, the match is made only from "نو شاہ گنج بخش" at the head of the Naushahi
    shajra and the book's own "حال نو شاہی سلسلے میں" (p. 304, folio 307); **the book says nothing about
    the site at Ranmal Sharif.** A human should confirm or drop it.
14. **shahwala-teja-singh-mandir — refused.** "ٹہاکر دوارہ راجہ تیجا سنگہ" (Moti Bazar, Lahore, Krishna
    and Radha[OCR?], built under English rule) is **not** that row (Dharowal, Sialkot, Shiva): shared
    personal name, different city, deity and building type (pp. 103–104, folios 106–107). A separate
    hazard sits beside it: the samadh **heading** names "سماوہ راجہ سچا سنگہ[OCR?]" against "راجہ تیجا سنگہ"
    twice in the body (p. 189, folio 192; p. 190, folio 193) — the heading's name is flagged and the
    body's is not; **read the printed heading before either name is used.**
15. **darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz — refused in three ranges, and the archive's "احمد" is absent from the
    book.** Mahmud Ghaznavi is named **without any companion, officer or governor** at p. 43 (folio 46), so
    nothing there supports a row whose figure is Mahmud's slave, minister and governor of Lahore. "ملک
    ایاز[OCR?]" is flagged at its first occurrence (p. 17, folio 12) and the archive's "احمد" is **absent
    from this book at all four occurrences** (pp. 17, 18, 21, 23; folios 12, 13, 16, 18); the contents put
    "مزار ملک ایاز" at ۱۶۰[OCR?] out of sequence (pp. 2–5). "بیرون دروازہ شاہ عالمی" is a locality only,
    not this row in Shah Alam Market (p. 65, folio 68). And **"خواجہ ایاز / خواجہ محمد ایاز" must not be
    read into it**: the book's Ayaz is a Shah Jahan-era noble in the **buildings department under Ali
    Mardan Khan** (p. 278, folio 281) and the **mir-e-imarat of Shalamar Bagh** (p. 279, folio 282). **No
    match made.**
16. **The Sufi-order list is doctrinal and attached to nothing.** "قادریہ ۔ چشتیہ ۔ سہروردیہ ۔ نقشبندیہ ۔
    مجدویہ [OCR?] ۔ شطاریہ [OCR?] ۔ نوشاہیہ وغیرہ" (p. 44, folio 47), two names flagged, recorded exactly
    as printed and **not** corrected or expanded. Anyone tempted to align these with archive `silsila`
    values such as "Naushahia Qadiri" (ranmal-sharif) or "Qadri Shattari" (shrine-of-shah-inayat-qadiri)
    must note that the list is connected to **no shrine, no saint and no place**.
17. **"کنہیا لال" at the Guru Ram Das gurdwara shares a name with the book's own author** (p. 102, folio
    105). The chunk says only that a person of that name is the gurdwara's "معتمد و معاون حلقہ", and the
    author's evaluative clause is lost to "[illegible]". **The chunk does not identify them and nothing
    should be inferred either way**; a human must decide from the front matter and surrounding chapters.
    See also the colophon chronogram naming "کنہیا لال صاحب" under "Provenance".
18. **The Miani Sahib rows — tahir-bandagi-qadri, ghazi-ilm-din-shaheed,
    khawaja-feroz-ud-din-gharib-nawaz, wasif-ali-wasif — get site history and nothing else.** "میانی"
    appears in the contents only as a mohalla at printed page ۸۸ with no shrine named in it, and the four
    rows may not be attached to it from that entry (p. 2). The graveyard passage (p. 86, folio 89) is
    usable as **site history**; **nothing in that range is evidence about ghazi-ilm-din-shaheed,
    khawaja-feroz-ud-din-gharib-nawaz or wasif-ali-wasif**, and the only burial it names there is Shaikh
    Muhammad Tahir's mazar. In the third range the Miani graveyard names only **شیخ ظاہر / طاہر** within
    it, plus Rani Gul Begum's enclosure; **nothing in it is attributed to any other Miani row.**
19. **tahir-bandagi-qadri — the identification rests on one passage and the name is printed three ways in
    one entry.** "شیخ طاہر بندگی[OCR?]" is flagged in the contents (p. 4, folio 3). The match rests on the
    p. 292 (folio 295) passage — the Miani location, the Qadiri family and the 1040[OCR?] AH year — and on
    that alone; a human should confirm the reading **ظاہر / طاہر / بندگی**, which the book prints three
    ways in one entry ("مزار شیخ ظاہر ہندی" in the heading, itself flagged as "و گورستان پنج پیر و میر
    [OCR?] اسبیا [OCR?]"; then "شیخ ظاہر بندگی [OCR?]"; then "شیخ طاہر"). **"چار دیواری شیخ طاہر" at
    p. 294 (folio 297) is named only as a boundary**, with no dates, silsila or description. His death year
    "سنہ ۱۰۴۰ [OCR?] ہجری" is given with **the chronogram stated only as coming out of the word "غم", no
    chronogram phrase printed**, so **the year cannot be checked from the text**, and no month or day is
    stated; the archive's "8 Muharram" is not in the book (p. 85, folio 88).
20. **gurdwara-chhevin-patshahi-mozang — rests on one clause and a flagged heading word.** Four archive
    rows carry Guru Hargobind; the match rests solely on "موضع مزنگ کے شرق کی طرف … جو جیلخانہ[OCR?] کو
    جاتی ہے" (p. 225, folio 228), and the heading word "جہیٹی[OCR?]" (for چھٹی/چھیویں, sixth) is itself
    flagged. Confirm the heading before relying on the id.
21. **shrine-of-shah-inayat-qadiri — competes with a second archive "Shah Inayat", and every word of the
    evidence is flagged.** The chunk's "شاہ عنایت اللہ" is given **no place and no date** (p. 144, folio
    147); the only thing tying him to a row is his pir's printed order, "خاندان[OCR?] شطاریہ[OCR?]
    قادریہ[OCR?]", which matches the Lahore row's silsila and not the Sindh row's blank. **All three words
    of the silsila are OCR-flagged.** And **the date 1126[OCR?] belongs to Shah Zia Qadri, not to him**
    (p. 144, folio 147). Separately, **عبد اللہ بلوچ قادری**, "whose rauza is famous in mauza Mozang"
    (p. 163, folio 166), is **not matched** to this row although it is also at Mozang and also Qadiri: the
    name is different and the chunk gives neither date nor description — recorded because the shared
    locality and order make it a likely future mis-match. موضع مزنگ [OCR?] as a bare locality is likewise
    not evidence for this row or for gurdwara-chhevin-patshahi-mozang (pp. 32, 34–36; folios 35, 37–39).
22. **shah-jamal — name, order and place agree; there is no date, and the range breaks at his entry.**
    "شاہ جمال سہروردیہ" with tomb and madrasa near "موضع اجہرہ[OCR?]" (p. 169, folio 172) agrees with the
    row's name, silsila and locality, and the book is a history of Lahore; but the row's figure is dated
    and **the chunk gives no date**, and the spelling "اجہرہ" is flagged. No other archive row carries a
    Shah Jamal. The other mention is one damaged line at p. 313 (folio 316) at the point where the folio
    series breaks — **nothing about this shrine may be written from that range.**
23. **madho-lal-hussain — an incidental mention resting on a name alone, and the book's pir contradicts
    the row's silsila.** At p. 169 (folio 172) the chunk gives only "حضرت لال حسین" — no place, no date, no
    shrine, and the statement is hearsay ("کہنے میں کہ"); confirm before it is cited as a fact about that
    shrine. The book says "شیخ بہلول قادری کا مرید ہوا" (p. 233, folio 236) while the archive row records
    the silsila as *Malamati*: **record the book's statement as a named pir, not as an order, and flag the
    divergence.** In the contents both the name and its page numeral are flagged, "مادھو لال حسین[OCR?]"
    and ۲۳۶[OCR?] (p. 4, folio 3).
24. **mazar-of-bulleh-shah — the sentence that would link him to Kasur and to Shah Zia Qadri is broken.**
    As printed: "چنانچہ یہ شاہ عنایت اللہ اسی کا مرید تہا بلکہ [illegible] قصور کی ایک [illegible] مشہور
    دلی ہوا ہے پنجابی زبان مین بلہے[OCR?] شاہ کی کافیان … گاتے ہین [illegible] ایک زنانہ [illegible] بزرگی
    کا قایل ہے" (p. 144, folio 147). **Two or three gaps fall exactly where the relationship between the
    three names would be stated. Do not reconstruct it.** The name بلہے شاہ is itself flagged. "تحصیل
    قصور" elsewhere is a locality only and is not this row (p. 61, folio 64).
25. **samadhi-of-maharaja-ranjit-singh — six separate refusals to attach material to it.** Hazuri Bagh is
    named with no samadhi mentioned (p. 138, folio 141); the Maharaja of the p. 148 (folio 151)
    mosque-shops settlement is **unnamed** and must not be merged with the named Ranjit Singh of p. 161
    (folio 164), nor attached to this row; Ranjit Singh appears on six pages of pp. 177–181 (folios
    180–184) only as patron or ruler with no samadhi described; the book describes **حضوری باغ** and its
    baradari in detail (pp. 339–342, folios 346–349), the archive's stated location for this row, but
    **never mentions the samadh**, saying only that Ranjit Singh laid out the garden and sat at darbar on
    the baradari's shah-nashin, and the same silence attends the mentions of the intended marble baradari
    in "حضور سی[OCR?] باغ" (p. 311, folio 314; p. 322, folio 329) — **the silence on the samadh is itself
    informative for dating**, and a human may wish to decide whether the Hazuri Bagh material belongs to
    that row's build history; and the samadhs of **Rani Nakain and Rani Chand Kaur** are separate
    monuments and are **not** this row, which that chunk does not describe (p. 443, folio 450).
26. **"میران بادشاہ" / سید اسحاق گاذرونی[OCR?] must not be mapped.** The archive carries two Lahore rows
    sharing the element *Miran*; the chunk's name, nisba and location match neither, and it gives no death
    date to test against either. **Treat as a candidate for a new row and note that the shared element plus
    the shared city makes this a likely future mis-match** (pp. 156, 161; folios 159, 164).
27. **krishna-mandir-ravi-road and krishna-mandir-kabari-bazar — eight buildings refused.** The three
    Krishna temples inside the walled city are **not** krishna-mandir-ravi-road (pp. 105–118, folios
    108–121). The two Krishna thakurdwaras on the Ravi bank — Rani Lachhmi's and Bhauri Sarkar's, both
    "نالہ دریاے راوی کے کنارے" and both with Sri Krishan and Radha ji installed (pp. 199–201, folios
    202–204) — are **left unmapped**: "either, neither or both could be it", and the chunk gives no street
    and no modern name. Likewise the **Thakur Dwara of Bawa Mangal Das** (p. 189, folio 192), outside the
    Delhi[OCR?] Gate and named after its founder; and the Krishna and Radha murtis of **Thakurdwara Jwala
    Dai** (p. 127, folio 130) and of the **Mehr Das** enclosure (pp. 129–130, folios 132–133), both in
    Mohalla Wachhowali. And the Thakurdwara of **Lala Bhagwan Das**, whose principal image is "سری کرشن جی
    مہاراج" (p. 461), sits at the Shah Alami tank, not on Ravi Road, **so no id was assigned** — "a human
    should decide whether the two are the same foundation."
28. **Baba Sri Chand's Tali Sahib has no archive row and must not be attached to the existing Nanakpanthi
    / Udasi rows**, which the index places in Sindh or Balochistan — even though the chunk makes this
    Lahore site "اس سب قوم کا مرجع", the resort of that whole community (p. 228, folio 231). A strong
    candidate for a future row and a likely site for a future mis-match.
29. **The Kali rows and the Hanuman row — four refusals.** **مندر کالی دیوی** in Kali Mata ki Gali
    (p. 132, folio 135) and **مکان بہدر کالی** in Mohalla Pahla[OCR?] Lakhpat Rai (pp. 125–126, folios
    128–129) must **not** be attached to `kali-bari-mandir` (Peshawar), `kalat-kali-temple` (Kalat) or
    `kalka-cave-temple-asthan-of-kalka-devi` (Aror, Sukkur). The Hanuman murti on the Radha Kishan
    shivala's dome (p. 132, folio 135) is not `shri-panchmukhi-hanuman-mandir-karachi`.
30. **langer-makhdoom — a place-name match only, and the figures do not agree.** The book names "موضع لنگر
    مخدوم جو دریائے چناب پر واقع ہے" and its resident pir as **مخدوم عبدالکریم**, Makhdum Abdul Karim
    (p. 238, folio 241); the archive row's principal figure is **Makhdoom Burhan-ud-din (d. 1245)** at
    Langer Makhdoom, Chiniot. The book says nothing of Burhan-ud-din, gives no dates for the place, and
    **does not call it a shrine**. The safe reading recorded in the notes is that this is the same *place
    name on the Chenab* with a different saint attached; a human must decide whether the sites are the same.
31. **The Bukhari rows — three refusals and one single-clause mapping.** **Ghore Shah Bukhari must not be
    attached to shrine-of-mauj-darya-bukhari**: he is a Bukhari Sayyid of **Uch** descent with a Lahore
    mazar, which is also how the Mauj Darya row reads, but the names, the ages and the place differ and the
    book never mentions Mauj Darya in his entry — **he has no archive row; treat him as a candidate for a
    future one** (pp. 236–237, folios 239–240). **Sayyid Jalal-ud-Din Bukhari at Bibi Pak Daman is not the
    archive's Uch figure**: he is identified only as "میران تہہ[OCR?] شاہ موج دریا بخاری کا بہائی", buried
    in a tomb of **1017 AH** at Bibi Pak Daman (p. 306, folio 309); the name coincides with
    **shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari-jalaluddin-bukhari** and **shrine-of-makhdoom-jahaniyan-jahangasht** (both Uch
    Sharif) and on the book's own description he is neither — **do not merge them.** The
    **shrine-of-jalaluddin-surkh-posh-bukhari-jalaluddin-bukhari** bullet rests **solely** on the single clause placing Mauj
    Darya's ancestor's tomb at Uch (p. 279, folio 282). Mauj Darya's own locality انار کلی is a bare
    place-name elsewhere (pp. 32, 34–36; folios 35, 37–39).
32. **shrine-of-akhund-panju-baba — refused.** "سید عبد الوہاب قادری" is placed at mauza Garhi Shahu,
    Lahore (p. 265, folio 268); the archive row carries "Hazrat Syed Abdul Wahab" at Akbarpura, Nowshera.
    **No id assigned.**
33. **ganj-e-inayat-sarkar and mohra-sharif-khanqah — refused on a shared Naqshbandi affiliation.** "شیخ
    محمود شاہ نقشبندی" and "شیخ محترم نقشبندی" must not be attached to either row on the strength of
    "خاندان مجددیہ نقشبندیہ" (p. 260, folio 263) or "نقشبندیہ سلسلہ" (p. 256, folio 259); those rows'
    figures are differently placed and dated. Both are candidates for future rows. Note that Shaikh
    Muhtaram's rauza is reported turned into a bungalow with its graves destroyed — **a shrine that may no
    longer exist** (p. 257, folio 260).
34. **shergarh rests on one damaged phrase.** "خواجہ داؤد شیر گڑہ [OCR?] کہ بہی" (p. 283, folio 286); the
    place-name is OCR-flagged and the words between it and "کا مرید" are unrecoverable.
35. **darbar-abul-muali-qadri and abul-faiz-qalander-ali-suharwardi — ابوالخیر refused.** The only saint
    in that chunk is **ابوالخیر** at the Garhi Shahu compound (p. 365, folio 372). The index carries two
    superficially similar Lahore rows, "but the second element of the name differs in both cases, and the
    chunk gives no death date, silsila or street to test against"; **no id assigned**, and a human should
    decide whether ابوالخیر warrants a new row. The book gives **no year for Abul Khair's death**, only the
    age of "ایکسو پانچ برس", 105 years, and the reign of Muhammad Shah with the nizamat of "خان بہادر[OCR?]"
    printed twice over (p. 363, folio 370).
36. **مقبرہ سید بدال دین[OCR?] گیلانی corresponds to no row**, and "its principal figure's name is itself
    OCR-flagged, so even the reading 'Badal-din' is uncertain" (pp. 394–395, folios 401–402). Related and
    unresolved: the **"پرانا مقبرہ" in Government House's lower storey is unnamed** — "whose tomb it is is
    (not stated) in this chunk … Do not attach it to any archive row on the strength of this chunk"
    (p. 396, folio 403). One chunk describes the Gilani tomb at the same site (pp. 394–395, folios
    401–402) and the next does not name it; **the identification is left to a human.**
37. **The mela چراغان is attributed to no shrine, saint or khanqah.** The text says only that it is held
    "اس باغ کے اندر", inside this garden (p. 351, folio 358); which shrine's mela it is, if any, "must be
    settled at consolidation from chunks that name it, not from this one", and **no chunk in any partial
    names it**. It stays unattributed.

#### Nothing may be mapped on a shared place-name, a shared deity or a shared name-element

- **Localities named without their shrines.** انار کلی (where the archive places tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak
  and shrine-of-mauj-darya-bukhari), بھاٹی دروازہ (peer-makki, gurdwara-chowmala-sahib), موضع مزنگ [OCR?]
  (gurdwara-chhevin-patshahi-mozang, shrine-of-shah-inayat-qadiri), دہلی دروازہ, شاہ عالمی دروازہ, لہاری
  دروازہ, قلعہ گوجر سنگھ, گڑھی شاہو [OCR?] — in every case cantonments, sarais, bazaars and quarters only
  (pp. 32, 34–36; folios 35, 37–39). Add تحصیل قصور (not mazar-of-bulleh-shah), تحصیل شرقپور (not
  shrine-of-pir-sher-muhammad) and "بیرون دروازہ شاہ عالمی" (not darbar-malik-ahmad-ayaz) (pp. 61, 65;
  folios 64, 68); and **انارکلی** as the position of the Roman Catholic church (p. 425, folio 432), where
  three archive rows carry Anarkali in their location but "that is a coincidence of street, not a
  description of any of them".
- **Name-traps recorded as likely future mis-matches beyond those above**: the four occurrences of امام
  الدین for four different men, and likewise غلام محمد, احمد شاہ and چراغ الدین (pp. 50, 52, 57, 59;
  folios 53, 55, 60, 62) — **do not merge on name alone anywhere in that chunk**; "پریم نانہہ[OCR?] جوگی"
  (p. 106, folio 109), "بابا پریم ناتہ[OCR?]" (p. 108, folio 111) and "پنڈت پریم ناتہ" at a different
  building (p. 115, folio 118), which **may be two or three men**; two men called Rahim Bakhsh never
  connected (pp. 61, 75; folios 64, 78); **two different Nur Muhammads and three mosques** — "نور محمد
  سادہو", builder of Masjid Takiya Sadhuwan in 1266[OCR?] (p. 170, folio 173), and "نور محمد" titled
  **نورا ایمان والہ** by Ranjit Singh, builder of the kotwali mosque in 1239[OCR?] (pp. 173–174, folios
  176–177) and of a second mosque in Kashmiri Bazaar (p. 175, folio 178), **separate men in separate
  entries; the chunk nowhere links them**; **"نواب خان بہادر" used for two people, or one, with the book
  not saying which** — Nawab Zakariya Khan "المعروف خان بہادر صوبہ لاہور" (p. 262, folio 265), Sharaf
  un-Nisa "ہمشیرہ نواب خان بہادر" (p. 264, folio 267), and Begampura's founder "بانی مکان" Khan Bahadur
  whose mother built it (p. 278, folio 281) — **do not join them**; **"گامی شاہ" occurring twice**,
  founder of the Imambara buried in its sard-khana (p. 302, folio 305) and a link in the Shah Kanhh[OCR?]
  shajra (p. 304, folio 307), the book not saying whether one man is meant; **"شاہ شرف [OCR?]" vs "شیخ
  اشرف" vs "شاہ اشرف"** — the grave by Khwaja Muhammad Saeed (p. 287, folio 290), the elder who died
  1104[OCR?] with an Alamgiri tomb destroyed in the Sikh time (p. 288, folio 291), the "روضہ و مسجد شاہ
  اشرف" near Data Ganj Bakhsh also said to have fallen to the Sikhs (p. 289, folio 292), a **مقبرہ شاہ
  شرف** "جس کا مقبرہ دروازے بہائی کے آگے تہا" among the tombs stripped for the Hazuri Bagh baradari
  (p. 340, folio 347), and a fourth **شاہ شرف** who is Muhammad Fazil's son at the Chand Mari khanqah
  (p. 297, folio 300) — **whether these are one man, and whether one destruction is told twice with two
  different causes (Ranjit Singh's road order and Sikh plundering), is not resolved**; a **سید صوفی**
  named both on the first platform (d. 1050[OCR?] AH) and among the second enclosure's graves at the
  Gilani Sayyids' graveyard (pp. 308–309, folios 311–312); and **"دوارکا داس"** appearing both in the
  damaged list of subsidiary samadhs and as a mahant in the succession at Chhajju Bhagat's (pp. 185–186,
  folios 188–189), "not stated" whether the same man.
- **Ranges that contain no archive-row subject at all, and say so.** pp. 119–132 (folios 122–135) —
  "**Treat the whole chunk as candidate material for future rows, not as evidence about existing ones**";
  pp. 177–191 (folios 180–194) — "**No archive row is the subject of any entry in this chunk, and nothing
  here should be mapped to one**"; pp. 321–348 (folios 328–355) — the Asaf Jah, Nur Jahan, Fazl Shah,
  haveli, fort, Hazuri Bagh and Shalamar material **carries no shrine id**; and **pp. 349–463 in their
  entirety — chunks 025–032 match no archive row at all and every one of them declines explicitly. No id
  is asserted anywhere in that partial.** Two matched rows in pp. 162–176 are **incidental mentions on a
  single page** (p. 169, folio 172).

#### Sites with material but no archive row — row-creation decisions, not guesses

- Lahore shrines with a location, a date or an endowment in the book and no row: **Sayyid Ishaq Gazruni,
  Pir Zaki[OCR?], Pir Bai[OCR?], the Huzuri sayyids, Hassu Teli** (pp. 162–176, folios 165–179).
- Sites with urs, mela or land recorded and no row: مزار حسو تیلی with its khalifas (pp. 268–269, folios
  271–272); مقبرہ سید جان محمد حضوری with the second tomb of Sayyid Mahmud Huzuri (pp. 265–267, folios
  268–270); مزار فقیر سائین قطب شاہ قادری (p. 271, folio 274); مزار فتح شاہ مست (pp. 273–274, folios
  276–277); مزار شاہ ابو تراب / شاہ گدا لاہوری (pp. 274–275, folios 277–278); مزار شاہ شرف and محمد فاضل
  (p. 297, folio 300); مزار شاہ درگاہی (p. 299, folio 302); مقبرہ شاہ سروانی (pp. 302–303, folios
  305–306); مقبرہ شاہ کنہہ[OCR?] (p. 304, folio 307); خانقاہ فضل شاہ مجذوب (pp. 325–326, folios 332–333);
  and جنگلی چراغ شاہ (p. 314, folio 321).
- **Neither Maharaja Kharak Singh nor Kunwar Nau Nihal Singh has an archive row** (pp. 221–223, folios
  224–226).

#### Sites the book reports destroyed, converted or vanished — bearing on whether a present-day site is the book's site

Shaikh Muhtaram's rauza turned into a bungalow with its graves destroyed (p. 257, folio 260); Bahadur
Khan's grave "گرا دی گئی ہے جس کا نشان بہی باقی نہین" (p. 257, folio 260); Nusrat Khan's grave floored
over (p. 262, folio 265); Makhduma Begum's husband's tomb quarried so that "نشان باقی نہ چہوڑا" (p. 260,
folio 263); Shaikh Ashraf's tomb replaced by a sweepers' takiya (p. 288, folio 291); Khwaja Muhammad
Saeed's tomb and mosque demolished entirely (p. 287, folio 290); Nawab Mian Khan's grave "نیست و نابود"
(p. 273, folio 276); Zeb-un-Nisa's grave replaced by "ایک مٹی کا تودہ" (p. 311, folio 314); Asaf Jah's
tomb reduced to "ایک کہنڈر" with only the ta'wiz left (p. 322, folio 329). **Any of these may bear on
whether a present-day site is the site the book describes.**

#### Absence from the contents proves nothing

Three of the archive's own Lahore rows are conspicuously absent from the visible contents —
**tomb-of-qutbuddin-aibak**, **jain-mandir-lahore** and **gurdwara-babay-nanki** — as are **mazar-e-iqbal**,
**ganj-e-inayat-sarkar**, **wasif-ali-wasif**, **ghazi-ilm-din-shaheed** and
**abul-faiz-qalander-ali-suharwardi**. **Absence proves nothing**: roughly a fifth of the contents titles
on pp. 2–5 are `[illegible]` in whole or in part, and the author's own inclusion criterion is fame, not
existence (p. 93, folio 96).

### Dates — the pattern first, then the sets that cannot all stand

**The pattern.** Almost every chronogram numeral in this book is `[OCR?]`-flagged, and the verses that
carry the chronograms are flagged more heavily than the digits. Not one date in pp. 148–157 (folios
151–160) is unflagged: "۱۱۶۵[OCR?]", "۱۰۵۸[OCR?]" twice, "۱۰۸۲[OCR?]", "۱۰۸۰[OCR?]", "۱۰۴۳[OCR?]",
"۱۰۴۴[OCR?]". Across pp. 236–348 nearly every death and building year carries a flag, and across
pp. 349–463 the same holds for the dated English-period buildings. **No date from this book may enter an
entry without being checked against the lithograph at the page given.** Two further rules follow from the
partials:

- **An unflagged numeral is not a verified numeral.** Where digits are clean the verse words carrying them
  may still be flagged — at the Gulabi Bagh the digits are clean while the chronogram words are flagged
  ("نغش[OCR?]", "تاریخی[OCR?]", "سہ اعمی[OCR?]") (p. 307, folio 310; p. 310, folio 313). The unflagged
  years are therefore the ones **worth checking first**, not the ones that can be used: "طاق ۱۰۲۳"
  (p. 142, folio 145); "سمت ۱۹۳۰", "سمت ۱۹۳۱ مطابق ۱۸۷۵ء", "ماہ فروری ۱۸۷۵ء" (pp. 130–131, folios
  133–134); "١٢٨٢ ہجری" (p. 176, folio 179); "سنہ ١٢٦٦ ہجری" (p. 177, folio 180); "سنہ ١٢٩٤" on Mulla
  Majid's inscription (p. 183, folio 186); "سمت ١٨٩٥ بکرمی" and "سمت ١٩١٩" (pp. 190–191, folios 193–194);
  "سمبت ۱۸۹۸ بکرمی" (p. 228, folio 231); "سنہ ۱۰۵۵ ہجری" (p. 295, folio 298); "۸ ذی الحج سنہ ۹۹۰ ہجری",
  "۲۳ ذی الحج سنہ ۱۰۳۰ ہجری", "۱۶ ذی الحج سنہ ۱۰۳۵" (no era) (pp. 303–304, folios 306–307); "سنہ ۶۱۵
  ہجری" and "سنہ ۱۰۱۷ ہجری" at Bibi Pak Daman (pp. 305–306, folios 308–309); "سنہ ۱۸۴۹ ع" (p. 302, folio
  305); "سمت ۱۸۹۵ بکرمی" (p. 276, folio 279); "سنہ ۴۳۱ ہجری", "سنہ ۴۶۵" and "چونتیس برس" at Data Ganj
  Bakhsh (p. 289, folio 292); "ایک ہزار ایک سو دو" (p. 257, folio 260); "۲۳۵" and "۱۰۶۶" (p. 307, folio
  310); "سنہ ۱۰۸۰ ہجری" (p. 310, folio 313); "۱۲۵۵" (p. 314, folio 321); "سنہ ۱۸۷۲ء" (p. 381, folio 388);
  "سنہ ۱۸۶۲ء", "سنہ ۱۸۶۵ء", "سنہ ۱۸۶۴ء" (p. 402, folio 409); "سنہ ۱۸۸۱" (p. 429, folio 436); and سمت ۱۹۱۰,
  ۱۹۳۷, ۱۹۳۴, ۱۹۳۵, ۱۹۳۸ (pp. 445–456, folios 452–463). Flagging is also **inconsistent within one
  inscription**: Mulla Majid's "سنہ ١٢٣٥[OCR?]" is flagged twice while the rebuild year "سنہ ١٢٩٤" on the
  same inscription is not, and the same year is flagged one page earlier for a neighbouring mosque
  (pp. 182–183, folios 185–186); "سمبت ۱۸۹۸ بکرمی" at Tali Sahib is unflagged (p. 228, folio 231) while
  "سمت ۱۸۸۰[OCR?] بکرمی" at Than Bhairo is flagged (p. 231, folio 234).
- **Years printed with no era at all. Supply none.** "۱۲۸۲[OCR?]" with no era and no gloss (p. 12, folio
  7); "سنہ ۱۲۹۳" with no era word printed, so "Hijri" there is (not stated) (pp. 52–54, folios 55–57);
  "۱۰۲۳" (p. 142, folio 145); "سنہ[OCR?] ۱۱۲۶[OCR?]" (p. 144, folio 147); Shah Bilawal's "ایک ہزار
  چہیالیس[OCR?]" written out in words with **no ھ and no "ہجری"** (p. 235, folio 238); Ranjit Singh's
  "۱۸۹۶[OCR?] مہینہ ہگرمی[OCR?]", where **the numeral is flagged, the month name is flagged and
  unreadable, and the calendar is not stated** (p. 217, folio 220); ۱۰۵۸[OCR?] (Abdul Wahab Qadri, p. 265,
  folio 268); ۱۱۹۴[OCR?] (Jani Khan, p. 270, folio 273); ۱۲۹۰[OCR?] (Qutb Shah Qadri, p. 271, folio 274);
  ۶۰۴[OCR?] (Shah Husain Zanjani, p. 275, folio 278); "سنہ ۱۲۷۴[OCR?]" (Abdul Jalil Qutb-e Alam, p. 247,
  folio 250); ۶۳۱[OCR?] (Shah Sarwani, p. 303, folio 306); ١٠٥٧[OCR?] (Shalamar's chronogram, p. 348,
  folio 355); and the Sunehri Masjid's year printed twice in one sentence as "یعنی سنہ ۱۱۶۳ھ[OCR?] بیرونی",
  the force of "بیرونی" unclear (p. 147, folio 150). The colophon's two numerals are also era-less — see
  "Provenance".

**Mutually impossible or unreconciled date sets, with their pages. Recorded as printed; not reconciled.**

- **The book's own date.** Chronogram "۱۲۸۲[OCR?]", no era, no gloss (p. 12, folio 7) against the notables
  chapter dating itself "سنہ ۱۸۸۳ع یعنی اسی سال میں" (p. 67, folio 70). **Do not convert either.**
- **The two mosque elapsed-age figures cannot both be reckoned from one moment of writing.** Masjid
  Muhammad Salih Kambo dated 1058 AH[OCR?] and called 226[OCR?] years old (p. 152, folio 155); Masjid
  Wazir Khan 1043–1044 AH[OCR?] and 251 years old (p. 155, folio 158). The inscription dates are about
  fourteen or fifteen years apart while the elapsed figures are twenty-five years apart. **Do not use
  either figure to date the book's composition.**
- **The Baoli's Bikrami dates cannot all stand.** Ranjit Singh's illness "سن ۱۸۹۶[OCR?] بکرمی" (p. 112,
  folio 115); the building begun "سن ۱۸۹۰[OCR?] بکرمی مین[OCR?]" and running five years (p. 113, folio
  116); his death "سن ۱۸۹۶ بکرمی", unflagged, with the building unfinished (p. 113, folio 116). **An
  illness in 1896 cannot precede a construction start in 1890.** No CE or Hijri equivalent is printed for
  any of them.
- **The fourth "صدمہ" contradicts itself on the reign, twice over.** Headed as falling "سلطان جلال الدین
  فیروز شاہ خلجی کے وقت", but the king who answers is "شمس الدین التمش [OCR?]" returning from a **Gujrat**
  campaign (p. 26, folio 29) — and Iltutmish has already been placed two pages earlier as Aibak's
  successor (p. 25, folio 20). The invading commander is "تیمور [OCR?]" while his overlord is "جغتائی خان
  [OCR?]" of Transoxiana. **The reign attribution and the named respondent cannot both stand. Do not carry
  any of this as a date.**
- **Text year against marginal numeral: three disagreements in one chunk.** Nawab Imam ud Din Khan's
  mosque, text "سنہ ١٢٦٦ ہجری" against margins "٧٦[OCR?] ١٢" and "٩٦[OCR?] ١٢" — **three different values
  for one mosque** (pp. 177–178, folios 180–181); Moran's, text "سنہ ١٢٢٣[OCR?]" against margin "٢٤[OCR?]
  ١٢" (pp. 179–180, folios 182–183); Bokan Khan's, text "سنہ ١٢٥٥[OCR?]" against margin "١٢٥٧[OCR?]"
  (pp. 181–182, folios 184–185). **Carry both numbers wherever either is used.**
- **A marginal "١٢٩٦" with nothing to attach it to**, printed immediately before Chhajju Bhagat's death
  date where there is no chronogram verse, and **unflagged** unlike the other margins (p. 186, folio 189).
  It cannot be assigned.
- **Chhajju Bhagat.** Two flagged dates given as equivalents, "سمت ایک ہزار چہہ[OCR?] سو چہیانوین[OCR?]
  بکرمی" and "سنہ ١٠٥٣[OCR?] ہجری" (p. 186, folio 189), while the entry opens by placing him in Shah
  Jahan's reign (p. 184, folio 187). **Do not convert or reconcile.** His death is also reported twice and
  differently: a dated death, then "عام مشہور اسطرح پر ہے کہ چہجو بہگت کی وفات ظاہر طور پر وقوع میں نہیں
  آئی بلکہ چوبارہ میں بیٹہا ہوا ہی غائب ہو گیا" (p. 186, folio 189).
- **Sayyid Ishaq Gazruni's dynastic placing and his year sit together unexplained** — both "تغلقیہ سلطنت
  کے وقت لاہور میں آیا" and death "سنہ ۷۰۶[OCR?]" (pp. 162–163, folios 165–166). The year is flagged, the
  era statement is not, and the book does not reconcile them.
- **The Wasdev renovation carries two years one line apart** — "فی زمانہ حال یعنی سمت ۱۹۳۷[OCR?]" for the
  work and "سمت ۱۹۳۶[OCR?] مین بنوایا" on the brick (p. 130, folio 133). Both flagged.
- **Ghore Shah's death year cannot be reconciled with the story.** He died "پانچ برس کی عمر میں", aged
  five (p. 237, folio 240), yet the death is dated "ماہ ربیع الاول سنہ ۱۰۰۳[OCR?] ہجری" with no birth year
  and no dates for his grandfather or father (p. 237, folio 240).
- **Mian Mir's and Mian Wada's death years are printed identically** — both "سنہ ۱۰۴۵[OCR?] ہجری" (p. 239,
  folio 242; p. 242, folio 245). **Verify as a real coincidence rather than an OCR repetition.**
- **Hamid Qari's dates cannot all stand.** Put "محمد شاہ بادشاہ کے وقت" in Lahore, the present mosque said
  to be his own building, its chronogram "۱۱۴۱ [OCR?]", his death "سنہ ۱۰۶۴ [OCR?] ہجری" (pp. 252–253,
  folios 255–256) — **both numerals flagged and seventy-seven years apart**; the reign agrees with the
  mosque, not the death year.
- **Bahadur Khan's date is broken twice over**: "دربار اکبر بادشاہ مین … اور شاہجہان [OCR?] ۱۰۱۰ [OCR?]
  مین فوت ہو کر" (p. 257, folio 260) — an Akbar-court amir, a flagged emperor's name and a flagged year in
  one clause. **Do not carry "1010" without this note.**
- **Jani Khan: two statements that sit badly together.** The tomb "احمد شاہ بادشاہ کے عہد مین بنا تہا"
  (p. 269, folio 272) while its occupant is said to have **defeated Ahmad Shah Durrani** and to have died
  "۱۱۹۴ [OCR?]" (p. 270, folio 273).
- **Shah Gada Lahori's age and career do not agree**: came with **Humayun[OCR?]** on his second[OCR?]
  entry into Hindustan, twelve years in the jungles, died "۹۹۱ [OCR?]", reached "ایکسو چودہ [OCR?]" years
  (pp. 274–275, folios 277–278). All three numerals flagged.
- **Shah Ismail's three figures do not agree**: arrival "سنہ ۴۱۸[OCR?] ہجری", death "سنہ ۴۵۸[OCR?] ہجری",
  residence "۳۶[OCR?] برس", chronogram "مہتاب" for "۴۵۸[OCR?]" — all four flagged, and the stated
  residence does not match the interval (p. 298, folio 301).
- **Muhammad Fazil's era against his death year**: "شاہجہانی عہد مین" against "۱۱۱۰[OCR?] ہجری", with his
  son's at "۱۱۴۷[OCR?] ہجری" (p. 297, folio 300).
- **Shah Sarwani's year carries no era** — "سنہ ۶۳۱[OCR?] میں اسکا انتقال ہوا" (p. 303, folio 306) — and
  the same entry makes him the murid of Shaikh Shams-ud-Din Turk Panipati while the Zarin Khan family
  whose silsila "شاہ سروانی کے ساتہہ ملتا ہے" is dated 990–1246 AH (pp. 303–304, folios 306–307).
- **Data Ganj Bakhsh's three numerals do not agree, and are kept apart.** Arrival 431 AH, death 465 AH and
  "thirty-four years" are internally consistent and **unflagged** (p. 289, folio 292); the "۳۷۶ [OCR?]"
  printed under the gilded gate verses on the same page is not reconciled with them, and the verses are
  flagged line by line.
- **darbar-abul-muali-qadri: the book against the row.** The book prints "[OCR?] ماہ ربیع الاول سنہ ۱۰۲۵
  [OCR?] ہجری" and, lower on the same page, "۲۱ ربیع الاول سنہ ۱۰۲۵ [OCR?]" (p. 285, folio 288); the index
  row reads **16 Rabi ul Awal 1024 AH**. Both stand; the book's own day-number in the first statement is
  flagged and absent.
- **Zeb-un-Nisa Begum's death year is printed twice, incompatibly, on one page**: prose "سنہ ۱۰۸۰ ہجری"
  (unflagged) against the chronogram's "۱۰۹۰[OCR?]" (p. 310, folio 313). Her **parentage** is also given
  one way and implied another within two pages: "دختر نیک اختر شاہ جہان بادشاہ" (p. 361, folio 368), then
  a page later she acts together with **عالمگیر** in summoning Abul Khair (p. 362, folio 369) and it is
  "شاہ عالمگیر" who embanks the river after her garden is destroyed (p. 361, folio 368). The author
  separately settles an identification on his own authority: "زیب النسا بیگم اور زیبندہ[OCR?] بیگم دونو
  نام ایک ہیں" (p. 362, folio 369).
- **Jahangir's tomb carries two years**: construction "سنہ ۱۰۳۴[OCR?] ہجری" (p. 314, folio 321) and the
  grave inscription "سنہ ۱۰۳۷[OCR?]" (p. 316, folio 323).
- **The Kambo entry contradicts itself about who died first.** "عنایت اللہ نے اسکی وفات کے بعد اسکی تکمیل
  کی" makes **Inayatullah complete the book after Salih's death** (p. 312, folio 315), while the dates make
  **Inayatullah die first, 1051[OCR?] AH**, and Salih build the tomb over his grave, dying in **1080[OCR?]**
  (p. 313, folio 316).
- **Abdul Jalil Qutb-e Alam's enclosure date and its chronogram disagree**: prose "سنہ ۱۲۷۴[OCR?]" (era
  unstated) against the chronogram's marginal "۱۲۸۵[OCR?]" on the same page — an eleven-year gap, both
  flagged, with the chronogram's second line itself flagged (p. 247, folio 250).
- **Repeated numerals that may be OCR repetition rather than fact.** "۱۰۸۰" recurs three times within four
  pages for three different people — Zeb-un-Nisa (unflagged, p. 310, folio 313), Muhammad Salih
  (1080[OCR?], p. 313, folio 316), Shah Kamal (1080[OCR?], p. 313, folio 316): **treat all three as
  unverified.** "۱۲۴۷[OCR?]" appears twice in three pages — the Gul Begum garden chronogram (p. 293, folio
  296) and the Muhammad Sultan mosque's founding slab (p. 294, folio 297) — and again, unrelated, as the
  year of Data Ganj Bakhsh's dome (p. 290, folio 293): **check whether all are genuine.** The same flagged
  Samvat year "سمت ۱۹۰۰ [OCR?] بکرمی" is given for two unrelated things, the building of the Bhagat Ram
  shiwala (p. 100, folio 103) and the death of the majzub whose samadh is at the Wazir Khan shiwala
  (p. 94, folio 97) — where **as printed it is the samadh, not the man, that dies**, and the occupant's
  name is damaged ("و نہ کا سایئن [OCR?]"): **suspect an OCR artefact**; the Bhagat Ram date is said to be
  inscribed on the building, so it is checkable. The same flagged 1876 is given for two different men
  (p. 68, folio 71).
- **Montgomery Hall's completion sits against its re-roofing**: completed "سنہ ۱۸۶۶ء[OCR?]" while the
  replacement roof was ready "سنہ ۱۸۶۵ء کے اخیر میں", on the same page; the 1866 numeral is flagged and
  the 1865 is not (p. 402, folio 409).
- **The Mian Mir church's dates and its stated duration disagree**: "سنہ ۱۸۵۰[OCR?]ء و سنہ ۱۸۵۷[OCR?]ء
  یعنی قریباً چھہ سال کے عرصہ میں" gives seven years but glosses it as about six (p. 390, folio 397).
- **Bhai Jawahar Singh is both dead and collecting.** p. 114 (folio 117) says a raja's or sardar's rupees
  "بہائی جواہر سنگہ لیتا ہی", and four lines later "اب بہائی جواہر سنگہ کو ایک سال کا عرصہ ہوا کہ مر گیا
  ہے". The present-tense passage probably describes the period before his death, but the book does not say
  so. **Quote both.**
- **Rai Mool Singh is dead on p. 61 (folio 64) and a sitting committee member on p. 62 (folio 65)**, with
  the death dated to 1883 at p. 67 (folio 70). Not a contradiction in fact but a seam in the book's
  revision: **any entry using the Municipal Committee list must date it before 1883.**
- **A doubled duration in one sentence**: "برسوں[OCR?] تک یہ شہر چند سال تک ویران پڑا رہا" (p. 23, folio
  18) — "for years[OCR?]" and "for some years" both appear; the same event is "چند سال" at p. 17 (folio
  12), so the length of the desolation is unfixed.

**Years the book does not give, or that are lost.** Recorded as **(not stated)**; do not supply one.
Tuhfat al-Wasilin's "سنہ[OCR?] [illegible] ہجری" (p. 14, folio 9); Amir Khusrau's "آٹھویں صدی ہی
[illegible] ہجری کی ابتدا" (p. 13, folio 8); the Durrani farman's "ماہ رمضان [illegible] ہجری"
(pp. 52–54, folios 55–57); the lost decade digits in "آخر سنہ ۱۸۵[illegible]ع" and "سنہ ۱۸۳[illegible]ع"
(pp. 63–72, folios 66–75); the Badshahi Masjid's completion figure, "اس مسجد کی عمارت عرصہ[OCR?]
[illegible] مین باختتام پہنچی" (p. 142, folio 145); Hassu Teli's year wholly lost, "شیخ حسو تیلی سنہ
[illegible] ہجری مین فوت ہو کر یہان مدفون ہوا" (p. 269, folio 272); Shah Chiragh's death year (p. 281,
folio 284); the year of Sardar Khan Baloch's rebuilding at Abdullah Shah (p. 282, folio 285); the Hijri
equivalent of Taj Shah's death (p. 285, folio 288); the year of the Prince of Wales's visit to Landa
Bazar, **illegible** (p. 360, folio 367), and no year at all for his session at the New College (p. 383,
folio 390); the **railway station's foundation year, lost to OCR** in the clause naming John Lawrence's
hand, with an `[illegible]` immediately after (p. 425, folio 432); "١٨٧٤", legible but with the words on
both sides illegible, so **what it dates cannot be recovered** (p. 330, folio 337); Abul Khair's death
year (p. 363, folio 370); Makho Shah's purchase dated only "سات برس کا عرصہ گزرا ہے" (p. 437, folio 444);
Sukh Dayal's shiwala only "چالیس سال ہو گئی ہیں", with no anchor for the reckoning (p. 459, folio 466);
Sardha Ram's death only "چند سال سے" (p. 441, folio 448); the Ishri[OCR?] Prashad shiwala only "سکہوں کے
وقت" (p. 440, folio 447); Baba Dehona Das's foundation only "ہر کہی[OCR?] وقت میں", which gives no usable
year (p. 442, folio 449); and the last temple entry's "اسی سال" (p. 459, folio 466). Note that
**"۱۸۷۸[OCR?]ع" is the single absolute anchor** for a whole run of relative intervals in pp. 46–60 — five
years, four years, last year, six or seven years, the author's own 32[OCR?] years — **and it is flagged**
(p. 60, folio 63); "سنہ ۳۹۱[OCR?] ہجری" and "۸۲۰ [OCR?] ہجری" are **the only two absolute dates** in
pp. 15–30, both flagged (pp. 22, 29; folios 17, 32); and in pp. 31–45 only "۱۲۹۱ ہجری" is unflagged
(p. 42, folio 45) beside "۱۸۵۰ [OCR?] ع" twice and "۱۸۶۰ [OCR?] ع" (pp. 37, 41; folios 40, 44). Other
flagged years on which a single fact depends: "سنہ ۱۸۵۶ [OCR?]" for the Khoja mosque nazul and claim
(p. 84, folio 87); "سنہ ۱۸۵۰ [OCR?] ء" for the Kotwali and bazaar widening, with the Assistant
Commissioner's surname "تہارت مل [OCR?]" unrecognisable (p. 96, folio 99); "سنہ ۱۲۷۳[OCR?] ہجری", "سنہ
۱۲۸۰ ہجری", "سنہ ۱۲۸۲[OCR?] ہجری" (pp. 52–54, folios 55–57); "۱۸۶۵ء[OCR?]" for the rains that felled Bagh
Aluwalia (p. 352, folio 359); "۱۰۳۵[OCR?] ہجری" under "جہانگیر بادشاہ غازی[OCR?]" for Sarai Golian Wali
with "وس[OCR?] سال" of cannonball storage (p. 365, folio 372); "سنہ ۱۸۵۰ء[OCR?]" for the kotwali (p. 369,
folio 376); "سنہ ۱۸۷۰[OCR?]ء" for the hospital (p. 377, folio 384); "۱۰۷۲[OCR?] ہجری" for the Gilani
death (p. 395, folio 402); "۱۸۸۱[OCR?]ء", "۱۸۸۲[OCR?]ء", "جنوری سنہ ۱۸۸۲[OCR?]ء" (pp. 384, 387, folios

**Chronograms.** **No date taken from a chronogram anywhere in this book may be used without checking the
plate.** The Mian Mir chronogram is flagged in **every line** — "میان میر مسعود فتر جار خان[OCR?]", "رشک
اکسیر شد[OCR?]", "سفر جانب شہر چار بکرد[OCR?]", "دلگیر شد[OCR?]", "بفردوس والامیا تمبر شد[OCR?]" (p. 244,
folio 247) — so it cannot be arithmetically checked, and Mian Mir's death year rests on it plus the
flagged prose "سنہ ۱۰۴۵[OCR?] ہجری" (p. 242, folio 245) alone. The Data Ganj Bakhsh gate verses are
flagged line by line (p. 289, folio 292). In pp. 251–264 every one of five quoted sets carries at least
one flagged word and in three cases the flag falls **in the rhyme or in the chronogram's operative
phrase** (pp. 254, 257, 261; folios 257, 264, 260). Both chronogram qitas in pp. 177–182 are transcribed
with flags inside the rhyme words, so **no chronogram in that chunk can be counted to check a date**, and
the name in Bokan Khan's own chronogram ("زمکن[OCR?] خان" against the entry's "بوکن خان") is itself in
doubt. All the Persian inscription verses of pp. 162–176 are heavily flagged and **should not be quoted as
text** — Mirza Muhammad's chronogram (pp. 171–172, folios 174–175), Nura's (p. 174, folio 177), and the
second Nur Muhammad mosque's four lines with two broken marginal year-figures, "٩٧ ١٢[OCR?]" and "٩
١٢[OCR?]", the second incomplete (p. 176, folio 179). The **seventh inscription of the Wazir Khan mosque
is largely unrecoverable** and the fourth carries an `[illegible]` in its second line (pp. 157–158, folios
160–161). Of the Ahmad Shahi gun: the hemistich "پیکر ارث[OCR?] و بہا کم آتش بار[OCR?]" and the resulting
"سنہ ۱۱۶۴[OCR?] ہجری" are both flagged, the introducing phrase "جسکے بغیر مصرع میں مصرع تاریخ" is itself
garbled (بغیر where a word like آخر would be expected), and the hemistich's numerical value **has not
been checked and must not be recomputed for the archive** (p. 404, folio 411).

### Quantities, sums, dimensions and money — the same pattern, and the sums that do not add

**The pattern.** Whole stretches of this book have no unflagged quantity in them. **Every financial figure
in the Baoli entry except the eight thousand rupee jagir and the two rupees daily carries a flag**:
"چہتیس[OCR?]" and "پینتیس[OCR?]" steps, "ایکسو[OCR?] سنگل[OCR?] دوکانین", "بہتر[OCR?] ہزار روپیہ",
"پچاس[OCR?] روپیے ماہوار", "اڑہائی[OCR?] روپیے ماہوار", "کئی[OCR?] ہزار روپیہ", and "ہمیشہ[OCR?]" in the
two-rupee daily offering (pp. 109–114, folios 112–117). **Every quantity in pp. 192–206 (folios 195–209)
is flagged or garbled** — "گیارہ[OCR?]" asthans, "ایکسو[OCR?] درویش", "آٹہہ[OCR?] گہاؤن[OCR?]", "قریب دس
گہاؤن[OCR?]", "نہ چار دہ نہ[OCR?] چاہ" (an unreadable count of wells), "بیشمار[OCR?] روپیہ", "ادہیس[OCR?]
سال", "گیارہ[OCR?] میں بیٹھے" — the unflagged figures there being only "پانچ فٹ", "بیس قدم", "ڈیڑہ گز",
"قریب ڈیڑہ سو آدمی" and "پچیس پچیس روپے": **no land or income figure from that stretch may enter an entry
without a check.** In pp. 207–221 the flagged figures are "انیس[OCR?] سیڑہیان", "بائیس[OCR?] زینہ",
"اونیس[OCR?]", "دو نو پاؤن", "پچیس[OCR?] برس", "چوودہ[OCR?] بیگہ", "ستو[OCR?] برسا[OCR?]" and "ایک
سو[OCR?] روپیہ سالانہ"; the unflagged are "بیس بیگہ", "چالیس بیگہ", "پانسو روپیہ", the eight-to-sixteen
columns, the eleven ranis and two pigeons, and "سترہ برس". In pp. 423–425 (folios 430–432) the machinery,
water-lifting and printing passages carry runs of flagged nonsense and **the quantities in those sentences
should not be used** at all.

**Counts and sums that cannot all be right.**

- **The bell-count of the fourth Mehr Das mandir has three readings on one page**: heading "اسی گہنٹے والی
  دیوی"; text "جو اسی[OCR?] عدد گہنٹے" with **"۸۲[OCR?]" printed in the margin**; custodianship note "دیوی
  چوراسی گہنٹے والی", the Devi of eighty-four bells (p. 131, folio 134). **Eighty, eighty-two and
  eighty-four cannot be reconciled.**
- **"گویا وہ نو شوالے ایک ہی[OCR?] سال مین"** (pp. 130–131, folios 133–134) reads literally as "those nine
  shivalas", but this book writes دونوں as "دو نو" throughout and only **two** shivalas in the enclosure
  are dated Samat 1930. **Do not carry a count of nine.**
- **Shalamar's bigha figures cannot be contained in their own total.** Faiz Bakhsh alone "ساٹہ[OCR?] بیگہ";
  Hayat Bakhsh "اسی قدر جبکہ قدیم فیض بخش کی ہے", the same; Farah Bakhsh "۳۰[OCR?] بیگہ" under trees and
  ten more, "کل ملکر[OCR?] چالیس بیگہ تخمیناً"; then "کل باغ کی زمین ساٹہ بیگہ ہے" (pp. 351–352, folios
  358–359). Every numeral except the ten and the forty is flagged or written out in words.
- **The count of seven gardens is incomplete as printed**: "اول" Angoori, "دوسرا" Inayat[OCR?] Abad, then
  "اول / دوسرا / تیسرا" for Faiz Bakhsh, Hayat Bakhsh and Farah Bakhsh, then a jump to "ساتواں" Gulabi;
  **Mahtabi Bagh is named but never numbered**, so which garden the book counts as sixth is not stated
  (pp. 349–351, folios 356–358; p. 350, folio 357).
- **"ہشت[OCR?] چاہ کلاں", eight great wells, against a list that then itemises ten** (p. 327, folio 334).
- **The image list at the Bhagat Ram shiwala prints twelve ordinals but repeats Ganesh and conflates two
  deities at the eighth — do not report a count** (p. 100, folio 103); the **kalas count** there is
  self-contradictory as transcribed, so **no total should be quoted** (pp. 98–99, folios 101–102).
- **A parapet count that disagrees with itself in one paragraph** — "اونیس[OCR?]" small finialled domelets
  against "سات سات گنبدیوں منڈیروں پر", seven and seven, in the unnamed samadh at p. 207 (folio 210).
- **Mufti Ghulam Sarwar's output is "ستر کتابیں", seventy, at p. 53 (folio 56) and "اٹھارہ [OCR?]
  کتابیں", eighteen[OCR?], at p. 78 (folio 81)** — a contradiction inside one range.
- **The shop count at Rai Singh's Thakurdwara is uncertain**: "دو کانیں" in one clause and "چہ[OCR?]
  دوکانوں کے اوپر" in the next (pp. 455–456, folios 462–463).
- **"بایس[OCR?] لاکہ" and "ایک گیارہ[OCR?]" slave-girls in the hakim's reward — cite the components, not
  the total**, since the printed total is not the sum of the stated items plus jewellery of unstated value
  (p. 160, folio 163).
- **"دس[OCR?]" in the Wazir Khan minaret-repair passage**, where **the noun for the openings is illegible
  and the numeral does not construe — report the alteration, not the numbers** (p. 174, folio 177).
- **"بارہ دفعہ دہلی" sits in a sentence broken by an [illegible] and no arithmetic should be built on it**
  (p. 25, folio 20). The wage series turns on "اونے [OCR?]" (p. 45, folio 48).
- **The jagir "نو ہزار نو سو پچپن ۹۹۵۵[OCR?] روپیے" agrees in words and numerals**, which is some
  protection, but the verb "ما گزار تہی[OCR?]" is corrupt, so **whether this was an assignment or a payment
  of revenue cannot be settled** (p. 67, folio 70). The mortgage of Muhammad Sultan's estate is
  "کسی[OCR?] لاکھہ روپے کو", **the number of lakhs lost** (p. 75, folio 78). Rent figures that cannot all
  be right: "کوئی دو سو روپیہ سال کوئی تین[OCR?] روپیہ سال کوئی چار روپیہ سال" (p. 336, folio 343) — the
  first figure is two orders of magnitude off the others, and "دو سو" is unflagged while "تین" is flagged.
  "بارہ ہزار[OCR?] چاہ", **implausible on its face** (p. 347, folio 354); "تین[OCR?] حاکمان لاہور"
  (p. 348, folio 355).
- **Money lost outright.** **Both money figures in the Haqiqat Rai endowment passage are lost to
  `[illegible]`** — the Sikh-period jagir revenue "جمع [illegible] سال" and the British cash pension
  "[illegible] روپیے سالانہ نقد" (p. 209, folio 212): **the structure of the grant survives; neither
  amount does.** Money figures with a flag on the decisive word: the hospital's "ایک لاکھ سچپین[OCR?]
  ہزار روپے" (p. 377, folio 384); the kachahri's "ایک لاکہہ روپیہ" preceded by an [illegible] word
  qualifying "سرکار" (p. 375, folio 382); the church's "نوے[OCR?] ہزار" and "پینتالیس ۴۵[OCR?] ہزار"
  (p. 394, folio 401); the Senate Hall donor's title "نوابا[OCR?]" though the sum is clean (p. 376, folio
  383); the Medical School's "ایک لاکہہ ۳۲[OCR?] ہزار" (p. 432, folio 439); the Chhote Lal shiwala's
  "ہزار[OCR?] روپیہ" (p. 447, folio 454); Nau Lakha's "سالانہ آمدنی نو لاکہہ روپیہ تہی[OCR?]" (p. 355,
  folio 362) — whose **name has two explanations printed without a choice**, annual income of nine lakh
  and, on "بعض کہتے ہیں", nine lakh spent (p. 355, folio 362; p. 356, folio 363). The Qur'an and sword
  taken by the Sikhs are valued at "کسی ہزار روپیے" (p. 265, folio 268).
- **Words and numerals disagreeing inside one measurement, left as printed**: the Deputy Commissioner's
  hall "طول میں سو[OCR?] ۳۴[OCR?] فٹ" (p. 373, folio 380); a Senate Hall room "اُنتیسا[OCR?] ۹[OCR?] فٹ
  طول" (p. 376, folio 383); the hospital's breadth "ساڑھے اکیاون ۵۱ فٹ" (p. 378, folio 385); the kachahri
  "۳۰۲[OCR?] فٹ" overall against a northern range alone of "۲۳۷[OCR?] [illegible] فیٹ" (p. 373, folio
  380); the Art School hall "اٹھائیس فٹ لمبا اور چوبیس ۳۴[OCR?] فٹ چوڑا" (p. 385, folio 392); Lawrence
  Hall's great room, **unusable as printed**, "ساٹہ ہر پینتیس[OCR?] فٹ لمبا اور ساٹہ ہر تیس فٹ چوڑا"
  (p. 401, folio 408); the Ravi bridge's piers "۳۵[OCR?] پائے اور ۳۳[OCR?]" in one clause (p. 410, folio
  417); the District Jail's width printed twice, "عرض ۵۵۰[OCR?] ۷۵۰[OCR?] فٹ" (p. 415, folio 422); a
  European prisoners' room with **four numbers for three dimensions** (p. 413, folio 420); a District Jail
  workshop "سو ۹[OCR?] فٹ", nine or one hundred and nine (p. 417, folio 424); the Medical School's
  "بیالیکس ۲۴[OCR?] فٹ لمبا" and width "اکیسو[OCR?] اکتالیس فٹ" (pp. 430–431, folios 437–438); and the
  Mian Mir church's **110 ft doing double duty** as external north–south width (p. 390, folio 397) and as

- **Dimensions impossible as printed, recorded unreconciled**: the asylum's great gate "۷۳۴[OCR?] فٹ اونچا
  سقف تک" against a ten-foot width (p. 418, folio 425); the District Jail hospital "۸۴۳[OCR?] فٹ لمبا"

  (pp. 412–413, folios 419–420); and the unnamed church's interior width of 15 ft inside an 80 ft exterior

  folio 422), against the Central Jail's "چہار گوشہ", four-cornered (p. 411, folio 418), while only one
  length and width are given for it.
- **Other flagged quantities on which a single stated fact rests**: "یہ[OCR?] سو برس" — the numeral of the
