### Damaged and unreadable text — gaps that destroy a fact, and passages needing the lithograph before use

**The most consequential damaged sentences.** Each of these would change a site's status, so each must be
re-read from the PDF before anything is written from it.

- **"مسجد بیچ گئی ہے" — a mosque appears to have been sold.** At p. 178 (folio 181) the passage on the back
  of Hafiz Chabuk Sawar's mosque reads "مسجد کی پشت پر پشت سنگ کا ہے[OCR?] متعلق مسجد ہی تہی میں جو
  نہر[OCR?] کی شرک میں اُسی میں ہین مگر مسجد بیچ گئی ہے", not construable as transcribed but **ending in a
  statement that the mosque was sold. Potentially the most consequential sentence in that chunk for the
  mosque's status.**
- **An "old mosque" listed among Shahid Ganj's own appurtenances.** "ایک مسجدہ کہنہ و خراس وغیرہ چند
  سمادہیں اسی مکان کے متعلق ہین" (p. 217, folio 220) — the word is OCR-clean but the syntax makes the
  mosque one of the establishment's properties. **Given what this site later becomes, this single clause
  must be re-read from the PDF before it is quoted.**
- **The plinth-storey sentence at the Ranjit Singh samadh is unusable as it stands** — "جنکے دروازے شمالی و
  دیوار کے نیچے مسجد[OCR?] و ہین اور مستفرق[OCR?] لوگ اُسمیں میام[OCR?] پذیر ہین" (p. 221, folio 224):
  **three flags in one clause, one of them on the word "mosque"**, in a passage about who lives under a
  samadh adjoining the Badshahi Mosque. **Potentially significant; currently unreadable.**
- **The handover clause of the Badshahi Masjid contains an unread phrase** — "سرکار نے براہ دریا ولی[OCR?]
  مسجد مسلمانون کے حوالے کر دی" (p. 141, folio 144) — which governs the **manner** of the restitution and
  cannot be glossed safely; compare "بکمال دریا دلی" used of MacGregor (p. 143, folio 146).
- **The tomb of Rasul Shah** — "بار[OCR?] اگر وہ اس فرقہ کا [illegible] میں سر جہان[OCR?] انکے پیشوا کا
  مقبرہ ہے" (p. 248, folio 251): **a human must re-read this page before anything is said about it.**
- **"مرا وبیگم" — the name of the woman who ruled Lahore is lost.** "جب حکومت لاہور کی مرا وبیگم اُسکی
  زوجہ کے قبضہ اقتدار مین آئی" (p. 149, folio 152), i.e. the wife of Mir Muin-ul-Mulk; the preceding word
  is garbled and no title survives intact. **Re-read this line before the murder of Nawab Bukhari Khan is
  attributed to a named person.**
- **A garbled clause puts Ranjit Singh inside the Mughal building history of Mian Mir's tomb**: "مہاراجہ
  رنجیت سنگہ نے اس مقبرہ کے قیمتی پتہر اوتر والئے[OCR?] میں پہر اس عالیشان مکان کی بنیاد رکھی" (p. 242,
  folio 245) sits between Dara Shikoh's first building and Dara Shikoh's laying of the foundation, two
  centuries out of sequence. **Do not cite the removal of the precious stones from this sentence without
  re-reading the page.** On the same page **"پوتا خلیہ[OCR?]" is the hinge of the Dara Shikoh patronage
  and it is flagged**; the text glosses the sense itself as a disciple at one remove, but the reading of
  the word must not be relied on.
- **A responsible office lost to an illegible word.** The annual repair, new construction, management and
  supervision of Government House "سب [illegible] کے متعلق ہے" (p. 399, folio 406). Because the مولف is
  named in the same role for Montgomery Hall (p. 402, folio 409) and the women's jail (p. 409, folio 416),
  "a reader may be tempted to fill the gap; the chunk does not, and neither do these notes."
- **The Thakur Dwara's builder is lost.** "یہ مندر در بسبت[OCR?] سنگہ مہاراجہ کی علمداری[OCR?] میں تعمیر
  ہوا تہا" (p. 189, folio 192) — a Maharaja's name is in the text and the OCR has not recovered it. **Do
  not supply it.** Likewise "یہ[OCR?] مہاراج" — **which Maharaj gave the land is not recoverable; do not
  supply one** (p. 120, folio 123); and "دار [OCR?] اسکو کے حکم سے" — **the patron who ordered Miskin Shah
  Amri's tomb is unreadable; record the patron as (not stated)** (p. 264, folio 267).
- **Two contradictions inside the waterworks account.** The brick pavements are said to have been taken up
  "بعد اجرا [illegible] پانی کے", after the water was brought into operation, yet the same page says the
  tank's operation was suspended from the day it collapsed; and the closing clause "نلکون کے ذریعہ سے شہر
  نہین پانی آنے لگا" can be read either as water **not** coming through the pipes or, if "نہین" is a
  mis-set "مین", as water **beginning** to come (p. 37, folio 40). **Whether piped water was delivered is
  undecidable from this transcription.**

**Named figures and relationships lost to the damage.**

- **Father-and-son attributions break across the p. 53/p. 54 boundary**: p. 54 (folio 57) opens "اس کے
  پانچ فرزند ہوئے" **with no named subject**, so the five sons cannot be assigned a father; and p. 52
  (folio 55) ends "مفتی رحیم الد[...]" while p. 53 (folio 56) opens "کا گوہر بارش سے گر گیا", so whose
  house fell in the rain is uncertain. Two names are given for one mufti's father (رحیم الدین at p. 53,
  folio 56; رحیم اللہ at p. 54, folio 57), and **the mufti genealogy contains a repeated generation** —
  "محمد ولی بن محمد تقی بن محمد تقی بن مفتی کمال الدین" (p. 52, folio 55), a real father and son of the
  same name or a doubling, with محمد تقی the reference point for two other relationships (pp. 52, 54;
  folios 55, 57). Nawab Muzaffar Khan's eight sons do not reconcile cleanly with the survivors named and
  the intervening clause is corrupt (p. 74, folio 77): **do not state which five died.** The Faqir Nur-ud-
  Din branch promises four sons and does not clearly name the first (p. 72, folio 75).
- **The qaza of Lahore is awarded to two different men one page apart.** p. 50 (folio 53): "مولوی حمید
  ال[OCR?] کو اب بھی سرکار نے لاہور کی قضا کے عہدہ پر ممتاز فرمایا ہی", of the Khalifa Ghulam
  al-Madad[OCR?] house. p. 51 (folio 54): "مولوی جمیع الدین[OCR?] خلیفہ غلام اللہ کا فرزند قاضی بنایا
  گیا". They may be brothers, the same man under two garbled names, or successive appointees. **A human
  must resolve this from the PDF.**
- **Two founders of two different buildings, one relationship, no name.** The founder of Jawahar Singh's
  samadh is identified only as **the mother of Maharaja Dalip Singh and sister of Sardar Jawahar Singh**,
  her personal name unreadable (p. 196, folio 199); the heading nine pages later gives "رانی جنداں والدہ
  مہاراجہ دلیپ سنگہ" but for a **different building**, the Masti Gate thakurdwara (p. 205, folio 208).
  **Do not silently merge the two; record the relationship, not a name.**
- **The Huzuri shajra must not be printed as continuous**: "سید نعمد[OCR?]", "جعفر صورانی[OCR?]", "عبد
  الصمد[OCR?] شاہ", plus "منصور [illegible]" and the descendants' unreadable occupation "جابکگری[OCR?]"
  (pp. 166–167, folios 169–170). The three clauses closing the Huzuri entry are illegible, so **the number
  and identity of the graves in Mufti Ghulam Sarwar's enclosure cannot be given** (p. 267, folio 270). A
  name variant inside that entry is unresolved: the subject is headed "سید جان محمد حضوری" but p. 267
  (folio 270) reads "خاص مقبرہ میان محمد حضوری کے غرب کی سمت"; the book does not say whether these are the
  same person.
- **Dai Anga's personal name is doubly flagged**: "نام سکار [OCR?] زیب النسا [OCR?]" (p. 267, folio 270).
- **The first Bibi's name at Bibi Pak Daman is flagged in both lists**, "بی بی ملچ[OCR?]", along with the
  site's direction, the mosque's builder "نورا ایمان[OCR?] وہ اب اے[OCR?]", the eastern[OCR?] enclosure's
  doorway clause, **the prayer itself** ("ہمکو زمین کلا[OCR?] بیو[OCR?] ندر کرلے"), "چہر[OCR?] بعد اس
  چہیں[OCR?]" before the graves are built, the maidservant "بی بی نور ہی[OCR?]", the father's name and
  year ("سید احمد تخنہ[OCR?]"), and "تذکرہ حاکمیہ[OCR?]", **the title of the source the author prefers**
  (pp. 304–306, folios 307–309). **This is the most heavily damaged material in its range.**

**Passages the partials say need the lithograph before any use.**

- **The Sadhu story's middle is largely lost.** p. 137 (folio 140) carries at least seven `[illegible]`
  gaps, several inside the trial and the gallows speech; the protagonist is called **چوہڑا / سادہو / چور**
  interchangeably, and the trade he robbed is "کہوڑا[OCR?] فروش" in one place and implied to be a
  pakora-fryer in the next. **Whether he was executed for the theft he actually committed cannot be
  recovered** (pp. 136–137, folios 139–140).
- **The crux of the Pir Zaki[OCR?] head story is unreadable** — "مگر جسم ہے سر بہی [illegible] میں
  [illegible] رہا اور بہت سے آدمي [illegible] آخر الامر شہر کے اندر جا کر سر دہو[OCR?] دیا" (p. 165, folio
  168): **who carried the head, and where it was finally set down, cannot be established.**
- **The custodianship paragraph of the Radha Kishan thakurdwara is damaged and internally inconsistent**:
  heading "پنڈت مدسو[OCR?] مرحوم" against body "پنڈت مدسودن[OCR?]"; successor "پنڈت لکہی[OCR?] کشن" in one
  line and "پنڈت رادہا[OCR?] لکہی کشن" in the next; "خاندان [illegible] بحیثیت ننگ[OCR?] کیوقت سے معزز
  وجلیل[OCR?] آتا ہے" not construable; "اُسکا یہ عہدہ آنریری مجسٹریٹی لامحدود[OCR?] سے ممتاز ہے"
  unreadable with confidence. **Do not quote the "Chief Pandit" succession without re-reading the page**
  (p. 134, folio 137).
- **Reign-attributions inside the Sarfaraz Khan story are partly flagged** — "دارا شکوہ" appears as
  "اشکوہ[OCR?]", "اورنگ زیب[OCR?]" is flagged at its key occurrence, and the compensation order contains
  "محالات منزدول[OCR?]" and "دستکاہ[OCR?]", both unglossable, plus four `[illegible]` gaps. **The
  substance — a pre-emptive waqf, accepted and compensated — is legible; the terms of the compensation are
  not** (p. 154, folio 157).
- **A dynasty name is garbled where it matters.** The stripping of the Chinian Wali[OCR?] gateway is dated
  "بوقت زوال سلطنت جیستانی[OCR?] جب گہر گہر راج ہو گیا" (p. 153, folio 156), while the same chunk writes
  "سلطنت چغتائی" for the Mughals (p. 158, folio 161). **The two may be the same word; the chunk does not
  settle it.** The despoiler of the same gateway appears twice, differently — "کو گوہر سنگہ[OCR?] نے" and,
  two clauses later, "سو یہا[OCR?] سنگہ نے نکلوالی" (p. 153, folio 156): **one agent or two cannot be
  determined.**
- **The Tali Sahib architecture is unreconstructible** — p. 227 (folio 230) carries "بارہ گز[OCR?] و
  نیچا[OCR?] چہندڑا[OCR?] بانسی", "منقطع[OCR?]", "گرو کہنا[OCR?]", "پیر و کہنا[OCR?]" three times,
  "سماوہ[OCR?]", "شل[OCR?]" and two `[illegible]` spans. **Only an octagonal platform, a dome, a second
  samadh and a stair survive, and "پیر و کہنا[OCR?]" is treated as a place-name within the enclosure and
  may be nothing of the kind.** **Diwan Kabia[OCR?] Peshawari's sentence is mostly flagged and should not
  be quoted; even his name is uncertain** (p. 227, folio 230).
- **The Than Bhairo mela date cannot be read with confidence** — "ماہ بہادوں کے نو چند سے اتوار کو"
  (p. 232, folio 235): the month is Bhadon and the day a Sunday, but the words between do not resolve, nor
  does "اٹہویں[OCR?] روز[OCR?] کا چڑہاوہ". **Re-read p. 232 before a date is printed.** **The Ratan Chand
  tank's mela clause is unusable as printed** — only "تمام دن … میلا لگا رہتا ہے" can be read, and even its
  subject is unclear (p. 225, folio 228). **A badly damaged passage closes the Hindu-buildings section at
  p. 233 (folio 236)**, losing a builder's name, a structure, and the author's own closing remark about the
  Hindu religious buildings of Lahore — **this page must be re-read.**
- **The two dated inscriptions in pp. 207–221 are its least secure text.** Rani Rind Awi's[OCR?] reads
  "سرگباشی[OCR?] ۱۴[OCR?] مگہر سمت ۱۹۲۷[OCR?] اتوار کے روز" — **day, month, era-year and the verb of death
  all flagged** (p. 211, folio 214). Rani Partab Kunwar's survives only as "سمت ۱۹۱۴[OCR?]" in a sentence
  carrying an `[illegible]` and an unreadable run, so **her name, her title and her date are all in doubt
  together** (p. 212, folio 215). The book says a marble brick is fixed on her door too but **does not
  transcribe it** (not stated).
- **Fazl Shah's first occupation in Lahore is unrecoverable**: "چند سال پہلے ایک مسجد کا نالا رہا" (p. 325,
  folio 332) — the noun is printed نالا and what office or work of a mosque is meant cannot be determined.
  "اب منتظر فاتہ سالانہ ہوتی ہے" (p. 325, folio 332), read as an annual fatiha, is likewise garbled, as is
  "من بعد مینک[OCR?] راجہ دینا ناتہ زندہ رہا", the conjunction governing how long his urs flourished.
- **The Ali Mardan Khan canal sentence is the worst-damaged passage of substance in its stretch**: only
  "Shalamar", "Hansi Hisar" and the Delhi canal are legible enough to cite; **the canals' own names and
  source points are not** (p. 254, folio 257).
- **Three passages are too damaged to paraphrase from context**: the clause about Khushal Singh's unfitted
  door (p. 192, folio 195); the two-line verse whose four rhyme words are flagged (p. 193, folio 196); and
  "کئی بار اس مکان کی قیمت کی فرو دہی میں حکی[OCR?] ہے" in the Suthra demolition passage, **which appears
  to concern a price or sale of the shrine but cannot be rendered** (p. 203, folio 206).
- **`[illegible]` spans elsewhere that remove a fact outright**: "مع [illegible] مین دو کوٹہریان" in the
  Bishno Devi plan (p. 121, folio 124); "چند مورتین سنگین ہین [illegible] اور زیور و تارون[OCR?]" in the
  Chor Mor Wala sanctum, so **the murti count and one item of the inventory are lost** (p. 125, folio 128);
  "بانیہ اسکی ایک ہندوستانی عورت [illegible] وہ مسمات بہگتن تہی", **the foundress's descriptor or place
  lost** (p. 130, folio 133); the pujari sentence in Chhajju Bhagat's house, where **one pujari's name is
  lost and the other's role unclear** (p. 136, folio 139); the closing fragment of the Pir Bhola notice,
  which **appears to add something about money and flowers and cannot be construed** (p. 150, folio 153);
  the Mochi Darwaza entry clause (p. 151, folio 154); the description of Masjid Chinian Wali's[OCR?] age
  and patrons (p. 152, folio 155); the informer's addressee in Aurangzeb's court (p. 154, folio 157); the
  founder's own words during the footprint stratagem (p. 159, folio 162); the opening of the Miran Badshah
  notice, "پہلی [illegible] یہ بزرگ کا" (p. 161, folio 164); the eastern cells' demolition, "انگریزی عہد
  مین [illegible] کسی مصلحت کیوقت" (p. 140, folio 143); the transition into Chhajju Bhagat's karamat at the
  foot of p. 184 (folio 187); the Thakur Dwara's land sentence, "متعلق [illegible] س مکان کے ہے" (p. 189,
  folio 192); the end of p. 191 (folio 194); the two `[illegible]` gaps in the Shiv Gar entry (p. 198,
  folio 201) and in the Guru Arjan bearings (p. 205, folio 208), **the latter swallowing the distance
  between that samadh and Maharaja Ranjit Singh's**; the end of the brick-seller sentence at Abdul Wahab
  Qadri's tomb (p. 265, folio 268); the Dai Anga fabric's closing clause (p. 268, folio 271); the
  comparison of Shahzada Parvez's dome (p. 270, folio 273); "کسب [OCR?] نامہ مسجد ہی اس کا نام حسین کے سا

  possession narrative, so **the sequence of transfers is not fully recoverable** (pp. 276–277, folios
  279–280); the number of rotis and the recipients in the vow at the third tomb, three separate gaps
  (p. 294, folio 297); the end of the Mastan Shah sentence (p. 295, folio 298); the outcome of Shah

  folio 293); the marble object on the Begampura garden platform, "[illegible] سنگ مرمر کا تہا" (p. 278,

  seven-gardens sentence (p. 349, folio 356); "وہ نو طرف[OCR?] اسی توپیں لگا کر کہہی[OCR?] نہیں" on

  Zeb-un-Nisa did with the finished garden (p. 361, folio 368); "یہ بارہ دری دخل جہادونی[OCR?] تہی"

  تہے" (p. 363, folio 370); **Raja Suchet Singh's death, reported through a broken sentence in which the
  place of death and the agency are unrecoverable and it is not determinable who is "اپنے برادر زادہ"**

  Lahore building whose marble was ground up for the church's plaster, which is not named** (p. 392, folio

  کیا", where the words governing the act of dedication are flagged, so **whether "جنار دہن" is the deity

  خاتمہ's "صرف تفضلات[OCR?] نامتناہی الٰہی با انضباط[OCR?] نبہیا یا[OCR?]" and its Persian couplet
  (p. 462, folio 469).

### Transcription and structural artefacts

#### Chunks opening or closing mid-entry, so a site is unnamed — do not name any of these from context

- **p. 14 (folio 9)** breaks off on Raja Jaipal, "لاہور میں صاحب تخت و تاج تھا جس کی"; **p. 45 (folio 48)**
  on the naqqashi trade; **p. 60 (folio 63)** on the officials at "پانچویں"; **p. 75 (folio 78)** inside
  Mian Karim Bakhsh's famine relief, "اس نے ہم قومی و"; **p. 89 (folio 92)** inside the Qasaban notice;
  **p. 104 (folio 107)** two sentences into Shiwala Gulab Rai Jamadar.
- **p. 105 (folio 108): the temple that opens with "کے متعلق چودہ دوکانین ہین" is unnamed** — its fourteen
  shops, gilded dome and Brahmin servant cannot be attributed to a named site from that chunk alone, and
  its heading is in the previous chunk's pages.
- **p. 119 (folio 122)** opens mid-entry: a thakurdwara described in full — image, custody, mela, economy —
  **whose heading fell on the page before.** Read the preceding PDF page before any of it is used.
- **p. 132 (folio 135)** the Mandir Kali Devi entry breaks off at "گویا وہ دروازہ اُس نشستگاہ"; the rest is
  at p. 133 (folio 136), where **the temple's name and quarter are still not stated.**
- **p. 147 (folio 150)** the Sunehri Masjid narrative breaks off as the city's Muslims go to the faqirs'
  house; the outcome is at p. 148 (folio 151).
- **p. 148 (folio 151): the mosque this page opens on is unnamed** — its section heading falls on an
  earlier page, so everything under the Baoli Sahib bullet and the first three endowment items belongs to
  **a mosque the chunk does not name**, identified only by its founder Nawab Bukhari Khan and by adjacency
  to "باولی صاحب".
- **pp. 165–166 (folios 168–169): a section heading is missing.** The Pir Bai[OCR?] paragraph ends
  mid-clause and p. 166 opens already inside the enclosure holding the two **Huzuri** tombs. **The Huzuri
  complex therefore has no heading and no stated location**, and it is not clear whether it is continuous
  with the Pir Bai / Sunehri Masjid site in Kashmiri Bazaar or a fresh entry whose title was lost. **Do not
  assign it an address from that range.**
- **pp. 169–170 (folios 172–173)** the Maqbara Jani Khan entry breaks off and p. 170 opens mid-account of a
  mosque whose name is not given: **the mosque in the Dilawar Khan land case is unidentified**, and it
  cannot be assumed to be Jani Khan's.
- **pp. 162 and 176 (folios 165 and 179)** a chunk begins mid-sentence inside the Wazir Khan shops passage
  and ends inside the Masjid Pholiyan Wali entry at the author's own self-reference, so **the relationship
  the author claims to Mian Umar Din is unrecoverable** ("راقم مولف کتاب ہذا کے با تحت[OCR?]").
- **p. 177 (folio 180)** opens inside a notice about an officer of "عہدہ اسپ اور سپیر[OCR?]" whose son the
  author appointed daroga; **the building or institution the notice belongs to is in the previous chunk.**
- **p. 191 (folio 194)** closes mid-sentence in the dome over Ram Singh's samadh/shiwala, so **whether the
  gilded kalas was in place is not established.**
- **p. 192 (folio 195)** opens mid-entry: a mandir and shivala with a great chabutra and a hall housing its
  servants, its expenses formerly met by Sardar Bhagwan Singh, **carries no name**; **do not attach its
  funding fact to any named site.**
- **p. 206 (folio 209)** closes mid-entry in the Guru Arjan samadh description at "سمادہ کا چبوترہ و پختہ
  چونہ گچ [illegible]" — anything about that building's interior, kalas, custodian or income is in the next
  chunk.
- **p. 207 (folio 210)** opens mid-entry on an **unnamed samadh** described for a full paragraph — marble
  chabutra and railings, gilded mirrored ceiling, silk or pashmina canopy, a Granth dalan, gilded portraits
  of the ten Gurus. **The heading that named it falls in the previous chunk.**
- **p. 221 (folio 224)** closes mid-sentence at "تو توپیوں کی سلامی", before the accident the previous
  sentence foreshadows and before any description of the Kharak Singh / Nau Nihal Singh samadh; **p. 222
  (folio 225)** opens mid-sentence with the parapet falling and **never names the two dead** — only the
  next sentence names Nau Nihal Singh, as the body cremated the following day. **Do not infer who they
  were from context.**
- **p. 235 (folio 238)** closes mid-entry: the Shah Bilawal Qadri account breaks off at "چنانچہ دوبارہ
  جنازہ کی نماز پڑہی گئی اور". **Where the coffin was reburied, and where the present mazar stands, are
  not in that range.**
- **p. 236 (folio 239)** opens mid-sentence, "حال کے موقع پر دفن کیا گیا یہ خانقاہ …". The mazar inside is
  "مزار شاہ بلاول", but **whether that is the entry's heading, and whose burial the first clause describes,
  cannot be told.**
- **p. 250 (folio 253)** closes mid-entry in "مسجد محمد صالح سندہی" at "انگریزی عملداری"; **p. 251 (folio
  254)** opens at "مین مسجد خالی ہوگئی تو نواب علی رضا خان نے اسکی مرمت کرائی" — the three mihrabs and
  three domes, the vanished garden, the old tank, mohalla Haji Sawari[OCR?], the nephew of Shaikh Muhammad
  Salih Haji Hanoz[OCR?] and Nawab Ali Raza Khan's repair all belong to **an unnamed mosque.**
- **p. 264 (folio 267)** closes mid-entry in the Sharaf un-Nisa Begum account at "آخر جب سلطنت مغلیہ جاتی
  رہی اور"; **p. 265 (folio 268)** opens with the Qur'an and sword taken out by the Sikhs, their valuation,
  and a government repair through the author. These belong to a tomb whose heading is in the previous
  chunk. **Do not attach them to Sayyid Abdul Wahab Qadri**, whose heading comes after them — though the
  two fragments are plainly two halves of the Sharaf un-Nisa entry, **that join is a human's call.**
- **p. 278 (folio 281)** closes mid-entry in "مسجد خواجہ ایاز" at "ماتحت نواب علی مردان خان"; **p. 279
  (folio 282)** opens at "کے کام کرتا تہا جب شالامار باغ …", the mosque identifiable only from its own
  inscription "بندہ درگاہ خواجہ محمد ایاز".
- **p. 292 (folio 295)** closes mid-sentence at "یہ عورت قوم طوایف", inside the Miani graveyard's second
  enclosure; **p. 293 (folio 296)** opens on the fragment "مین سے تہی", the subject recoverable only
  because the text later names "رانی گل بیگم کا مقبرہ".
- **The numbered enclosures at pp. 293–296 (folios 296–299) belong to a tract the book never names.** The
  text runs third, fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh, eighth and then **tenth**; the **first, second and ninth
  are missing**, the ninth falling at the p. 295/296 break where only "اور چبوترہ [illegible]" and "احاطہ
  مین بہت ہین" survive. **Do not infer the burial ground or quarter from the individual graves.**
- **p. 306 (folio 309)** closes mid-entry in the Dai Anga / Gulabi Bagh account; **p. 307 (folio 310)**
  opens at "میں یہ باغ بہت بڑا تہا…". The entry's own heading is in neither chunk — **"Gulabi Bagh / tomb
  of Dai Anga" is not the book's heading.**
- **p. 320 (folio 327)** closes mid-clause in "مقبرہ آصف جاہ وزیر شاہجہانی"; **p. 321 (folio 328)** opens
  inside a description with **no heading and a plural subject ("ان مکانات")**, naming Jahangir's tomb, Taj
  Bibi's tomb and Ali Mardan Khan's tomb in one breath. The site is named only retrospectively at p. 323
  (folio 330) and p. 324 (folio 331). **How much of p. 321 belongs to the Asaf Jah entry rather than to a
  preceding one cannot be settled.**
- **p. 348 (folio 355)** closes mid-sentence inside the Shalamar Bagh entry at "مرمت ہوتی رہتی" — the
  account of the annual repair grant **and the author's role in it** is incomplete.
- **p. 349 (folio 356)** the garden under discussion is "یہ باغ", this garden, with the name falling before
  the chunk; the chunk positions the seven relative to "شالامار باغ" but **never states that "یہ باغ" is
  Shalamar.**
- **p. 364 (folio 371)** the compound is unnamed; "خیر گڑہ[OCR?]", "گڑہی شاہو" and the mazar of ابوالخیر
  are supplied later from within the same entry, **not from a heading.**
- **p. 380 (folio 387)** is a hospital only because the word ہسپتال occurs inside it; **"which hospital is
  not stated here".**
- **p. 396 (folio 403)** the building acquired is given only its function, "جائے قیام لفٹنٹ گورنر بہادر و
  گورنمنٹ ہوس"; **its proper name lies earlier.**
- **pp. 411–415 (folios 418–422)** the jail described **never prints its title** and is taken to be the
  Central Jail only because the next entry places the District Jail "متصل سنٹرل جیل بجانب غرب" (p. 415,
  folio 422).
- **pp. 426–429 (folios 433–436)** the church **never has its name printed**; "its denomination, dedication
  and street are (not stated) here".
- **p. 442 (folio 449)** the Thakurdwara is identifiable only as a building "متعلق مندر بانکے بہاری"; **"Do
  not attach that name to it without the preceding page."**
- **p. 457 (folio 464)** the Mata temple's entry opens mid-sentence with its heading in the previous chunk;
  the mohalla's name **کوچہ بال ناتا** and the image's name **بال بائی[OCR?] ماتا** "are not connected by
  the text", and the building's own name is not stated — **"Do not name this site from context."**
- **Entries that simply break off mid-sentence within pp. 349–463**: Garhi Shahu (p. 363, folio 370), the
  government hospital (p. 379, folio 386), Government House (p. 395, folio 402), Central Jail (p. 410,
  folio 417), the Roman Catholic church (p. 425, folio 432), the unnamed Mori Gate shiwala (p. 441, folio
  448), and مکان مندر بال ناتہا[OCR?] (p. 456, folio 463).

#### Marginal numerals

- **Marginal numerals are the only non-body material in pp. 119–235** (pp. 131, 177, 178, 180, 182, 186,
  176; folios 134, 180, 181, 183, 185, 189, 179), and **they are printed split and out of order in the
  transcription** — a transcription artefact to check against the lithograph. Instances where the split or
  disorder is visible in the text: "٧٦[OCR?] ١٢", "٩٦[OCR?] ١٢", "٢٤[OCR?] ١٢", "٩٧ ١٢[OCR?]", "٩
  ١٢[OCR?]" (the last incomplete), and "۸۲[OCR?]" in the bell-count margin. The colophon numeral is
  likewise printed with a gap between the digit pairs, "۱۸   ۸۴ [OCR?]" (p. 463).
- Marginal numerals also disagree with their own text in four places — see "Dates" (pp. 177–182; p. 247;
  p. 186; p. 242).

#### Orthography, and a change of transcription hand

- **The lithograph's orthography affects every quotation**: ہین for ہیں, مین for میں, تہا/تہین for
  تھا/تھیں, بہائی for بھائی, نہین for نہیں, تہو for تھے, "زبانہ" for زمانہ (p. 32, folio 35). These are
  printed thus and are **not** flagged by the transcriber; every quotation in this file is reproduced as
  printed and **must not be silently normalised** in an entry.
- **A change of transcription hand falls at p. 201 (folio 204).** Up to p. 200 the transcription writes
  "تہا", "بہی", "سانتہہ", "سماوہ", "ٹہاکر دوارہ", "چڑہاوہ"; from p. 201 the same words appear as "تھا",
  "بھی", "ساتھ", "سمادہ", "ٹھاکردوارہ", "چڑھاوہ", **and the flag density changes with it.** **Search both
  spellings when looking for anything in this book, and do not treat "سماوہ" and "سمادہ" as different
  words.**
- **Architectural vocabulary recurs under a flag and should be normalised by a human once**: "سقف
  قالبوتی[OCR?]" on seven pages of pp. 177–205; "ریختہ[OCR?] کار" for exterior finish (pp. 193–200);
  "سقاوہ[OCR?]" (pp. 182–183, folios 185–186); "سماوہ[OCR?]"/"سادہ[OCR?]" for samadh throughout
  (pp. 184–191, folios 187–194) — **the samadh/سادہ alternation makes the two marble buildings outside
  Masti Gate easy to confuse in any index built from this text**; "کلس[OCR?]" (pp. 180, 191; folios 183,
  194); and "گنبد پہاڑ سی[OCR?] دار" (pp. 207, 208, 211, 215; folios 210, 211, 214, 218) beside the
  unflagged "گنبد کشتی نما" and "گنبد طولانی". **Any architectural use of pp. 192–206 turns on terms the
  transcription itself is unsure of.**

#### Words used in a sense the transcription does not support — settle from the lithograph before any entry uses them

- **"اوقات" is printed where the sense requires awqaf, and is not flagged**: "حمام وزیر خان جو مسجد کے
  اوقات میں سے شمار ہوتا ہے" (p. 15, folio 10). **An endowment fact this useful must be verified against
  the lithograph before it is cited as awqaf.**
- **"بارونق" in four places where the sense appears to be a stored commodity, not liveliness.** Masjid
  Naqiban "مین بارونق بہر ہی رہتی تہی" under the Sikhs and fell empty when "بارونق تلف ہو کر" under the
  English (p. 252, folio 255); Colonel Gulab Singh "اس مسجد مین بارونق بہر دہی تہی" (p. 259, folio 262);
  and at Abul Hasan Khan's tomb "بارونق کو آگ لگ کر وہ سنگین عمارت پاش پاش ہو گئی" (p. 260, folio 263).
  **The transcriber left no flag on any of these.** A building filled with it, emptied of it by a new
  administration and blown apart when it ignites is doing something a word meaning "flourishing" cannot
  do. **A human must re-read these four places before any statement is made about military use of these
  mosques and tombs.**
- **"نزدول [OCR?]" / "نزول [OCR?]" / "نزمول[OCR?]" — nazul.** Flagged at every occurrence (pp. 257, 260,
  261, 268, 270, 272, 322; folios 260, 263, 264, 271, 273, 275, 329). What the book shows it doing is
  enough to cite — properties entered in a **register or schedule** and **auctioned on application** to
  private buyers — but **the word should be quoted, not glossed**, until the reading is confirmed. (See
  also the author's own purchase, below.)
- **"کانسی" / "کانسی کار" against "کاشی کاری".** "کانسی" is flagged on nearly every occurrence and is the
  book's term for the mosques' decorated surface work (pp. 152–157, folios 155–160); "کانسی کار" against
  "کاشی کاری" is **unflagged and unreconciled** across pp. 260–286 (folios 263–289). **Whether bronze or
  glazed tile is meant must be settled before the material of these surfaces is stated.**
- **"شہید" applied to shops** — "مسجد کے سب دوکانیں شہید ہین" (p. 162, folio 165) — is used of buildings,
  not persons, and is glossed in this file's other sections as "ruined". **Confirm the reading against the
  lithograph**; the following clause is broken by `[illegible]`.
- **"بہائی"** is written the same way whether it means **brother** or the **title Bhai** (p. 336, folio
  343); both senses appear in consecutive clauses and neither is chosen.
- **"ڈہیرہ [OCR?]"** — the Punjabi word the author glosses as "grave" in deriving **Panj Pir Dher**; the
  word itself is flagged, and it is that range's only linguistic argument (p. 292, folio 295).
- **"معابد مقایر[OCR?] ہنود و اہل اسلام"** — the section heading that defines the book's own taxonomy, read
  as "the temples and graves of Hindus and Muslims", but the preposition "سوا سے" is irregular and **the
  reading should be checked against the lithograph before the taxonomy is cited** (p. 347, folio 354). The
  companion taxonomy sentence at p. 326 (folio 333) has damaged numerals, so **how many categories and
  sections the author counts cannot be stated**; only the **two فصل** are legible.
- **"روز قائم کے مہندی"** — the Muharram rite's name printed **قائم**, carried exactly as printed and not
  corrected (p. 332, folio 339).
- **"گنجینہ سرودی [OCR?] المشہور گنج تاریخ" against "گنجینہ سروری"** — the chronogram-collection's title
  spelled two ways, the second unflagged (pp. 254, 261; folios 257, 264).
- **"حضور سی[OCR?] باغ"** — the destination garden of Zeb-un-Nisa's marble, named three times and flagged
  (p. 311, folio 314; p. 322, folio 329); relevant to the samadhi row above.
- **"ٹہیکہ گوشت رسانی گورون کا"** — the word for the beneficiaries of the butcher's meat contract is
  unresolved, rendered as printed with no gloss (p. 150, folio 153).
- **"قوال" at a gurdwara**, unflagged but unusual enough to verify (p. 102, folio 105); and the gurdwara's
  great assembly day, "آٹہوین روز انوار[OCR?] کے دن", so that **the periodicity of the largest gathering at
  an archive site is unresolved** (p. 102, folio 105); the lecture day "انوار[OCR?] کے روز" recurs
  (pp. 428–441, folios 435–448).
- **"اس واقع کے بعد گرو امرتسر سے چلا گیا"**, which reads literally "*from* Amritsar" in a narrative set in
  Lahore — **quote the Urdu rather than a direction of travel** (p. 112, folio 115). Khuda Singh's travels,
  where "دو دو روز" cannot be reconciled with "کئی سال" in one sentence — **neither the itinerary nor its
  length should be carried** (p. 102, folio 105).
- **"کہتربان گوٹ [OCR?] میرین [OCR?] کی برادری"**, the marriage-offering brotherhood — **the custom and
  amounts are clear, the community is not** (p. 97, folio 100).
- **"محکمہ خفیفہ[OCR?]"**, the department paying the Baoli's fifty rupees, **whose real name must be
  confirmed** (p. 113, folio 116).
- **"پانی ولنے [OCR?] کی بیماری"**, the disease at Shah Dargahi's well, for which **the book's own gloss
  (pustules filled with white water) should be used, not the name** (p. 82, folio 85).
- **"سرکار قیصر[OCR?] ضلع دار[OCR?]"**, the government's own designation (p. 449, folio 456); the qaum
  "اڑوٹری[OCR?]"; "اردو نرسی[OCR?] وارڈ", a flagged English term in Urdu script whose reading is uncertain
  though its function is clear (p. 416, folio 423); the Roman-tongue terms "آلٹار[OCR?]", "ٹہیبر[OCR?]
  ناکل[OCR?]", "مشریا[OCR?] ڈیکا[OCR?]" (pp. 428–441, folios 435–448); the origin story's dynasty "عہد
  سلطنت لودہی[OCR?]", the informants' trade "کہمیار فروش[OCR?]" and the container "ٹہایہ[OCR?]" (p. 457,
  folio 464); the day of sale "تیسرے[OCR?] روز" (p. 458, folio 465); the founder's liability clause "جو یہ
  ذمہ[OCR?] بانی ہے" and the location of the fourteen shops "سڑک[OCR?] سے بسمت شرق" (p. 462, folio 469);
  the helpers' assistance "مددوی[OCR?]" / "کافی امدادی" (p. 462, folio 469); "جیل خانہ[OCR?]" at Ichhra and
  "سیکہہ[OCR?] زمین" / "ٹہہریوں[OCR?]", so **what exactly Ranjit Singh put into the sarai is unclear**
  (p. 365, folio 372); and the garbled nouns "کہو ٹُڑون[OCR?] کا اصطبل", "ششئی[OCR?] شکل",
  "گمیان[OCR?]"/"بگیاں[OCR?]", "سنگین[OCR?]", "کتاب با[OCR?]", "گلشندان[OCR?]", "درسی بافی[OCR?]"
  (pp. 397–410, folios 404–417); "تنبیر[OCR?] کے پتہر", "جمہوری[OCR?] پتہر", "زردچشمہ[OCR?]",
  "ناٹک[OCR?]", "گنبشتا[OCR?]" (pp. 442–456, folios 449–463). The phrase "چونکہ لاہور کے اندر نہیں[OCR?]
  حاکموں کی حکومت تہی" (p. 364, folio 371) **is certainly mis-transcribed at "نہیں[OCR?]" and the rulers in
  question are not identified; none has been supplied.**

#### One person under two names, and heading against body

Do not merge, and do not reconcile from outside the text.

- **"راجہ ہیر سنگھ [OCR?]" (p. 32, folio 35) and "راجہ ہیرا سنگھ" (p. 33, folio 36)** on facing pages.
- **The hakim "خاندان حکیم ولی شاہ۔ یہ ہو دلی شاہ"** (p. 57, folio 60) — the second clause may be an
  alternative name or OCR noise; the family is called Wali Shah thereafter.
- **Raja Jaipal's genealogy is given twice at different lengths**: four generations at p. 17 (folio 12)
  against three plus a title at p. 22 (folio 17).
- **The Shivala Raknath/Ragnath is named three ways on two pages**, *Mistri* and *Mishr* differing inside a
  flag (pp. 115–116, folios 118–119).
- **The founder of the Talwara shivala is spelled two ways on facing pages** — "ولباغ رے[OCR?]"
  (pp. 121–122, folios 124–125) and "دلباغ رے[OCR?]" (p. 122, folio 125), both flagged; **one reading is
  wrong** and the section heading uses the first.
- **"چہ[OCR?] دینا ناتہ" vs "راجہ دینا ناتہ"** — the man who raised the Thibi Wala dome (p. 123, folio 126;
  p. 124, folio 127). **No date and no office; add none.**
- **Two Pandits Hari, or one?** "پنڈت ہری[OCR?] سراے[OCR?]", the Lahore bhagat who sent, and "پنڈت ہری
  نارائن", who went to Rangilpur and brought the image back (p. 120, folio 123). Cannot be settled.
- **The mahant who told the chor-mor katha may or may not be Balram Das**: the building was given to Mahant
  Balram Das (p. 124, folio 127), the katha is told by "مہنت ہر[OCR?] کشند[OCR?]" a few lines later
  (p. 124, folio 127), and the succession list names the incumbent only as "مہنت موصوف" (p. 125, folio
  128).
- **Two servants of Sher Singh given near-identical names on one page** — "مسمی نگا سنگہ", the murdered
  khidmatgar, and "لڑکا مسمی نکا سنگہ", the seventeen-year-old paid caretaker, **neither flagged** (p. 212,
  folio 215). **They may be one misread name or two men.**
- **The governor's name is given in two orders in two entries** — "نواب زکریا خان بہادر صوبہ لاہور"
  (p. 207, folio 210) and "نواب خان بہادر زکریا خان" (p. 216, folio 219). **Not an OCR flag; the book's own
  variation.**
- **One founder under two names or titles, twice in one chunk** — "میلا رام ٹھیکہ دار" and "راے میلا رام"
  on one page (p. 223, folio 226), the chunk not saying they are the same man; and "شوالہ لالہ رتن چند" in
  the heading against "دیوان رتن چند" in the text (pp. 223–224, folios 226–227).
- **Ghore Shah Bukhari has two "original names" in one entry**: "اصلی نام سید جہولن شاہ بخاری" and, three
  lines later, "جہولن شاہ … جسکا اصلی نام بہا والدین تہا" (p. 236, folio 239). The entry's own explanation
  makes *Jhulan Shah* his grandfather's transferred epithet; **the book states both.**
- **Heading against name, third tomb of the unnamed tract**: the heading gives "میرزا ہدی[OCR?]" (p. 293,
  folio 296), the next page names him "محمد مسلم" with the title "پیر زیدی[OCR?]" (p. 294, folio 297).
  Both flagged; **whether "میرزا ہدی" names the man, the tract, or is an OCR error cannot be settled.**
- **Two men named Nur Husain die in consecutive clauses** at the Rasul Shahi house (p. 248, folio 251).
  Either the second name is an OCR corruption or the book lists two members of the sect; **the transcription
  gives no way to tell.**
- **Pir Muhammad Shah's son is "شاہ سروار[OCR?]" at p. 308 (folio 311) and "شاہ سردار" at p. 309 (folio
  312).**
- **Heading and body disagree on three founders' names**, and the notes forbid reconciling them from outside
  the text: heading **ٹہاکردوارہ پنڈت اوقت[OCR?] ناتہہ** against the body's "بانی اس مندر کا پنڈت وشنا تہہ"
  (pp. 449–451, folios 456–458); heading **مندر شوالہ لالہ چہوٹے لال** against the body's founder "لالہ
  درگا[OCR?] پرشاد خلف الرشید لالہ چہوٹے لال مرحوم", the temple named for the father and the building
  attributed to the son (p. 447, folio 454); heading "چہوجہی مل کہتری مہروائی[OCR?]" against the body's
  "دہیرو والی[OCR?]" (pp. 451–452, folios 458–459).
- **One gate printed two ways**: "مندری[OCR?] دروازے کے باہر" (p. 440, folio 447) and "بیرون دروازہ موری"
  (p. 441, folio 448) for the same gate; the Devi Dwara's gate printed "بہائی دروازہ" (p. 438, folio 445).
  All left as printed.

#### Passages that contradict themselves as printed

- **An internal contradiction in the Suchet Singh narrative.** "راجہ ہیرا سنگہ اپنے برادر زادہ کے برخلاف یہ
  جموں سے آیا تہا" makes **Hira Singh** the one who came from Jammu against his nephew; but the surrounding
  sentences make Suchet Singh the uncle who came to displace Hira Singh, and quote Hira Singh saying "چچا
  میری جان کا دشمن بنکر آیا ہے" (pp. 194–195, folios 197–198). **One of the two names is wrong on the page
  or in the transcription; read folio 197 from the PDF before citing.**
- **The Haqiqat Rai offence is stated twice and differently** — words about "پیغمبر صاحب" in the narration
  (pp. 207–208, folios 210–211) and irreverence toward "اہل بیت" in the governor's sentence (p. 208, folio
  211). **Carry both.**
- **A possible dropped negative.** Of Bhai Rupa's samadh: "مگر طلائی کلس لگایا گیا" — "but a golden kalas
  was fitted" — where the "مگر" and the pattern of every other building in the chunk suggest a lost
  negative (p. 198, folio 201). **Do not assert either way.** Likewise **a negated verb where the sense is
  positive**: the two sacred charpais are printed "جو مستان[OCR?] رجو و دہرمن کے سونے کی **نہیں**",
  although the sentence and the next treat them as theirs and sacred (p. 440, folio 447) — **check the
  lithograph.**
- **The kinship sentence at p. 321 (folio 328) is broken and contradicts p. 324 (folio 331).** p. 321 reads
  "چونکہ یہ شخص نور جہاں بیگم زوجہ محبوبہ جہانگیر اور وزیر عظم شاہ جہان بادشاہ تہا", with no relational
  word, so the occupant appears to *be* Nur Jahan while also being the grand vizier; p. 324 calls the
  occupant "اسکے بہائی آصف جاہ", her brother. **Neither is reconciled.** "اپنی والدہ کے مقبرے کے پاس اسکا
  مقبرہ بنوایا" at p. 321 is ambiguous as to whose mother is meant, and the noun for the emperor's grief is
  flagged.
- **A haveli said both to be gone and to be inhabited**: "یہ دونو حویلیاں باقی نہیں کہ مہاراجہ رنجیت سنگہ
  نے خاندان نوابان ملتان کو سکونت کے لیے دے دیں اب تک وہی اُس میں سکونت پذیر ہیں" (p. 338, folio 345).
- **Three mutually exclusive accounts of one alteration at Jahangir's tomb**, printed one after another and
  not chosen between: a mulla's fatwa under **Muhammad Shah**; **Sobha Singh** himself; or Ranjit Singh's
  general stripping — the author opening "و نہیں معلوم کہ کس وقت اتروا لی گئیں" (p. 317, folio 324).
  **This is the book's own practice and should be preserved, not resolved.**
- **Which haveli the Tehsil occupies.** At p. 370 (folio 377) the Tehsil building is "راجہ دہیان سنگہ کی
  حویلی" and in the next clause is said to have been built and lived in by **Raja Suchet Singh**; at p. 372
  (folio 379) the two are distinct buildings, the Assistant Commissioner sitting in Suchet Singh's haveli
  inside Lahore and the Extra Assistant Commissioner in Dhyan Singh's "جہان اب گورنمنٹ سکول ہے".
- **Bibi Maryam's image is stone and then clay in the same passage**: "مورت سنگی کی", then "پہ مورت اگرچہ
  مٹی[OCR?] کی ہے" (p. 428, folio 435), and again "سنگی[OCR?] مورت" (p. 429, folio 436).
- **A Thakurdwara's niches are filled and then empty.** Images are placed in the western and southern
  niches, and the passage then reads "ہر ایک طاقچہ خالی ہے", every niche is empty (p. 434, folio 441).
- **Rajo and Dharman are sisters, and mother and daughter, in one paragraph**: "رجو و دہرمن نام دو بہنیں",
  then "مسمات رجو ایک عورت بیوہ [illegible] اور دہرمن اُسکی دختر باکرہ" (p. 438, folio 445).
- **A sanctum described as unroofed and as a dark cellar**: "یہ مسقف نہیں" immediately before "اسکے اندر
  بطور تہہ خانہ کے بنا ہوا" and an account of darkness relieved only by two skylights (p. 440, folio 447).
- **A mela singular then two listed**: "یہاں بہار ہی[OCR?] میلہ ہوتا ہے" followed by two Ashtami dates
  (p. 439, folio 446).
- **Where Rani Nakain's samadh stands is ambiguous in the text.** The entry places both samadhs in Lahore,
  south-west of the Kachahri Sadr Zila Lahore, but the same page says she lived all her life[OCR?] in the
  fort of Sheikhupura and "جب یہ فوت ہوئی تو اس موقع پر اُسکی سمادہ بنی", at that spot — which could mean
  the Lahore site or Sheikhupura. **Both readings stand** (p. 443, folio 450).
- **The Tarpoliya theft, where what was cut — a piece of the jalhari or of the idol — cannot be settled**
  (p. 107, folio 110); **the treasure's contents, of which only the gilded and jewelled canopied bed is
  clear — do not itemise the hoard** (pp. 110–111, folios 113–114).
- **Four parts or five.** The preface says "چار حصوں میں تقسیم کیا" and describes four (p. 11, folio 6;
  pp. 11–12, folios 6–7); the contents carry "پانچواں حصہ" at printed page ۳۴۵[OCR?] and "تیسرا حصہ" at
  ۹۵[OCR?] against the plan's ordering, and end with "ضمیمہ" at an [illegible] page and "خاتمہ" at
  ۴۶۹[OCR?] (p. 5, folio 4).
- **The contents' own numerals disagree with the body in one place and are badly out of sequence in many.**
  The contents put پہلا حصہ at ۴ (p. 2) while the outline appears at folio 6 (p. 11) and the part heading
  at folio 7 (p. 12) — **either the numeral is wrong or a heading is repeated**; check printed page ۴. Clear
  column mis-alignments: "مدارس کا ذکر" at ۱۳ standing between ۳۲ and ۴۳; "مزار ملک ایاز" at ۱۶۰[OCR?]
  after ۱۷۹; "مزار حسو تیلی" at ۱۶۱[OCR?] between ۲۵۰ and ۲۷۲; and several more (pp. 2–5). **No
  title–number pairing from pp. 2–5 should be trusted without checking the printed page.** Several entries
  use the ditto mark "”" for the page number, and on pp. 3, 4 and 5 **the first row of a column dittos a
  value on the previous page, so those numbers are unrecoverable.**

### Where the partials disagree with each other

Three outright disagreements and two divergences of practice. None is resolved here; each needs the
printed page.

1. **Whether the ضمیمہ heading governs the temple entries to the end of the book.** The appendix heading at
   p. 432 (folio 439) reads "ضمیمہ — بعض ان مکانات کے ذکر میں جو بوقت تحریر مسودہ طبع کتاب کے لکہے نہیں
   گئے تہے اور اب بعد اختتام مسودہ و طبع کتاب اُنکا لکہنا واجب و منصور[OCR?] ہوا", and the first appendix
   entry repeats the reason in its own words: "اس کتاب کی تحریر مسودہ کے وقت اس مندر کی بنیاد کہید[OCR?]
   رہی تہی، اس سبب اسکا حال موقع پر درج نہوا۔ اب یہ مکان بنکر تیار ہو گیا ہے" (p. 432, folio 439). **One
   chunk note reads that heading as making everything after it later than the body of the book. The next
   records that pp. 442–456 (folios 449–463) "shows no appendix, colophon, errata or closing matter …
   nothing in it states or implies that it post-dates the main manuscript", and concludes that on its own
   evidence its entries date as the main text.** The two readings are not reconciled. **Whether the ضمیمہ
   heading of p. 432 governs the run of temple entries through p. 462 is exactly what a human must settle
   from the printed page** — and it governs the dating of every entry in that run. The last temple entry
   restates the point for itself: "بوقت تحریر حالات سابق کے یہ مندر موجود نہ تہا اب بعد تحریر و طبع کتاب
   اسکی عمارت اسی سال میں ختم ہوئی اسواسطے اسکا حال یہی زیب اندراج پاتا ہے" (p. 459, folio 466) — **the
   year "اسی سال" is not stated and no edition is named, so that entry cannot be dated from the notes;
   treat it as later than the body of the work and possibly later than a first printing.**
2. **Whether `gurdwara-baoli-sahib-guru-arjan-dev-ji-lahore` may be mapped at all in pp. 147–148.** One
   chunk **left it unmapped**: the text says only "باولی کا مکان", "باولی کے بہائی", "باولی کے محال"
   (p. 147, folio 150) — no guru, no name, no street — and this Baoli adjoins the Sunehri Masjid in
   Kashmiri Bazaar while the archive row is at Dabbi Bazaar; that note says "a human who knows the walled
   city's topography must decide, and the decision governs a substantial seizure and endowment-transfer
   narrative." The next chunk **mapped it provisionally** on the name plus the Granth Sahib alone —
   "باولی صاحب کے پاس جہان گرنتہہ صاحب رکہا ہو" (p. 148, folio 151) — with no quarter, bazaar, founder or
   date, and calls the mapping provisional. **Both readings stand; do not merge them.**
3. **The folio relation stated for pp. 46–60 contradicts the markers quoted in the same note.** The partial
   records chunk 004's relation as "folio = PDF index − 3 on every page" while quoting p. 46 → folio 49 and
   p. 60 → folio 63. **Read the printed marker off each page; rely on neither statement.** (See "The folio
   series".)
4. **Divergence of practice on `shrine-of-mian-mir`.** One partial carries material under that row (the
   rauza passage at p. 139, folio 142, with the enclosure problem noted); two others decline to connect the
   cantonment toponym to any shrine at all (pp. 34–35, 62; p. 357, folio 364; pp. 389–394, folios
   396–401). A human must decide whether the row is to receive material from this book at all before any
   of it is cited.
5. **Divergence of practice on the Guru Arjan rows.** One partial rules that **everything** about Guru
   Arjan in pp. 110–114 (folios 113–117) belongs to the Baoli and that **none** of it may go to
   `gurdwara-dera-sahib`; another maps a Dera Sahib site on locational bearings at pp. 205–206 (folios
   208–209) while noting that the same site has a baoli. The boundary between the two rows is therefore set
   differently in different ranges and must be fixed by a human before either row is written.
6. **An internal tension inside one partial, recorded as such**: the `shrine-of-baba-shah-kamal` mapping
   rests on the Ichhra location and puts him in the **سہروردی خاندان** (p. 313, folio 316), while the same
   partial declines "شاہ کمال کیتہلی [OCR?]", a **Qadiri** pir (p. 292, folio 295), and the archive row's
   own silsila field reads **Qadiri**. All three readings stand.

### The author's position as an evidentiary problem

This is not an OCR problem and cannot be checked against the plate. It is a source-critical caution on
particular classes of claim.

- **The book's judgements about ruin, neglect and repair are made by the officer who administered the
  repairs.** The partial for pp. 236–348 records **nine repairs and one report passing through the author,
  and one purchase by him**, all listed in that section's "What this book is". **Every judgement in that
  range about ruin, neglect, repair and the merits of successive regimes is made by that officer**, and in
  one case by the man who bought the auctioned property. The same pattern runs elsewhere: a government
  repair to the Sharaf un-Nisa tomb "through the author" (p. 265, folio 268); the annual repair grant for
  Shalamar and **the author's role in it**, in a sentence that breaks off (p. 348, folio 355); an officer
  "عہدہ اسپ اور سپیر[OCR?]" **whose son the author appointed daroga** (p. 177, folio 180); and the author
  named as "مولف کتاب" / "راقم" repeatedly through pp. 371–432 (folios 378–439), including in the same
  role for Montgomery Hall (p. 402, folio 409) and the women's jail (p. 409, folio 416) — while the
  equivalent office for Government House is **lost to an `[illegible]`**, "سب [illegible] کے متعلق ہے"
  (p. 399, folio 406), which "a reader may be tempted to fill; the chunk does not, and neither do these
  notes." **Any claim in this book that a building was repaired, neglected, well or badly administered,
  or that a regime was better or worse than another, must be attributed to him and not reported as
  neutral fact.**
- **The auctioned tomb-precinct property.** The nazul word itself is flagged at every occurrence (see
  above), and what the book shows the process doing is **properties entered in a register or schedule and
  auctioned on application to private buyers** (pp. 257, 260, 261, 268, 270, 272, 322; folios 260, 263,
  264, 271, 273, 275, 329). One of those purchases is the author's own. **No claim about the alienation,
  sale or auction of a tomb precinct in this book is disinterested testimony.**
- **His one large uncited statistic must be flagged as his own**: "ہزاروں مسجد میں سکہوں نے گرا کر اپنی
  عمارت کے شامل کر لی", the Sikhs demolished **thousands of mosques** — a figure offered without any count,
  and offered in order to praise an exception (pp. 343–344, folios 350–351). **It sits against his own
  flat, uncensured account of Muslim tombs stripped to build the Hazuri Bagh baradari** (p. 340, folio 347).
  Do not carry the figure as a quantity; carry it as his claim, with both pages.
- **His own office cannot be stated from this book.** The department he served for thirty years is
  **illegible in both places it is named** (pp. 7, 10; folios 2, 5) — **only "کارخانہ تعمیر" can be
  quoted.** His name on the title page is itself flagged, "رائے بہادر[OCR?] کنہیا لال[OCR?]" (p. 1), and
  the colophon's first chronogram heading cannot be parsed to say whether it is **by** or **addressed to**
  "کنہیا لال صاحب" (p. 463; see "Provenance"). **The partials "neither assert nor deny the identification"
  of the "مولف" / "راقم" of the text with the named author, and nothing here identifies him.** A
  "کنہیا لال" also appears as the Guru Ram Das gurdwara's "معتمد و معاون حلقہ" with the author's
  evaluative clause lost to `[illegible]` (p. 102, folio 105), and **the chunk does not identify them.**
- **His authorial presence is uneven, which matters when weighing silence.** He does not appear in his own
  person in pp. 349–363 (folios 356–370) or anywhere in pp. 442–456 (folios 449–463), where "the only
  trace of his authorial presence is the cross-reference at p. 452 (folio 459)"; he appears repeatedly in
  pp. 371–432 (folios 378–439). Where the book is silent about a site — the samadh in Hazuri Bagh, for
  instance — **the silence may be the silence of a particular officer's file, not of the city.**
- **His self-reference is broken where it would explain a relationship.** A chunk closes at "راقم مولف
  کتاب ہذا کے با تحت[OCR?]", so **the relationship the author claims to Mian Umar Din is unrecoverable**
  (pp. 162, 176; folios 165, 179).
- **On the informant question: the four inputs do not name one.** No partial records an informant, chain of
  transmission or named authority for the book's Muslim shrine material. What they do record of Mufti
  Ghulam Sarwar is that he is named as the author of "حدیقۃ الاولیا" among seventy works (p. 55, folio 58);
  that an enclosure of graves at the Huzuri tombs is his, its describing clauses `[illegible]` (p. 267,
  folio 270); that his output is given as seventy books at p. 53 (folio 56) and eighteen[OCR?] at p. 78
  (folio 81); and that a chronogram of his praising the printed *Tarikh-e Lahore* stands in the colophon
  (p. 463). **The partials nowhere state that he, or anyone, was the author's informant.** If the book
  makes such a claim, it is not in these inputs and must be established from the lithograph before any
  shrine entry relies on it. The only source-title the author is recorded as preferring is "تذکرہ
  حاکمیہ[OCR?]", **flagged, in the most damaged passage in the book** (pp. 304–306, folios 307–309).

### Provenance of the file and of the edition

- **Two library stamps, both illegible.** A faint diagonal stamp across the title page's central roundel
  (p. 1) and a heavy circular stamp **inked over** the upper right corner of the illuminated sarlauh
  (p. 6). **Neither institution can be named.** A human should identify the holding library from the PDF
  before the book's provenance is stated anywhere in the archive.
- **No digitisation, library or rights notice anywhere else in the book.** No "all rights reserved" line
  appears in pp. 1–118, and chunks 002–008 each record the absence of any digitisation, library or rights
  stamp on their pages; **no digitisation stamp, library watermark or rights line appears anywhere in
  pp. 119–235**, in any of eight chunks; **none anywhere in pp. 236–348** — "the pages carry only the
  book's own text, its `[p. N]` and `[folio N]` markers and the transcriber's `[OCR?]` / `[illegible]`
  flags"; and **none in chunks 025–031 (pp. 349–456)**. Recorded so the absence is not mistaken for an
  omission. **The one exception** is at the foot of p. 463, where the transcriber recorded "[image: partial
  circular stamp or decorative arc at the foot of the page, no legible text]" — **a stamp or ornament that
  may be a library or digitisation mark, from which the chunk yields no text.**
- **The colophon names no press, no place and no edition, and gives two unreconciled printing numerals with
  no era.** p. 463 is headed **"خاتمۃ الطبع"**, conclusion of the printing, and carries three chronogram
  pieces. The Persian piece includes "بحمد اللہ کہ مطبوع جہان گشت | بالطاف خدا تاریخ لاہور" and closes "چو
  شد مطبوع ہندی سال طبعش | ہگر[OCR?] شد مطبوع شد تاریخ لاہور", **speaking of a "ہندی سال" of printing
  without giving it**, and carries **a numeral printed on its own line as "۱۸   ۸۴ [OCR?]"** (with a gap
  between the digit pairs). The third piece, by Mufti Ghulam Sarwar, "خدا کے فضل سے تاریخ لاہور | نہایت
  عمدہ عمدہ نورانی چہپی ہے", carries **beneath it a numeral "۱۳۰۱ [OCR?]"**. **Both numerals are
  OCR-flagged; neither is labelled ھ, ء or بکرمی**; the intervening Urdu piece is "ایضاً قطعہ تاریخ اردو
  از مصنف سلمہ اللہ", by the author. **No press, no printing house, no place of printing, no edition and no
  patron of the printing is named** (p. 463). The only occurrence of "پریس[OCR?]" in the closing matter is
  inside a badly damaged clause of the خاتمہ about the author's government employment — "تم قیس تک[OCR?]
  پریس[OCR?] سرکاری کی طرف سے بہتر تعمیر سرکاری کے کام کر رہے ہو" (p. 462, folio 469) — **where it cannot
  be read as this book's imprint. Provenance of the edition therefore remains open, and the two numerals
  stand side by side unreconciled.**
- **The first chronogram's heading at p. 463 cannot be parsed as printed**: "قطعہ تاریخ از تاریخ طبع شاعر
  شیریں مقال رسا[OCR?] بہاور[OCR?] کنہیا لال صاحب ایکسٹرا[OCR?] کنٹو[OCR?] لاہور بحضور مصنف کتاب" — a
  flagged poetic name, the name **کنہیا لال صاحب**, a flagged office, Lahore, and the phrase **بحضور مصنف
  کتاب**. **Whether the piece is by Kanhaiya Lal or addressed to him, and what the office title reads,
  cannot be determined from the line**; the second piece is separately labelled "از مصنف", and the chunk
  otherwise does not identify the author by name or office. **No identification is asserted.**
- **The book's own date is unresolved and must not be converted**: chronogram "۱۲۸۲[OCR?]" with no era and
  no gloss (p. 12, folio 7) against "سنہ ۱۸۸۳ع یعنی اسی سال میں" (p. 67, folio 70), and against the
  colophon's two numerals above. The two mosque elapsed-age figures **must not be used to date the
  composition** either (pp. 152, 155; folios 155, 158).

### Cross-references and book titles a human must settle

- **"حدیقۃ الاولیا" appears at p. 55 (folio 58) as a book of Mufti Ghulam Sarwar of Lahore**, among seventy
  works, in a list several of whose titles are flagged. **That title matches the slug of the sibling book
  in this queue. Nothing in the chunk states that the Hadiqat-ul-Aulia it lists is that book, and nothing
  outside the chunk may be used to say so.** If confirmed, it makes the *Tarikh-e Lahore* a witness for the
  other book's author, his family, his Lahore mohalla, his haveli, his seventy-title bibliography and his
  claimed descent through the muftis of Kotli Muftian from Baha-ud-Din Zakariya's line (pp. 52–55, 78;
  folios 55–58, 81). Note the seventy/eighteen[OCR?] contradiction in his output (pp. 53, 78; folios 56,
  81) before the bibliography is cited.
- **The malfuzat title is transcribed "فوائد الفوائد"** and attributed to "شیخ المشائخ نظام الدین بدایونی
  دہلوی" (p. 13, folio 8). **Recorded as printed; check the lithograph before the title is quoted from this
  archive.**
- **"تذکرہ حاکمیہ[OCR?]"**, the title of the source the author says he prefers for Bibi Pak Daman, is
  flagged and sits in the range's most damaged passage (pp. 304–306, folios 307–309).
- **"گنجینہ سرودی [OCR?] المشہور گنج تاریخ" against "گنجینہ سروری"** — one chronogram-collection spelled
  two ways, the second unflagged (pp. 254, 261; folios 257, 264).
